# Agent Development Guide

A file for [guiding coding agents](https://agents.md/) working in this
Windows-only Ghostty fork.

## Commands

- **Build:** `zig build` (also stages the pinned `conpty.dll` and
  `OpenConsole.exe` beside the exe; see the 2026-10-10 bundled ConPTY entry)
- **Test (Zig):** `zig build test -Demit-test-exe=true --summary all`
  (full suite, ~4.5k tests, about a minute). This is the only invocation that
  is trustworthy on its own, and `--summary all` is part of it: the count is
  the evidence.
  - Bare `zig build test` errors in this fork.
  - `-Dtest-filter=<token>` runs the tests whose fully qualified name (file
    path, `.test.`, test name) contains the token, for quick iteration; it is
    not evidence on its own. Read the count: the `N/M tests passed` line
    counts the matching tests plus about 70 unnamed `test { ... }` blocks that
    no filter excludes, so a token that matches nothing still reports about
    70 passes, not zero. Use `ConPTY` to reach the probe tests in
    `src/pty_transport_probe.zig`. A filtered build compiles the whole tree,
    about a minute. `Build Summary: 41/41 steps succeeded` counts build
    steps, not tests. See the 2026-10-06 Self-Correction Log entry.
- **Formatting (Zig):** `zig fmt .`
- **Formatting (other):** `prettier -w .`

## libghostty-vt

- Build: `zig build -Demit-lib-vt`
- Build WASM: `zig build -Demit-lib-vt -Dtarget=wasm32-freestanding -Doptimize=ReleaseSmall`

## Directory Structure

- Shared Zig core: `src/`
- Win32 application runtime: `src/apprt/win32.zig` coordinates App, Host, and
  Surface behavior; `src/apprt/win32_theme.zig` owns theme policy; focused
  support modules under `src/apprt/win32/` own Win32 constants/externs,
  labels/input, startup/render tracing, chrome rectangle math, and GDI paint
  primitives.
- Windows packaging: `dist/windows/` + `scripts/package-windows.ps1`
- libghostty-vt headers: `include/ghostty/`

This fork removed the upstream `macos/` Xcode app and the `src/apprt/gtk`
runtime. Do not reintroduce them.

## Issue and PR Guidelines

- Never create an issue.
- Never create a PR to the upstream Ghostty repo; only work against this
  fork, `amanthanvi/noctty`.
- If the user asks you to create an issue or PR, confirm the target is this
  fork and refuse any upstream publish request. If publishing is still
  disallowed by the active task contract, offer a local patch summary or
  handoff instead.

## Self-Correction Log

- 2026-10-15: Default Latin letter bindings (`ctrl+shift+c`, `v`, `t`, `w`...) never matched on Cyrillic, Greek, Hebrew or Arabic layouts, and the ISO and ABNT keys were `.unidentified` (PKG-51, F052, F060). (1) The Win32 apprt reports the right physical key (`.key_c`) with text and `unshifted_codepoint` in the layout's script (`unshiftedCodepoint` masks Ctrl, Alt, Shift and Caps before `ToUnicodeEx`: U+0441 for the C key on Russian; if the layout yields nothing printable it falls back to the static US table, which already matched), so `Binding.Set.getEvent` found nothing. It now tries the key's own US letter after every lookup on what the layout types and before `catch_all`, for a LETTER key whose `unshifted_codepoint` is no ASCII letter. Two gates that were tried and are wrong: "unshifted is non-ASCII" misses Greek's `;` on the Q key and Hebrew's `/`; and letting digits and punctuation stand in changed Latin layouts (the AZERTY number row types accented letters, so Ctrl+à would have run `reset_font_size`; `goto_tab` registers the physical `digit_N` keys already). A letter key that types a Latin letter must keep it, so no layout is handed QWERTY positions. The lookup is in `Set.getEvent`, so key tables and `keyEventIsBinding` get it too, and an inner table's fallback beats an outer table's exact binding on the layout's own letter. Not measured on a live non-Latin layout (the maintainer's layouts are never changed; tests are pure and give `getEvent` synthetic events). (2) `VK_OEM_102` is `.intl_backslash` (ISO `<>|`; a Japanese Ro key shares the VK and is named the same, because splitting them by scan code 0x73 made `hotkeyPhysicalVirtualKey(.intl_ro)`, which has to be `VK_ABNT_C1`, disagree with the key event: a global hotkey and a key event must name a key identically); `VK_ABNT_C1` is `.intl_ro`; neither is in `codepoint_map` or the Kitty table, so encoding is unchanged, and `hotkeyPhysicalVirtualKey` names both. (3) Left out on purpose: `VK_CLEAR` as `.numpad_begin` would be written as `CSI E` by the legacy table, and ConPTY's `InputStateMachineEngine` has no `E` in `CsiActionCodes` (A B C D H F I O M m ~ P Q R S c t Z _), so with mode 9001 off numpad 5 would become a stray `ESC [ E` (Windows Terminal's own encoder does write it, the parser does not read it); NumLock-off numpad navigation as `.numpad_home` etc. would stop `shift+insert`, `shift+page_up` and copy mode's arrows from firing for the numpad unless `getEvent` also matched the main-cluster twin; Alt+Space (F061) still sends `ESC SPACE` and opens the window menu, since swallowing it removes `M-SPC` and needs a `keyEventIsBinding` check and a swallowed release. (4) Harness traps: `testing` helpers that unwrap `getEvent(...).?` abort the whole test process with exit code 3 when the binding is missing (a failing test then reports only `exited with code 3`): return null and compare. A heredoc through this agent's Git Bash mangled backslashes and `\x1b`: write scripts with the file tool. CI also builds `src/apprt/win32_compositor_native.zig` on its own, so no module it imports (here `win32_clipboard_html.zig`) may import outside `src/apprt`.
- 2026-10-15: The UIA echo queue (typed characters waiting to be seen echoed, `recent_input` in `win32_terminal_accessibility.zig`) was no longer cleared by a full-screen program once #373 stopped capturing the alternate screen: its redraws used to mismatch the queue, and now nothing reached it, so for up to one second after leaving the alternate screen a primary-screen line that started with the typed characters was stripped as their echo (UIA-ECHO-01). A screen switch (modes 47, 1047, 1049, entered or left) now makes the consumer forget the queue. Rules the review left. (1) The clear must sit at its place in the stream, not before the batch: `SemanticOutputCapture.reset_at` is the byte index of the first switch (0 is the full reset, which also drops what came before) and `TerminalOutputTransport.pushSemanticCapture` pushes the bytes ahead of it, then the rest with the reset. A batch-wide flag cleared the queue ahead of the primary-screen bytes that precede the switch in the same PTY read, which is where the echo of `vim file` sits when it arrives together with `?1049h`, and the first draft's own test asserted that outcome. An empty tail push becomes `trailing_reset`, so the reset still lands between the chunks and before the next batch's data. Only the first switch matters, since nothing is held again until the batch is delivered. (2) Only a switch that changes the active screen counts: `Terminal.switchScreen` returns early when the requested screen is already active, and `StreamHandler.switchScreenMode` compares `screens.active_key` before and after (`restore_mode` reaches it through `setMode` too). (3) Not covered: the user `reset` action and the redo of a reset call `terminal.fullReset()` directly, bypassing the handler's `resetForFullReset`, so with the program on the alternate screen the next primary text can still meet a stale queue. (4) A session-level test that only calls `resetSemanticOutputContinuity` passes without the fix and proves nothing; the test that counts drives capture, transport and session in order (`has_text` on the normalizer shows whether any text survived the echo strip: a line that is exactly the typed text strips to nothing, which is what separates the cases). (5) Harness traps: appending an `inline for` of comptime-string `stream.nextSlice` calls to the long "semantic output capture follows real stream handler parser" test fails the build with `evaluation exceeded 100000 backwards branches` at `src/lib/union.zig:124` (the test body is one comptime budget): give new stream assertions their own test with its own fixture. A heredoc through this agent's Git Bash turned `\\` into `\`, so `\x1b` in a Python string became a raw ESC byte in the Zig source (`string literal contains invalid byte`): write scripts with the file tool.
- 2026-10-15: Local undo/redo of `clear_screen` / `reset` restored the cursor to the last tab stop (PKG-53, F140), and Copy as HTML exported every OSC 8 URI as an anchor (F138). (1) `TerminalFormatter` with `extra = .all` (the `captureTerminalUndoStateBytes` snapshot) wrote the screen's CUP first and then DECSTBM/DECSLRM (they home the cursor) and the tab stops (`CSI n G` + `HTS` per stop), so replaying into a reset terminal ended on the last stop (column 8 of 12 in the undo test, 72 of 80 on a pane). The screen formatter now runs without the cursor and a second `ScreenFormatter` pass (`content = .none`, `extra.cursor` only) writes it last; that pass counts its own pin-map bytes, and the terminal-level discarding pass that counts the trailing extras runs with `extra.screen = .none`, so the CUP is never counted twice. In origin mode (DECOM, restored by the modes block) CUP counts from the corner of the restored scrolling region, so `ScreenFormatter.cursor_origin` is the region's left/top only when modes and scrolling region are both emitted (without the region the replaying terminal keeps full-screen margins and the position stays absolute). (2) Trap found by independent review: the tab stops must go out BEFORE DECSTBM/DECSLRM. In origin mode `CSI n G` is relative to the left margin and stops at the right one, so stops 8 and 16 came back as 11 and 14 on a 20-column terminal with `?69` margins `4;15`, and a round-trip test that compares only the cursor passes anyway: compare `tabstops` too. Left as is: pending wrap is still lost (CUP clears it; upstream 997a2aff2 re-prints the cell, which reorders the screen extras), and a cursor above or below the region in origin mode (reachable through DECSC, DECSTBM, DECRC) comes back on the region's edge row. (3) HTML: `hyperlink.isExportableUri` allows `http`, `https`, `mailto` and `ftp`, compared exactly from the first byte (a blank, tab or control character in front of or inside the scheme does not pass), so `javascript:`, `file://host`, UNC, `data:` and custom protocols keep their text with neither tag. The close tag follows an `anchor_open` flag, not the link id, and the point map counts only the bytes written; `ssh:`, `git:` and `tel:` stop being anchors in copied HTML while the in-terminal opener (`prepareWin32OpenTarget`) still accepts them. Clipboard code is tested through its serialiser seam (a `win32.zig` test: `ScreenFormatter` output wrapped by `win32_clipboard_html.wrapFragment`, read back with `fragmentRange`), never by copying, and `win32_clipboard_html.zig` must not import outside `src/apprt` because CI builds it standalone through `win32_compositor_native.zig`. (4) Harness traps. A heredoc through this agent's Git Bash turned `\\` into `\` and `\x1b` into a raw ESC byte in Zig sources (`invalid escape character: 'e'`, `string literal contains invalid byte`): write source and scripts with the file tool. A batch file that a running build invokes must not be edited: `cmd` reads batches by byte offset, and rewriting `run.cmd` under a running suite made its next line execute mid-command, so the run ended with exit 9009 and no test result. `Terminal vt` tests that build `Terminal.init(alloc, .{...})` in this fork take no `io` argument, unlike upstream's, when porting upstream tests.
- 2026-10-15: Windows paths in links and quick select (PKG-42, G053/G054/G065) came down to one regex, one resolver and a crash class that four review rounds kept widening. (1) Quick select kept its own drive/UNC patterns next to `config_url.regex`, and `resolveCandidates` keeps the LONGER of two matches on the same cells, so any second path pattern with looser bounds beats a fixed one: `default_patterns` now takes every path shape from `url.regex`, which has the Windows branches (drive, UNC, `.\`, `..\`, `dir\file.ext`, `:line:col`, `(line,col)`). A path ends at `;`, `,`, `"`, an unbalanced bracket and a trailing `.`, `!`, `'`, `}`; a bracket pair is one atom (`Program Files (x86)`, `(12,5)`), which makes the unbalanced one a delimiter without a lookbehind (Oniguruma cannot take a variable-length body in one). A space continues a path after a word whose last segment has no dot, and after one that has a dot (`git.exe`, `Node.js`) only when the next word is directory-like: `(?:DOTLESS gap | DOTTED gap(?=\w[^\x00-\x20:"/]*\\))* WORD`. Two earlier drafts failed in review: keying on the FIRST word only swallowed `git.exe failed with exit code 1` after any path with a space in a directory, and "the next word has a separator" joined a URL, `.\b.txt` or `b/a.txt` onto the file name before it (`C:\a.txt https://x/y`). Prose after a dotless folder is still swallowed and cannot be told from a folder with a space; forward-slash paths with a dotted folder and a space still split. `C:/x` never becomes `/x` because the drive branch starts earlier in the text (leftmost match wins), so the rooted POSIX lookbehind did not need touching; `\\?\C:\x` and `\\.\pipe\x` stay out (every Windows root refuses a leading `\`, `/` or `\w`, the UNC root a `.` or `?` server), `@C:\x` is a path (response files) and `user@h:/x` is not a drive (`(?<!\w@)`). (2) A location is not part of the name, but a name can end in `(1)`: `Surface.resolveOpenPath` tries the whole text, then `url.splitLocation`. Stripping creates shapes the whole text did not have, and every one that reaches `std.posix.faccessatW` as `STATUS_OBJECT_NAME_INVALID` becomes `unreachable`, i.e. Ctrl+click on text any program can print aborted the process. Found one at a time: a stream of only dots and spaces (`b:.`, `b: `; measured by the reviewer with `NtQueryAttributesFile`: `C:\Windows\b: ` invalid, `b:x `, `b:x.`, `b: x` not found), a final component that is a DOS device name (`nul`, `nul:12`, `nul.`, `nul `: `RtlGetFullPathName_U` makes `\\.\nul` and Zig prefixes that again; `con`, `aux`, `prn`, `com9`, `lpt1`, `nul.txt` were not mapped on build 26200 but older Windows maps them, so all are refused, and `./nul` aborted on main already), and a stream on a leaf named `.` or `..` (`C:\x\.:12`, which `resolve` does not fold). `windowsPathIsRepresentable` is the one gate for all of them; every `accessAbsolute` caller must pass it `resolve` output. Hand-picking shapes missed two classes, a sweep did not: the reviewer replaced `accessAbsolute` with the same `NtQueryAttributesFile` call in a scratch clone, counted the statuses `faccessatW` maps to `unreachable`, ran each chunk in a child process to bisect aborts, and drove it with every string up to length 5 (3.4M) over a nasty alphabet, every regex match of every string up to length 6 (67M strings), and 22M random strings, each bare and with `:12`, `:12:5`, `(3,4)`; at the final head: 0 aborts, 0 panics, 0 leaks. Do that for any new path-to-filesystem seam. (3) Zig 0.15.2 `std.fs.path.resolve` has two panics of its own: a drive-relative name joined onto an absolute base (`b:x`, `D:x`, also `1:x`: the parser takes any byte before the colon as a drive; `index out of bounds: index 0, len 0` in `compareDiskDesignators`), and `\\a\/` (a share that is a lone separator: the parser splits on `\`, `resolve` on both, and it unwraps a null at path.zig:593). `existingPath` returns null for a drive-relative name and screens `isNetworkOrDevicePath` before `resolve`. (4) Do not ask the filesystem whether a link target exists with `accessAbsolute`: it follows a directory symlink or junction to a share (`C:\link\x.txt:12` into `\\server\share`) and a mapped drive over the network on the UI thread, before the opener's gate can refuse them (Greptile found this after the `GetDriveTypeW` check that covered only mapped drives). `Surface.existingPath` asks `apprt/win32/local_target.zig` `open` instead, the opener's own bounded lookup (local drive letter only, `OBJ_DONT_REPARSE`, no unchecked reparse target followed), which also answers with a status where `faccessatW` has `unreachable`; a path without a drive letter is refused there too. (5) `selectWord` stays as it is: a double click already asks `linkAtPin` first, so once the regex matches the drive letter is in the selection (`StringMap` test with the click on the drive letter, the colon and inside); the UIA text provider cannot confirm it at runtime because `GetSelection` answers with a degenerate caret range until the accessibility session refreshes, which a posted double click did not trigger within 2.5 s. (6) Hidden-desktop harness traps: a window launched `SW_HIDE` cannot open quick select (`quickSelectCanActivate` needs a visible surface), so use `SW_SHOWNOACTIVATE` on the private desktop; OSC 7 needs a local host name and `file:///C:/...` is rejected, while `ESC ] 9 ; 9 ; <plain path> ESC \` sets the pane directory; `UIA TextPattern.FindText` returns the first occurrence, so a needle such as `\f\a.txt` can sit inside an earlier path; holding Ctrl without injecting input needs `AttachThreadInput` to STAY attached from `SetKeyboardState` through the key-down (a version that detached at once did not get the modifier through, and the label degraded to a copy); `bash` heredocs and `python -c` strings halve backslashes, so write scripts with a file tool; MSYS rewrites `:` to `;` in environment variable values passed to native programs (a path probe through `P42_TEXT` printed `b;.;1`); `-Dtest-filter=apprt.win32.test` ran 522 tests where `-Dtest-filter=win32` ran 1845, because tests declared inside `App` carry other qualified names. (7) Measured cost: the new branches add 15% to 39% per search on a mixed set (old 10,536, 9,856 and 8,270 ms, new 12,094, 11,569 and 11,532 ms for 120,000 searches, Debug oniguruma, on a loaded machine) and three times the matches; a quoted path needs its own alternatives per root, or a relative folder in single quotes runs on past its closing quote (`'.\folder' is here`; Greptile); a screen of 20 rows of 199 characters without a space plus a URL needs about 116k retry steps against the quick-select scan's 100,000 (the old regex 92k), a constant per position from the added top-level branches; the relative prefix's lookbehind has to run before its dot lookahead or each word is scanned at every position. (8) INCIDENT 2026-10-15 about 10:51-11:00 EDT: a quick-select label completed with Ctrl held falls back to a COPY wherever the target is not openable (every path target on main, and the branch while the probe's Ctrl hold was not yet effective); a hidden desktop shares the WinSta0 clipboard, so each run overwrote the maintainer's real clipboard with test text, and their previous content was lost. Nothing that can copy runs on WinSta0 now, and every probe logs `GetClipboardSequenceNumber()` before and after (739 and 739 on every later run); any step whose fallback is a copy must be A/B-ed through a unit seam or a private window station, including on the build you compare against. Reading the clipboard to check for a marker (`Get-Clipboard`, boolean only) was also over the line.
- 2026-10-15: PKG-62 bounds updater stalls with async WinHTTP. Zig 0.15.2's HTTP client exposes a connection only after its blocking TLS handshake, so a later socket watchdog misses a stalled handshake. WinHTTP phase settings alone also failed a 100 ms stalled-TLS probe; the worker now waits to a deadline, closes the async request on timeout and waits for `HANDLE_CLOSING` before releasing any callback context or read buffer. A `WinHttpReadData` for a whole buffer can wait while a peer keeps sending bytes: a progressing 600 ms fake body failed a 400 ms wait until the reader first queried available bytes and requested no more than that amount. Async read/query byte-count output arguments must be null. Keep the old 8 KiB aggregate response-head cap, gzip/deflate/identity advertisement, x-gzip alias and a buffered decoder input (flate requires at least ten bytes; this path uses 64). Hashing an already-open staged installer still rewinds before and after success. The portable managed-file lists are sets in PowerShell, but Zig must keep noctty.exe last for the swap transaction.
- 2026-10-15: Start-up UI-thread stalls (STARTUP-STALL; measured while PKG-38 found no mutex starvation). Three stalls fall in the first two seconds of every launch. (1) The jump list's startup profile discovery ran `windows_shell.discoverProfiles` on the UI thread from its 500 ms debounce timer; that waits for `wsl.exe --status` and `wsl.exe -l -q` (1.5 s timeouts each), 85–125 ms here and 466 ms once on a busy machine. It now runs on a detached worker (`App.startProfileDiscovery`, `profileDiscoveryThreadMain`) that holds a reference on the App delivery channel and allocates into an arena over the page allocator, like the update check; its `WM_WINHOSTTY_PROFILES` result is only stored by the message window procedure and applied at the top of the outer pump, because the message can be dispatched inside a native modal loop with the profile menu, whose items index `Host.profiles`, still open. `App.profiles_generation` (bumped in `Host.applyProfileDiscovery`, `Host.invalidateProfiles` and on an `ssh-config-hosts` reload) drops a result that went stale. A worker can hang for good: `std.process.Child` and `Command.zig` both spawn with `bInheritHandles=TRUE`, so a shell started at the same moment can inherit the write end of `wsl.exe -l -q`'s stdout pipe and the drain never sees EOF; after 10 s a new attempt starts. (2) The visible 140–230 ms frame gap is one `WM_PAINT` whose `drawFrame` blocks at its first GL call (`frame.uniforms.sync`, a one-struct upload): the driver waits on its own work, F028 (PKG-73). It happens with the wsl.exe calls stubbed out too, so do not blame the discovery for it. (3) `JumpList.flush`'s COM commit takes 52–92 ms on the UI thread at start-up and after recent-directory changes; not moved yet. Traps: instrument with checkpoints, not guesses. The first suspect for the paint, a long `draw_mutex` wait, was zero; the stall sat between two checkpoints around `uniforms.sync`. `Command.clone` leaked on a part-way allocation failure in `.direct`, invisible until a failing-allocator test used a `.direct` command. A WM_APP number can collide with a PR merged meanwhile (#368 took `WM_APP + 13` during this one); recheck `consts.zig` after every rebase.
- 2026-10-15: PKG-48's cmd/Clink checks found that a shipped `clink.lua` collides with Clink's own script and is skipped; use a unique filename. A last-priority prompt filter is skipped when a replacing theme returns `prompt, false`; `surround()` cannot inspect the final prompt and duplicates Clink's own marks. Guarded `_internal._filter_prompt` wrapping preserved normal marks in portable Clink 1.9.34 while leaving right/transient prompts alone (17 real checks, hidden conhost and portable noctty). Clearing `NoDefaultCurrentDirectoryInExePath` before a target immediately restores cwd lookup. Keep it inherited, apply it after overrides only for the final cmd wrapper, and leave the raw command unchanged: quoted absolute paths still expand percent/bang variables, while native PATH lookup handles those literal directory names. Existing empty guards are effective too; explicit relative PATH entries remain deliberate cwd searches. Native cmd tests need writable stderr for not-found diagnostics and `/D` to isolate AutoRun. The elevated host starts in the system directory; the terminal keeps its cwd argument.
- 2026-10-15: PKG-55 (UIA terminal text), five traps. (1) `UIA_ToolTipControlTypeId` was 50042 where UIAutomationClient.h says 50022 (50042 is no control type); a diff of every id in `win32_uia/constants.zig` against UIAutomationClient.h and UIAutomationCore.h (`LiveSetting`) found that one mismatch. `constants.zig` now carries a table of the SDK values that a test checks, and the test fails for a declared id that is not in the table, so a new constant has to be pinned when it is added; the table check needs `@setEvalBranchQuota` (59 x 59 inline comparisons exceed the default 1000). The tooltip provider's ControlType is also pinned to the literal 50022 in a widgets test, since asking the constant would agree with a wrong constant. The live tooltip could not be inspected: `presentTooltip` returns unless `GetCursorPos` lies inside the tab, and on a private desktop `GetCursorPos` fails with error 5 (measured, 40 of 40 calls), so a hidden-desktop run can never show a tab or search-button tooltip; only unit tests cover its UIA identity. (2) The spoken-output capture (`SemanticOutputCapture`, fed by `StreamHandler`) took every printed codepoint whatever the screen, so a program on the alternate screen, which repaints by cursor addressing, was announced as its changed cells in write order. `StreamHandler.capturesSemanticOutput` (main status display on the primary screen) now gates print, REP, tab, LF and CR, evaluated per action so a switch inside one PTY batch (47, 1047 and 1049) is honoured and the text before and after it still arrives ("main" + "back" in one batch). Measured on a hidden desktop with a UIA client (`AddAutomationEventHandler` accepts the Notification event id 20035 and counts it; the generic handler cannot read the notification text), bundled ConPTY, debug portable copies of main and the branch, two runs each: 3 lines on the primary screen raised 3 Notification events on both; 40 positioned repaints on the alternate screen raised 40 on main (one per repaint) and 0 on the branch, while TextChanged kept firing there (21-40 events; the UIA client coalesces) and 2 lines after leaving raised 2 on both. With `NOCTTY_CONPTY=inbox` set, the branch also raised 0 for the alternate-screen burst (the in-box stream differed, 3 Notification events for the last 2 lines; the in-box log line was not captured, so that it was the in-box host is inferred from the difference). Not measured: what NVDA or Narrator say about the alternate screen now. Everything run inside tmux or zellij is on the alternate screen and is no longer queued as speech. The echo-suppression queue (`recent_input`) is no longer emptied by a full-screen program's own redraws, so for up to its 1 s window after the last key a primary-screen line that begins with exactly the typed characters is treated as their echo (inferred from the code, not run). (3) `snapshotTerminalAccessiblePlainText` called `PageList.pointFromPin(.screen, pin)` for every byte of the document window while holding the renderer mutex; that call walks from the first page to the pin's page, so a snapshot cost bytes x pages. `ScreenRows` in `win32_uia/text.zig` resolves the first pin and derives later ones from the page they share or follow (a pin from anywhere else walks again, and `walks` counts them). Measured in a Debug test over 173 pages (100,000 printed rows, 41,920 pins of the last 524 rows): 10,368 us per-byte against 236 us, identical rows. `Selection.contains` derives `topLeft` and `bottomRight` on every call (two `order` calls and three more walks, about seven per call, from reading the code), so the rectangle-selection loop in `selectionOffsets` now tests the columns it computed once; the active row is an endpoint's row, hence inside the rectangle's rows. Test traps: `Terminal.printString("...\n")` is a bare LF, so lines staircase and wrap at 80 columns (use "\r\n" when a test counts rows); a page holds 579 rows at 80 columns, and the 10,000,000-byte scrollback limit already pruned a 20,000-row print, so a line's number is not its screen row; the formatter gives the `\n` between two pages the next page's first-row pin, so a test that checks each byte's row must skip it; a rectangle test on one page cannot tell a pin's own `y` from its screen row (a mutant using `pin.y` passed the old test and fails the later-page one). (4) About 330 net lines (469 removed from text.zig) of first-slice text-pattern code were deleted: `TerminalTextSnapshot`, `TerminalTextMetadata`, `terminalTextMetadata`, `snapshotTerminalPlainText`, `snapshotTerminalVisiblePlainText`, `visibleRangeInDocument`, the line and UTF-16 offset builders, their nine tests, the `mod.zig` re-exports, `mod.handleGetObject` and `widgets.handleTerminalGetObject`. The live `WM_GETOBJECT` root branch in `win32.zig` is a different, caching function that creates its provider with `std.heap.page_allocator` on purpose (a COM client can hold the provider past Host teardown), so it was not routed through anything. `com.SupportedTextSelection_Multiple` stays: it completes the SDK enum next to `_None` and `_Single`, which are used. (5) Harness: every process of the run was started with `CreateProcessW` and an explicit `lpDesktop = WinSta0\<desktop>`, and the driver checked each window's desktop by name (`GetThreadDesktop` + `GetUserObjectInformationW`, `UOI_NAME`) against its own before driving it; `Desktop-Of` as `-eq` on an `EnumWindows` list is not that check (it printed False for a window that was on the desktop). The test pane ran a non-interactive `pwsh -File` (no PSReadLine, so no history line), and the PSReadLine history file's size and mtime and `startup-attempts.json`'s hash and mtime were unchanged around every batch.
- 2026-10-15: Conditional config replay preserves diagnostic locations (SMALL-06 / THEME-02, Refs #356 and #363). `parseManuallyHook` records `cli.Location` alongside each replay argument; theme wrapping preserves it, `Replay.Step.clone` clones file paths, and `Replay.Iterator.location` clones the last yielded argument's location into the destination allocator. Using `idx` directly is wrong because `next` increments it before the parser asks for the location, and borrowing a file path is wrong because `loadTheme` frees the source config after replay. Failing first on fresh main: the conditional filter reported 76/78 passed, 1 skipped, 1 failed (`expected .file, found .none`), against a 69/70 passed, 1 skipped no-match baseline. Coverage includes unknown fields and invalid values in the original file, real light/dark theme files through a round trip, a cloned config after the source is freed, skipped conditional arguments, CLI and absent locations, and allocation failures while cloning replay steps and iterator file paths. Tests use scratch theme files without launching noctty or changing the system theme.
- 2026-10-15: The ConPTY key-input probe decoded the raw bytes between its markers, but the in-box conhost can put its window-title sequence (the test binary's path, with a build hash) in the middle of the child's hex digits, and on #367's head it failed on two runs in a row (FLAKY-04, seen as `KeyInputProbeOddHexLength` and then `InvalidCharacter` on #367's head while #365 and #366 passed). Strip escape sequences and line breaks from conhost output before decoding anything a child wrote, and still refuse other bytes.
- 2026-10-15: The quick terminal is its host, not a pane (PKG-43, F040-F042). `Host.quick_terminal` is the identity: `App.quickTerminalHost()` walks `hosts`, and `Surface.quick_terminal` is gone. The per-surface flag only ever marked the pane the window was created with, so once that pane closed `toggle_quick_terminal` found no quick terminal and made a second one while the first stayed topmost (measured with `test/windows/hidden-win11-quick-terminal-identity.ps1` on a ReleaseFast portable copy of main: 3 hosts after the toggle, the old one still visible), autohide stopped, and session save would have written extra quick-terminal tabs as ordinary windows (`hostSessionTabs` is now empty for that host). Traps. (1) Hiding it is `Host.setVisible(false)`, which marks every tab's surfaces not visible, so showing it has to set the active tab's panes visible again, except the ones a zoom keeps out of view; `WM_SHOWWINDOW` refreshes occlusion but not `window_visible`. (2) A quick terminal whose only tab was closed with `close_tab:this` stays as an empty host with the surface detached for undo. The old lookup found that surface by its flag and `setVisible(true)`d it. The toggle now dismisses the empty window when it is on screen, and otherwise opens a fresh tab in it (`createWindowSurface(.{ .host_id })`, `setFloatWindow(.on)`, `showHostSurface`): floating is per surface and a tab switch re-applies the active surface's, so a fresh tab that forgot it dropped the window below others. (3) `toggle_visibility` called `setVisible(true)` on every host, so a dismissed quick terminal came back. Each host now keeps `hidden_by_toggle`: set at hide time from `IsWindowVisible`, or-ed so a second hide does not forget a window an earlier one took off screen (review measured an ordinary window hidden for good by hide, quick-terminal toggle, hide, show), and cleared by any `WM_SHOWWINDOW` show. The final activation had the same bug through `primarySurface()`, which is hosts[0] and so the quick terminal when it was created first; it goes through `shownSurfaceForTarget` now. (4) New windows: `newWindowPlacement` is the one helper behind `App.inheritHostWindowState` and `Surface.inheritWindowStateFrom`, which both copied `GetWindowRect`. A minimized source's rect is not its place: on this Windows 11 build (26300) an iconic window reports (0,2049)-(237,2188), a 237x139 sliver under the taskbar (the audit's (-32000,-32000) is the older shell; both are off screen), so the window opened from it was unusable. It now takes `GetWindowPlacement().rcNormalPosition` (workspace coordinates, converted with `leaveFullscreen`'s convention, the source monitor's work-area origin; not measured with an offset taskbar, this machine's is 0), one cascade step (32 DIP) down and right and on past any window already at that corner (every forwarded launch is opened from hosts[0]), fitted into the source's work area (`fitRectInWork`, shared with `fullscreenRestoreRect`). Maximized sources keep `maximizeWithPlacement` and their own normal rect. Four more traps. `WPF_RESTORETOMAXIMIZED` stays set in the placement of a normal window that was maximized, minimized and restored twice (a build that read it alone failed its test, because a same-host `syncHostWindowState` maximized the user's window), so it counts only with `IsIconic`. A fullscreen source gets its monitor's rect (its own rect is a sliver once it is minimized), with no cascade and no `SWP_NOMOVE` from `window-position-x/y`, or the clone shows the taskbar. `window-position-x/y` still decides where it goes and the source decides only the size. And a copy of the quick terminal is topmost and sits on its strip (measured: topmost, (0,0)-(3840,522)), so `newWindowSource` follows an ordinary window instead, or none. Test traps: the `TestSession` fixtures create no chrome window classes, so `activateSurface`, `showHostSurface` and `Host.layout` on a host with a tab fail inside `refreshChrome` and leave `cached_window_title` behind, which the leak check fails (set `quick-terminal-keyboard-interactivity = none`, detach the tab, or free that string); `EnumChildWindows` in the harness also lists a surface detached for undo, so pick the visible pane; a `-Dtest-filter` token with a space does not survive `cmd.exe //c`; and `cmd.exe //c wrapper.cmd` does not search the current directory (use `.\wrapper.cmd`). Still open: saving a named layout from the quick terminal says "Layout could not be saved." (it has no session tabs), and a maximized source's clone restores to the source's normal rect, uncascaded and ignoring `window-position-x/y`.
- 2026-10-15: Adopted-console show/hide and first caption (SMALL-06 / HANDOFF-02, Refs #352). OpenConsole reports client ShowWindow with CSI 1t/2t; only an adopted Surface follows them, minimizing on hide and restoring only an iconic host on show so maximized/snapped state survives. Queue every request and test iconic state at native delivery: checking it while draining the Surface mailbox loses the last request in hide/show and show/hide bursts (both directions now tested on a private desktop). Reverse synchronization uses IsWindowVisible && !IsIconic for every adopted pane in all tabs, independent of renderer occlusion and DWM cloaking, and writes ShowHideWindow as ushort 1 plus a ushort boolean under the same signal mutex as resize/close. OpenConsole starts with a hidden pseudo HWND, so prime visible before publishing the accepted handoff, not at later IO startup: a modal loop can defer adoption after EstablishPtyHandoff returns and the first client SW_HIDE would be swallowed; a pipe test observes the packet before the queue callback. Install the startup title after core initialization and before Surface.init first shows the host; a WM_SHOWWINDOW observer measured noctty before the fix and the client caption after, while a configured title still wins and later console titles stay dynamic. Tests use private-desktop native windows, fake adopted handles and scratch HKCU registration only; no real default-terminal handoff or OpenConsole animation was run. The native visibility test drains the private messages into the production delivery helper, rather than dispatching through the real host wndproc.
- 2026-10-15: Undo/redo of an in-place close, the order a zoomed layout shows panes in, and the shell model's focused window (SMALL-04 B; follows PKG-18 and PKG-28, whose "Still imperfect" lists named all three). (1) Closing a tab behind the front one from its button leaves the front tab alone, but its undo brought it to the front (`restoreClosedTabEntryNative` set `active_tab = insert_index`, and the model's `restoreTab` set `window.active_tab`), so the redo then closed the front tab and `activeTabAfterRemoval` and `detachTab` handed the front to its neighbour: tabs [A, B, C, D] with C in front, close A, undo, redo ended with B in front. `CloseTabUndo.was_active` records whether the tab was in front when it closed. Undo restores a tab that was behind back behind the front one (the front index moves up when the tab lands before it) and the intent `restore_tab` takes `activate`, which a window with no tab in front ignores; redo refreshes `was_active` from the tab as it stands then, so a tab clicked to the front in between is restored in front by the next undo. Undo and redo now leave the tab the close left in front, natively and in the model (`validateShellNativeMapping` holds at every step). The commit-failure path of `restoreClosedTabEntry` also puts `active_tab` back, which it did not. Closing the front tab is unchanged (undo brings it back in front, redo gives the front to the tab at its place). The pane half was re-read, not changed: undo of a split focuses the pane it came from and redo the pane it made, which is what the close and the split chose. Measured on a hidden desktop with portable Debug copies, tab labels read with `WM_GETTEXT` (the front tab starts with `* `): four tabs, the third in front, middle click tab 1, undo, redo gave `* 2`, `* 1`, and three buttons with none starred on main (the redo failed, below, and the rolled-back tab had no button) and `* 2`, `* 3`, `* 2` after. The same measurement found a second fault in the flow: a redo whose tab is in front at that moment (the front tab closed in place, undone, redone) failed in the real app with `key binding action failed action=redo err=StaleRevision`, on main and on the first fixed build, and the native rollback it runs puts the tab back after `destroyTabButton` without a new button until the next chrome pass. `redoClosedTabEntry` prepared the shell `detach_tab` before hiding the tab's panes; hiding them moves the keyboard to the next tab, and that focus message commits to the model (`noteSurfaceFocused`) and bumps the revision before the redo's own commit, so the commit was stale. It now commits with `commitRetryingInterleavedFocus`, as a pane's close and a split's undo do. The seam `test_before_structural_shell_commit` (renamed from `test_before_split_undo_shell_commit`) interleaves that focus change in the test, which fails with `StaleRevision` without the retry. Live after the change, the front tab's redo completes and the strip is right (`* 2` of two tabs); the Debug `TabCountMismatch` invariant lines that focus message logs remain (the focus audit sees the native list one tab short, as PKG-28 noted). (2) `Host.layout` walked the zoomed tab's panes once, showing the zoomed pane when it reached it and hiding the others as it reached them, so moving the zoom to a pane the tree visits before the old one showed it while the old one was still up: two GL child windows over each other for the length of the pass, the hazard `prepareActiveTabVisibility` already guards between tabs (an NVIDIA present crash). The other panes are now hidden in a first pass. Leaving a zoom (what `goto_split` does by default, `split-preserve-zoom` off, and `toggle_split_zoom`) had the same fault in the non-zoomed branch, found in review: that branch shows each pane and only then places it, so a pane coming out of the zoom was shown at the full-size rect it last had, over the zoomed pane, whichever pane the tree visits first. Now the panes already on screen are placed first (the zoomed one shrinks to its slot) and each pane is placed before it is shown. Measured with a `SetWinEventHook(EVENT_OBJECT_SHOW..HIDE)` client on the private desktop (events come in the order of the calls): `goto_split:right` with `split-preserve-zoom = navigation` gave SHOW then HIDE on main and HIDE then SHOW after; the other direction was already in order. The unit test subclasses the stand-in panes and notes any `WM_SHOWWINDOW` that arrives while the other pane is on screen and its rect overlaps this one's (that message is sent before the change, at the rect the window still has); it moves the zoom both ways and leaves it from each pane, and fails with either half of the non-zoomed fix disabled (the order of the passes, and placing before showing). Live, with `split-preserve-zoom` off and the same hook also reporting `EVENT_OBJECT_LOCATIONCHANGE`: `goto_split:right` out of a zoomed pane1 gave SHOW pane2 then MOVE pane1 (the zoomed pane shrinking) on main, MOVE pane1 then SHOW pane2 after; out of a zoomed pane2 the order was already right on both. (3) `commitShellSurfaceFocus` after a no-focus activation (`activateSurfaceNoFocus`: a pane exits or a background tab closes in a window that is not in front) went through `Runtime.focusPane`, which also sets `focused_window`, so the model named a background window as the focused one. `Runtime.activatePane` makes the pane the tab's focused pane and the tab the window's active tab without touching `focused_window` (unless the model has none, which `validate` forbids), and `commitShellSurfaceActivation` is the no-focus path's call; real focus (`noteSurfaceFocused`, `activateSurface`) still moves the window. Nothing reads `focused_window` outside tests and the reducers (F045), so this was not measurable at runtime. Windows created without taking focus (the quick terminal with `quick-terminal-keyboard-interactivity = none`) are still recorded as the focused window by `createWindow`; left. Harness traps: a posted `WM_MBUTTONUP` to a tab button closes that tab in place and a `WM_LBUTTONDOWN` + `WM_LBUTTONUP` pair at x = 20 activates it; a tab button is a `BUTTON` whose control id is 1000 + the index it was made at; `GetGUIThreadInfo(...).hwndFocus` names the focused pane for posting a key; an out-of-context WinEvent hook delivers only while its thread pumps messages.
- 2026-10-15: Settings accessibility and feedback (PKG-40b). Seven things worth knowing. (1) A raw scalar field (scrollback, font size, opacity, padding) parsed on every keystroke and raised its error at once, so the "1" of "16" said "Font size must be from 6 through 72 points". The message now waits `validation_quiet_ms` (400) without a key (`SetTimer` with one fixed id restarts the countdown, so it is a quiet period, and `WM_TIMER` calls `validationQuietElapsed`), for `EN_KILLFOCUS` of that field (posted on as `WM_SETTINGS_FIELD_LEFT`, because the notification does not say where focus went, and skipped when it went to Discard changes, whose click throws the text away), or for `save()` and the Ctrl+S that is refused because of it. Two invariants carry it. A dirty raw field always has a message recorded in `raw_scalar_error`, shown or not, and Save stays off through `hasRawScalarEdits`, never through `validation_control`, which is only set once the message is shown; so anything that asks "is something invalid" before saving has to call `showRawScalarErrors` first (`save()` does, which also covers the close prompt's "Save and close" calling it directly, and a mutation that skips it saves past a message that never appeared). An error already on screen follows the text instead of vanishing and reappearing. (2) The cues are not colour alone: "Error: " leads the status line, the field keeps EXT-06's error stroke (which now wins over the focus stroke: the error is only on screen after the quiet period, so it no longer flashes per key, and a focused field that says it is wrong has to look wrong), the section's rail button draws a larger disc with "!" for an invalid value or a small dot for unsaved changes (a text colour in High Contrast, where the stroke and the error text are the same colour), and the title reads "noctty settings (unsaved changes)". `syncMarkers` runs at the end of `updateSaveEnabled`, so every state change reaches the rail, the title and UIA through one path. The rail button's UIA name is unchanged (the accessibility harness finds the sections by it); the state is `ItemStatus` ("Has an invalid value", "Has unsaved changes"), and a field says `IsDataValidForForm` false. (3) The status line and the close prompt are `live_text` (Text, LiveSetting Polite) and `announceText` raises them only while visible, because a reader drops an offscreen live region. The status line changes with nearly every edit ("Applies when you save."), so only errors, conflicts and the outcome of a save are announced (`statusAnnounces`). (4) A UIA client attached to the machine holds a provider that raised an event until it has read it, which outlives a test that frees the provider: the two new provider tests leaked on this machine and not on CI. `events.raisePropertyChanged` now counts under test (`testing_property_changes`) like the structure changes. (5) Adding one `utf8ToUtf16LeStringLiteral` to `SettingsWindow.open` failed the build with "evaluation exceeded 1000 backwards branches" in an unrelated literal (the blur label): the function's literals share a quota. A new label goes in a file-level constant (`accent_follow_label`) or the quota gets raised. (6) "Discard changes" beside Save re-reads the saved config through `adoptCurrentConfig`, the path a reopened window uses after a discarded close, so live previews roll back through `clearPending`, inside `adoptConfig`. Discard copies the saved config from the window's own `current` snapshot, not from `currentConfig`: the App's preview writes the draft into `app.config`, which is what `currentConfig` returns, so cloning that (what the reopen after a discarded close does) kept the discarded values in Settings while the terminals went back (the review found it; a Settings fixture whose `previewField` does not write into its config cannot fail it, `Fixture.live_previews` does). A first fix that rolled the previews back and then copied cleared the tracking before the fallible copies (Greptile): with memory failing, the form kept 16, `hasPendingChanges` said false and a close skipped its prompt; the copies come first now, and a test with a failing allocator pins it. `annotateAccessibleControls` now loops `settings_control_specs` for the fields and dropdowns only: a checkbox or button speaks its own caption through MSAA, and the spec names ("Clipboard trimming" for "Trim trailing spaces on copy") would break voice control by label. The accent-follow-system checkbox, whose caption, label and name are all "Follow Windows accent color", is control 27 and shifts the action-bar controls to 28..34 (`settings_header_control_count` 7); its preview rebuilds the theme (`PreviewEffect.theme`), because `resolveTheme` reads it. (7) Not done: F093 (Open in default editor on a profile with no `.ghostty` association). `HKCR\.ghostty` does not exist on the dev profile, so the premise holds here, but what `ShellExecuteW("open")` does with an unassociated file (the Open-with picker, or `SE_ERR_NOASSOC`) was not measured: both would show UI, which the hidden-desktop rule forbids running, and the open itself is deferred to the message loop (`runDeferredOpens`) with no path back to the Settings status line. A fix needs both. `test/windows/interactive-win11-accessibility.ps1` now lists "Discard changes" and "Follow Windows accent color" among the controls it expects; it drives the real desktop and was not run.
- 2026-10-15: Renderer pacing (PKG-37: F114, F022, F119, F120, F160; F112 was already fixed by #357's `frame_update_deferred and !draw_active`, now `followupOwesFrameUpdate`). (1) Synchronized output (mode 2026) held across reads re-presented the same frame about once per 15.6 ms tick until ESU or termio's 1 s reset: `updateFrame` returns early while the mode is set, but `renderOnce` still queued the paint, and the dirt the program wrote under the hold kept the follow-up rendering. A program that holds BSU for 200 ms across four writes, ten times (scratch `held.c`), got 127-141 presents inside the holds on main and 0 after, every cycle still shown. `FrameUpdate.synchronized_output` marks the held update; `renderOnce` then releases its reservation, keeps the update owed (`frame_update_deferred`) and returns true so the follow-up polls, because termio's reset always wakes the renderer but an ESU only when it brings dirt (`shouldWakeRendererAfterOutput`): a clean ESU after a renderer-side change (a search highlight) is found only by that poll. Two passes still present the held frame: the custom-shader draw timer's (`RenderPass.animation`, the shader animates over it) and any pass while the apprt is owed pixels (`HeldFrame.repaint_owed`, set by a wake `wakeNeedsPixels` accepts and kept through a pass pacing put off). A WM_PAINT that finds no frame reserved draws nothing itself, it only wakes the renderer (`apprt_paint_retry`), so without that an exposed or newly shown pane stayed unpainted until the hold ended: invalidating the child every 50 ms during holds gave 0 presents inside the holds on the first draft and about one per invalidation now, also under saver pacing (independent review found both, the throttled case in its second round). `apprt_paint_pending` is not a pixels request: it forces the pending paint, whose WM_PAINT asks again if it has no frame. (2) Pacing measured its interval with GetTickCount64 (15.6 ms steps), so `power-saver-rendering` and `unfocused-render-fps = 30` (34 ms) presented every 47 ms: a 60 fps TUI under saver pacing got 21.2 presents/s on main and 29.3 now (mean interval 47.0 to 34.0 ms), and typing bursts under saver pacing showed 27.8 / 53.9 ms median / p95 to the emulated display on main against 11.8 / 33.9. Both halves were needed. `win32_power.pacingClockMs` reads QPC, but libxev's IOCP timers wait in GetQueuedCompletionStatusEx, whose timeout expires on the system tick, so a timer-driven present (a deferred update, a paced shader frame) still lands up to a tick after its slot opened, and measuring the next interval from there turns 34 ms into 46.875 again (17 ms into 31.25). `PresentPacer.take` starts the next slot where the last one opened when the present was at most a tick late (`timer_tick_ms`); a later present (output that came late, idle) starts a new cadence, so no catch-up burst follows. Crediting half an interval (the first draft) left 17-20 ms intervals at 32 fps, and crediting up to a whole interval let a late wake-driven present be followed almost at once (two presents 1 ms apart at `unfocused-render-fps = 1`); review caught both. Timer-driven frames still cannot come faster than one a tick (64 fps), and focused panes are not paced at all. (3) The `now` and `hasVsync` comments described forced display-link draws for resize and damage; only a display link notifies `draw_now` and Win32 has none, so they never run here (upstream plumbing kept). Harness traps: `cmd //c "start \"\" /belownormal /wait ..."` hung in this agent's Git Bash even with the start inline (no child, no log); setting the calling bash's own priority class to BelowNormal (`/proc/$$/winpid`) and running `cmd.exe /d /c x.cmd </dev/null` works, and children inherit the class. Stopping the background `bash final.sh` task again left the script running its next matrix (the LAT-02 trap); its runner's command line was relative (`pwsh -File matrix.ps1`), so an ownership check keyed on the scratch path skipped it: match the script name too, by exact PID. And Python's `write_text` here wrote CRLF into a `.zig` file; it compiled, `eol=lf` normalized the commit, and the next exact-anchor patch failed on the working copy. Tails on this shared machine are noise: repeating the same saver typing case gave main a p99 of 1120 ms in one batch and the fix 50.8 ms in another, each 17-18 ms in the rest, and one 60 fps run on the fix had a 375 ms paint (`max_paint_draw_duration_ms`) followed by 190 repaint-retry wakes, which eight more runs of each build never showed; compare medians and p95 over interleaved repeats, and read the render trace before blaming a change for a tail.
- 2026-10-15: Config diagnostics belong to a load, not its first terminal (SMALL-05 / CONFIG-02, Refs #356 and #336). `reportConfigProblems` now keeps the load's banner text even with `initial-window = false`, sends it to every host and gives each later `createHost` a copy. A host keeps a separate owned config message behind another error: a pending launch-layout failure stays until dismissed, then the config problem appears; dismissing that acknowledges it on that host only, and a clean load clears queued copies before clearing active config text. Failing first on main: the two banner regressions reported 71/74 passed, 1 skipped, 2 failed (no-primary hosts got no error, and config replaced the layout error). Missing families are checked from all four config lists without needing a terminal or grid, including fallback entries; installed families with an unavailable requested style are not missing families. Families dedupe across styles and tabs, unnamed/default embedded fonts do not initialize discovery, and reload/Save re-announce the already-recorded load rather than checking twice. The font regressions first reported 87/92 passed, 1 skipped, 4 failed. The final combined banner/font filter passed 98/99, 1 skipped, against a 69/70, 1 skipped no-match baseline. A successful discovery iterator yields an OWNED `DeferredFace`: a family-existence probe must deinit it even though it never loads that face; the first implementation passed its behavior assertions but the allocator caught leaks, independently identified in review and fixed. Conditional diagnostic locations remain unavailable: `ConfigFileIterator.location` has the original place, but `Config.Replay.Step.arg` stores only text and `conditional_arg` only conditions/text, and `Replay.Iterator` exposes no location. Preserving file:line across `changeConditionalState` requires carrying `cli.Location` from `src/cli/diagnostics.zig` on replay argument steps, cloning file paths and exposing the yielded argument's place from the replay iterator; the GUI cannot reconstruct it after replay. Tests exercise the host model without launching noctty; later-host creation calls the same presentation helper used by `createHost`, without an end-to-end GUI launch. No real clipboard, config or history contents were accessed.
- 2026-10-15: Clipboard retry waits use a 5 ms QPC budget (CLIP-02), with at most ten opens spaced 0.5 ms apart and `SwitchToThread` between attempts, instead of nine 5 ms sleeps that round up to timer ticks. All three callers (text read, text write, HTML write) share it. An injected always-busy opener measured 135 ms on main and 4.501 ms median / 4.514 ms maximum over eleven runs after the change; no real clipboard was opened. Clock/yield calls can change `GetLastError`, so the last failed open's error is saved and restored. Scheduling and `OpenClipboard` itself may overrun the budget; the deadline prevents further retries, not OS preemption. Tests use an injected clock for exact-deadline and overshoot checks, and the QPC path with an injected opener. The operation stays synchronous and dispatches no messages, preserving copy/paste order and pending-confirm ownership. Never test contention by holding the real clipboard on a hidden desktop: the window station owns it.
- 2026-10-15: Tab list UIA events, background-tab prompts and the undo-off banner (SMALL-04 A; follows CLOSE-01 and PKG-25). (1) A tab joining the strip raised no UIA structure event, so a reader's tab list went stale for every add path. They all end in one place: `syncTabButtons` makes the button (new tab, new window and its clones, session restore, undo of a close, a tab moved in, a rolled-back close), so it raises `children_invalidated` on the host's root once per pass that created a button, after the layout, or from an `errdefer` when the pass fails after making one (`notifyTabListUiaChanged`, renamed from `notifyTabRemovedUia`, which still serves every removal site and the failed-`Surface.init` rollback that destroys a button a reader was told about). Raising at each `tabs.insert` would have fired before the button exists, since a same-host tab gets its button from the next activation's chrome pass. A hidden strip stays silent, as for selection. Measured on a hidden desktop with a portable Debug copy, a Windows PowerShell 5.1 `-MTA` client subscribed with `AddStructureChangedEventHandler(host, Subtree)`: new tab twice, close_tab:this, undo gave `ChildrenInvalidated` 0, 0, 1, 0 before and 1, 1, 1, 1 after (UIA also delivers a few `ChildAdded` for the new elements, before and after). The unit test needs real chrome children: the `BannerTestWindow` fixture and a private desktop, with child windows for the surfaces because layout repaints the active panes (a surface without one logs `NoWindow`, an error-level log, and fails the test). (2) `close_tab:this` for a tab that is not in front (the tab X, a middle click, Delete on its button) with `undo-timeout = 0` asked "Close this tab?", which says nothing about which. `askBeforeClosing` now words the `.this_tab` prompt for such a tab as the OSC 52 prompt words its tab: title `Close tab 2?`, body `A process is still running in Tab 2 (vim). Closing that tab will terminate it, and undo is off.`, through `labels.promptTabName` (the one place that cuts the title to 24 cells and code points and replaces controls and bidi formatting), now shared by both prompts. The other prompts: the OSC 52 prompts already name the tab; the other-tabs and right-tabs prompts run from the tab in front (a tab's context menu selects the tab it opens on); window and quit prompts have no tab. `Surface.close`'s "Close this terminal?" usually runs from the key in front, but a read-only terminal closes itself when its program exits and asks first (core `needsConfirmQuit` answers true for read-only before it looks at `child_exited`), in whichever tab it is, so it names a tab behind too (`Close terminal in tab 2?`, `Host.nameBackgroundTerminal`). That is almost always after the program has exited, so its body says so ("The program in Tab 2 (vim) has exited. Read-only mode makes closing that terminal ask first.", from `child_exited`) and says a process is running only if one is; the plain prompt for the front terminal still says "A process is still running" in the same read-only case (left). The front tab and front terminal keep their wording. A review found that case; `askBeforeClosing` takes the Surface as `userdata` for `.this_tab` (documented on `CloseScope`). (3) The banner after the last tab closes said "Use undo to restore it." (or named the undo key) with `undo-timeout = 0`, where the prune timer takes the entry at once. `tabClosedMessage` takes `undo_available` and says "Tab closed." then; `App.undoOff` is the one test for exactly 0, shared with the ask-first rule (a 1 ms timeout still offers it and still does not ask). Harness traps measured on the way: a `-Dtest-filter` value with spaces reaches `zig build` as several arguments and matches nothing (69 passed, 1 skipped is the no-match baseline), so use one token; a window launched with `STARTUPINFO.wShowWindow = SW_HIDE` stays hidden, and a child's `IsWindowVisible` is then false, so launch with `SW_SHOWNOACTIVATE` (4) on the private desktop; reading a control's text in another process needs `WM_GETTEXT` (`SendMessageTimeout`), not `GetWindowText`.
- 2026-10-15: A typed key no longer presents a frame ahead of its echo (LAT-02). `keyCallback` queued a render for every key that typed, though only a dropped selection or a jump back to the bottom changes the screen; the echo arrives as output, which wakes the renderer itself. On a vsynced display the keypress frame takes the next refresh and the echo shows a refresh later. `dropSelectionAndScrollForTyping` and `scrollToBottom` (also behind the text/csi/esc/cursor_key bindings and paste) now redraw only for what they changed. Measured with LAT-01's harness under its emulated 60 Hz present (a model, not a display): local echo 25.85 ms median / 33.17 p95 on main, 8.80 / 16.66 after; presents while typing fell from 2-3 per key to 1. Four traps. (1) That frame masked an older gap. termio wakes the renderer after output only for dirty state (#8), and moving the cursor within its row marks nothing dirty: BS, CUB, CUF, CHA, and CUP to the same row, which `Terminal.setCursorPos` sends through `cursorLeft`/`cursorRight` (a move to another row goes through `cursorChangePin`, which marks rows). DECSCUSR, DECTCEM and OSC 10/11/12 mark nothing either. Such an echo (Left at a prompt echoes a BS) was drawn by whatever frame came next: on main an arrow's echo 30 ms late reached the display at a median 113 ms, carried by the next key's keypress frame; with that frame gone it waited for the blink timer (median 243-326 ms), and with a steady cursor it would wait for unrelated output (inferred). The renderer now records `render_dirty.Undirtied` (the cursor's place and look, the default colors) at each frame update in `renderer.State.drawn_undirtied`, and termio's output wake and the render follow-up count a mismatch as work (`needsFrame`), so it stays pending across reads, synchronized output included, until drawn. A frame woken by OSC 10/11 still redraws only dirty rows' cells, an older renderer gap (inferred from the code, not run). (2) Every change of the binding mods set `dirty.clear` and redrew the whole screen, a frame for each Shift and Ctrl press. Mods only decide link highlighting, which needs `renderer_state.mouse.point` (set by `mouseRefreshLinks` only while a link is under the pointer, and now cleared when it finds none) or an `always_mods` link, so `modsChanged` checks that first. `keyCallback` compared the raw event mods with the binding-reduced mouse mods, #134's trap again: with Num Lock on, every key ran the link refresh ahead of its pty write and, in a mouse-reporting app, queued a frame. (3) libxev's IOCP loop runs a due timer before the async wakes of the same pass, so output reached the 8 ms render follow-up before its own wake: the follow-up drew it, the wake found the paint slot reserved, and its retry presented the same frame again (every other key or so; about 285 of 930 presents in 10 s of a 60 fps animation). The follow-up now leaves dirt to a recorded wake (`wakePending`), and termio records `io_output` under the lock, before the renderer can see the state. (4) Under pacing (power saver), a frame update put off that was not dirt (a scroll, a blink) was dropped by the follow-up, which only looked at dirt. It now carries one, except while the custom-shader draw timer runs: that timer carries it (#297), and a follow-up that also tried would re-defer at every slot the timer took and keep it doing full frame updates. Harness traps: `lat-echo` writes each byte separately and wraps after 60 echoed bytes counting escape bytes, so an `ESC[D` echo can arrive in three reads with CR LF inside the CSI; that, and the 500 ms blink-reset wake riding on a lone ESC, is the tail of the arrow-key numbers. From Git Bash, `cmd //c "start \"\" /belownormal /wait /b cmd /d /c \"\"x.cmd\" ...\""` reached cmd with broken quoting and sat idle with no child and no log; put the `start` in a `.cmd` wrapper called by full path. And stopping the agent's background `bash script.sh` task reported success while the script kept running its remaining steps (their logs were stamped minutes later, alongside the next run); check by PID that every process of a stopped run is gone before starting another.
- 2026-10-15: Config problems reach the GUI (PKG-22, F084 / F124 / F091). The Win32 runtime never read `Config._diagnostics`, so a hard reload said "Configuration reloaded" and a Save said "Settings saved" over an unknown key, an invalid value, a missing include or a missing theme, and a `font-family` no installed font matches fell back to the built-in font with one `log.warn`. Now `App.recordConfigProblems` keeps a copy of the diagnostics (the config that owned them is freed after a reload) and of the fonts the open terminals could not find (`config_problems.Problems`, pure and unit-tested), and `reportConfigProblems` raises an `.err` host banner, `N config problems: <first>. See Config problems in the command palette.`, after the first window of a launch, after a hard reload and after a Settings save. A load that finds nothing takes down that banner only (it compares the banner text on every host, so another error that took its place stays). Every config install also runs `refreshConfigProblems` from the `.config_change` handler, because the system switching between light and dark under `theme = light:A,dark:B` installs the other half's config without any reload or Save (Greptile caught that on #356); it keeps the list current and raises the banner only when `Problems.fingerprint` differs from before, so a banner the user closed does not return at every switch. That path was not run on a real light/dark switch (changing the system theme on the maintainer's machine is not ours to do); a unit test drives it. "Config problems" is a reviewed help row of the palette (`HelpTarget.config_problems`) that opens Settings > Advanced, whose page now reads the save target, the numbered list (`key: message (file:line)`, the place last so a 220-byte cut loses the tail of a long path and not the problem; whole entries, `noctty +validate-config` for the rest and for a cut entry) and the pending changes. Measured on a hidden desktop with a portable ReleaseFast copy and a scratch config holding `font-szie = 14`, `font-size = big`, a missing `config-file`, `theme = No-Such-Theme-pkg22` and a missing font: the banner read `5 config problems: font-szie: unknown field...` at startup, the Advanced page listed all five with file and line, the palette row opened it, a reload with only the font left said `Config problem: font-family: "..." was not found...`, a clean reload said `Configuration reloaded` and a broken one raised the banner again; a Save of a setting a later `config-file` overrides said `Saved, but a later config file or command-line option overrides font-size, so the saved value is not used.` Traps. (1) A font miss belongs to the GRID (`SharedGridSet.ReffedGrid.missing`), not to the `collection()` call: a reload that leaves the font config alone reuses the cached grid and discovers nothing, so a miss kept only at discovery time would vanish from the list on the next reload while the font was still missing (measured: it persists). The apprt reads it through `Surface.missingFontFamilies` after `updateConfig`, because that is when each surface has swapped to its new grid; a family is listed once however many tabs use it. It is reported only when NO family of the regular style matched (`font-family = SF Mono` then `font-family = Consolas` is a fallback list and the built-in font is not shown), never for the built-in font's own name `JetBrains Mono`, and never for the bold or italic lookups, which ask for the same family. (2) The loader reports the arguments that start this process as unknown: the `--safe-mode` switch (a safe-mode reload runs `Config.load`) and the positional `wgh://activate?...` URI of an Action Center launch. Both stay in argv, so every reload and every Save would raise a false error; `isLaunchArgumentDiagnostic` drops them. It cannot rely on the `.cli` location: a config rebuilt for a conditional state comes back through an iterator that has no place to give, so its diagnostics have none (greptile caught that on #356; a unit test now rebuilds with `changeConditionalState` and checks), so it keeps only the diagnostics that name a file line, and drops `safe-mode` only in a process started with `--safe-mode`. (3) `themepkg.open` appended one "not found" diagnostic per theme directory, so one typo counted as three problems; it is one diagnostic naming every path tried (and says so when there is no theme directory at all, which used to add nothing). (4) The Save notification replaced the error banner twice over: `settingsNotifySuccessThunk` raised an `.info` banner, and when the WinRT toast is refused (measured here, and under Focus Assist) `showDesktopNotification` falls back to another `.info` banner. When the reload found problems the thunk now returns after re-raising the error banner; the status line in Settings already says the save went through. (5) The problems are read from `self.config`, the config in effect, after `updateConfig`, not from the config just read: the app applies its conditional state first (`theme = light:A,dark:B` on a dark system), and the base config names the light half. Measured with a unit test: the applied clone keeps the count but its replayed diagnostics lose their file and line (the replay iterator has no location), so a config with a conditional key lists `font-szie: unknown field` without the path. (6) A static text holds 2047 UTF-16 units (`setWindowTextUtf8`, `measureStaticTextHeight`), so the Advanced page gives the target and the problems 1400 bytes in whole entries and the pending changes the rest; a diagnostic can carry non-UTF-8 bytes (Notepad's "ANSI" files), and those Win32 text calls turn an invalid string into an empty one, so `Problems.append` replaces such bytes with `?` rather than blanking the banner or the whole page. (7) `config_edit.openPath` creates the config folder and file, so showing the save target uses `file_load.preferredDefaultFilePath` (`settingsTargetPathForDisplay`); a Save still goes through `settingsTargetPath`. (8) The masked fields come back through `App.settings_masked_keys` (an `AppHandle.maskedKeys` lender, like `failureDetail`) and `settingsSaveAndReload` still returns `error.SavedButMasked`; the status line and the notification name the config keys (`font-size, theme`), which is what a user greps for in the files, and the names are written to a fixed buffer because anything that can fail after `persistSettings` wrote the file reports a failed save for a saved file. (9) An `append` that frees its arguments on failure plus callers that `errdefer` the same buffers is a double free under OOM; `Problems.append` keeps ownership with the caller and `checkAllAllocationFailures` covers it. Harness traps: a pwsh driver on a private desktop starting noctty with `Start-Process` may land it on the visible desktop (HARD RULE 5), so the driver calls `CreateProcessW` with `lpDesktop = WinSta0\<name>` itself; with `STARTF_USESHOWWINDOW` and `wShowWindow = 0` the host window is created hidden and the driver sees no window; `EnumWindows` from a thread on the private desktop finding the host is the proof of which desktop it is on; and this agent's Bash heredocs turn `\\` into `\`, which broke a Zig string literal (write test code with a file tool). Left as it was: the banner is raised on the primary window only; with `initial-window = false` the problems wait in Settings and the palette until a reload; a startup `launch-layout` failure banner is replaced by the config banner; a font a later new window or profile asks for is not checked; the fallback banner of a refused toast reads `Settings saved: ` with a trailing colon when the body is empty.
- 2026-10-15: PKG-29 (updater check and staging lifecycle). Four findings, all re-verified on main before the change. (1) `checkLatestStableRelease` answers from the 24 hour throttle with `cachedAvailableRelease`, which has no `windows_install` (the state file keeps the version and URL, not the asset list), so `stageWindowsInstall` returned `WindowsInstallNotEligible` for every cached release: a restart inside the window offered "Open Release" although a verified install was staged, a stage that failed once was not retried until the throttle expired, and a forced check downloaded the same 30 MB again because staging never looked at `staged_version`. `stageWindowsInstallReusing` now reuses a staged install first (same version, kind and feed, then `verifyStagedWindowsInstall`: hash, Authenticode, the portable manifest and extraction, under the staging lock, with the `portable_apply_phase` guard kept ahead of it). A cached release with nothing to reuse is still `WindowsInstallNotEligible`, on purpose: a first draft fetched the feed for the asset list and staged from that, and review showed a stage that keeps failing (a signature the pin rejects, a bad checksum) then cost a feed request and a ~30 MB download at every launch and every timer tick instead of one per throttle window, with the fetched metadata thrown away besides. A stage that failed once is retried at the next throttle expiry or by a manual check, as before. `reuseStagedWindowsInstall` only reads the state until it finds a match, because "portable staging fails closed before download without signed manifest asset" pins that a refusal leaves no `updates` directory behind: taking the staging lock first, the obvious place, breaks it. The unit tests inject the verifier and downloader (`StageReuseFake`), since a unit test cannot produce a pinned-publisher signature. (2) `InstallKind` (installer, portable marker, other) is detected once per process (`App.installKind`); `other` (Scoop, a ZIP without the marker) used to be treated as `.installer`, staged a setup it could never apply (`canApplyStagedUpdate` needs `unins000.exe` and `.dat`) and raised a "ready to install" notice that only opened the browser. A failed probe is `other`: refusing to stage is the safe answer. (3) The automatic check ran once per process (`update_auto_check_started` is never cleared). It now also arms a repeating thread timer (`update_recheck_timer_id`, six hours) that calls the same non-forced, dismissal-respecting check; the throttle decides whether a request is made, a tick with no window, with `running` cleared (apply in progress) or with a staged notice already up does nothing (reusing a staged portable ZIP re-extracts it, and a user pressing Install meanwhile would extract into the same directory from the UI thread, which `applyStagedUpdate` does without the staging lock), and the timer is killed in `run`'s defer and `terminate` like the other thread timers. A config reload that turns automatic checks on in a process that started with them off now starts the first check (and arms the timer) too, which CodeRabbit pointed out; the Settings live preview does not, because an unsaved preview must not cause a request. Do not clear `update_auto_check_started` on window creation instead: every new tab would run a check. (4) `dismissUpdateNotice` skipped `recordDismissal` for a staged notice, so a dismissed "Install" came back at every launch; it records the dismissal now and leaves the staged files (a manual check shows it again and reuses them). `dismissUpdateNoticeAt` takes the state path because the real one is `%LOCALAPPDATA%\noctty\update-state.json`, which the existing banner test says never to press. Harness trap: `scripts\dev-windows.cmd` runs from a batch file need the full path of the batch file under `cmd /c "start /belownormal /wait cmd /c <full path> ..."` with `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1`, and filter tokens with spaces must come from a file, not from `shift`ed arguments, which silently split them and ran the 70-test no-match baseline.
- 2026-10-15: PKG-30 (installer apply UX and package-manager cleanup). Findings re-verified on main; none was run end to end, because nothing here may run an installer, an uninstaller, Scoop or a real update apply, so the Inno behaviour below is from the Inno Setup help for `[Run]` (fetched 2026-10-15; CI pins 6.7.1, the local ISCC compiler is 6.7.3) and a compile-only ISCC run whose output was never executed. (1) F212 as written does not hold: the audit said a `postinstall` `[Run]` entry runs with Setup's elevated token unless `runasoriginaluser` is added, but the help says `runasoriginaluser` "is the default behavior when the postinstall flag is used". The help also says it has no effect when Setup was started with "Run as administrator" or from an already-elevated process, so "Launch noctty" still opened an Administrator terminal there (review caught that); both `[Run]` entries now start noctty through `{win}\explorer.exe`, which runs the file as the logged-on user's shell, with `runasoriginaluser` kept for an ordinary Setup. Explorer handing the launch to the unelevated shell is the usual de-elevation route but is not in the Inno help and was not measured; the Medium-versus-High check still needs a Sandbox run nobody here may do. (2) F213 relaunch: Setup's silent `[Run]` entries are not `postinstall`, so they default to `runascurrentuser` (elevated), and Install starts Setup with `ShellExecuteW("runas")`, the case where `runasoriginaluser` does nothing; hence Explorer. The entry runs only when Setup is silent and was given `/RELAUNCH=1` (`{param:RELAUNCH|0}`, the documented `/Name=value` form; a bare `/RELAUNCH` is not documented as readable), which `buildInstallerApplyArgs` adds. A hand-run silent install (winget, Chocolatey) does not pass it and still starts nothing. No double launch: Restart Manager only restarts applications that called `RegisterApplicationRestart`, and noctty does not. Considered and rejected: starting Setup unelevated so it elevates itself and `runasoriginaluser` works. `ShellExecuteW("runas")` returns only after the consent prompt is answered, and a declined prompt returns an error with the app still open and every session intact; with a self-elevating Setup the app would have quit before anyone answered. (3) Install now asks first, through the PKG-18 confirm: `CloseScope.update`, `App.pendingCloseAsk` (the host choice `requestQuit` made inline, now shared: the prompt already open, then the window the button was pressed in, then a shown window with a running terminal) and `updateApplyConfirmAccept`. It covers the portable Apply too, which also exits the app. The staged notice says that installing closes all windows and running sessions.
