Unsaved Settings edits: `holdQuitForUnsavedChanges` resumes a quit, not an install, so wiring it in would quit without installing; Install declines with a banner until they are saved or discarded, checked before the prompt (nobody is asked to close their sessions for an install that then declines) and again right before the apply runs (`settingsHoldInstall`), since Settings is a separate window that can be edited while the non-modal prompt is open (Greptile). It fails closed: when the banner cannot be shown (CodeRabbit: allocation failure) the install is still held, because the edits are what it would lose. The confirm's answer only queues the apply (`DeferredOpen.apply_staged_update`, `queueApplyStagedUpdate`): the answer arrives inside the overlay's handlers and `applyStagedUpdate` reaches `ShellExecuteW`, which pumps messages (the LIFE-01 measurement), so the message loop runs it from `drainDeferredOpens` after the handler has returned, the way link opens run. `update_apply_queued` (cleared when the loop takes the request off the queue) and `update_apply_in_flight` together make a second answer, while one is queued or under way, do nothing. The Settings check is not at accept time: the run follows the accept by the rest of one message handler, so a check there would only be a second source of the same banner; the run-time check is the last point before the destructive step. `applyStagedUpdate` has a re-entry guard (`update_apply_in_flight`) because `ShellExecuteW` pumps messages and a second press that gets in would raise a second UAC prompt and start a second Setup. (4) The first start after Install is where a failed apply is found (`takeFailedInstallerApply`): `apply_requested_at` is set, Setup is no longer running, and this build is still older than `staged_version`. A first draft used a ten-minute grace window instead of asking whether Setup runs; review showed the common failure (Setup fails, nothing relaunches noctty, the user starts it by hand within minutes) then got no banner. Windows refuses write access to a mapped image, so `installerImageInUse` opens the staged installer for writing and reads a sharing violation as "still running"; no process id or clock is stored, and a clock change cannot matter. This rests on the outer Setup process staying alive until its extracted copy exits (the Inno loader waits for it), which is inferred, not measured; the unit test reproduces the sharing violation with a shared-read handle. The request is cleared whichever way it ends, so it is said once; the staged installer stays so Install is offered again; portable applies (`portable_apply_phase`) are left to their own recovery. The banner names `updates\<version>\logs\apply.log` if it exists, else says Setup left no log. It cannot mean a declined UAC prompt (that returns from `ShellExecuteW` with an error before anything is recorded); it means Setup started and could not open its log, or was stopped. Only a setup-managed copy reports it: the update state is shared by every copy of the user's, so an older Scoop or dev build starting after a successful Install would otherwise show the banner and consume the request. The check runs before the automatic update check starts, because staging compares `apply_requested_at` between its baseline and its commit. `applyLogPathAlloc` builds the path for both the apply and this check. (5) G048: the Scoop manifest gets a `pre_uninstall` that runs `noctty.com +unregister-default-terminal` (the console-subsystem shim, so Scoop waits; the GUI exe returns at once and the folder would be deleted under it). Review found two ways the first draft could do harm, both now closed: `+unregister-default-terminal` checks the CLSID, not the path, so a Scoop uninstall dropped the selection of an installed or portable copy elsewhere (the installer's `OwnsDefaultTerminalRegistration` is load-bearing for the same reason), so the hook only acts on a HKCU `LocalServer32` that points into this app's Scoop folder (`apps\noctty\`), any version folder of it: `scoop update` keeps the old version folders, the registration stays on the one it was made from (the registered path is the resolved image path, so never the `current` junction; a first draft matched `current` and only this version's folder, and a register, update, uninstall sequence then left it dangling), and uninstall deletes them all; and Scoop runs `pre_uninstall` before it checks for running processes and then refuses the uninstall (Scoop `scoop-uninstall.ps1`), so typing `scoop uninstall noctty` in a noctty tab unregistered a copy that stayed installed, so the hook does nothing while a process runs from the app folder. It is gated on `$cmd -eq 'uninstall'` (Scoop sets `$cmd`; `scoop update` runs the old manifest's hook) and reports (and does not fail on) a launch failure or a non-zero exit. `test/windows/package-scoop.ps1` runs the generated hook with stand-ins for `Get-Item` and `Get-Process` (functions shadow cmdlets for a script block run below them; `GetNewClosure` sees only its own scope, so copy the value in first) and a text file as `noctty.com`, so nothing real is read or started: eight cases for what reaches the call and what does not. It fails against main's script and is wired into test.yml. The hook parses on Windows PowerShell 5.1. The manifest in the live bucket changes with the next release. (6) F221 (split): only the `+unregister-shell-menu` part is done. The installer's HKA verbs are HKLM for the default all-users install and the action only ever touched HKCU, so it printed "Already absent" while the menu stayed; it now probes the three HKLM verb keys read-only and says they remain and that uninstalling removes them. The unsigned uninstaller and the missing PATH task are not changed: signing needs a `SignTool` definition on the ISCC command line from `package-windows.ps1` and a fail-closed path, and PATH is a design choice (`ChangesEnvironment`, and the log's 2026-04-28 rule that a child shell must see the app directory first). Harness traps: `ISCC.exe` takes `/DName=value` switches and a script path, so under Git Bash set `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1` and pass the script as a Windows path, or it reports "You may not specify more than one script filename" or "Unknown option"; `zig fmt src dist` rewrites the generated `src/build/uucode_tables.zig`, so format the files you touched, not the tree; and this agent's Bash tool turned a doubled backslash in a heredoc into a single one (a Python `\\a` became BEL in a log entry), so write scripts with the file tool.
- 2026-10-15: A program's OSC 52 clipboard request (a read under `clipboard-read = ask`, a write under `clipboard-write = ask`) now raises its own prompt (`ConfirmPrompt.program_request`, `labels.buildProgramClipboardPromptBody`): "Let a program read/change your clipboard?" naming the tab by number and title, opening on Cancel, with Allow working only `program_confirm_arming_ms` (500) after the prompt opened AND after the last non-modifier key it received (`Host.holdProgramConfirm`), Enter in the preview pane never accepting it, and declined rather than shown while any overlay is open (a confirm, the palette, search, a rename) or when the terminal is in no tab (`Surface.declineProgramRequest`, checked before the clipboard is even read); closing the requester's tab into undo history cancels its prompt (`Host.cancelConfirmFromTab`) (PKG-25, F136). Before, a read used the user's own "Allow clipboard paste?" prompt with Allow focused, and Space or Enter, auto-repeat included, accepted at once, so a program could send the query right after a key it read and take the clipboard with the user's next key. For every confirm, Space no longer accepts and a repeated key presses nothing (`confirmButtonKey`); the user's own paste prompt keeps Allow focused, is not armed, and stays one Enter. `hideOverlay` now answers any confirm it closes as Cancel, after the overlay is down (F058): a new confirm, the palette, search or a rename opening over one used to drop it without its `on_cancel`, which left its clipboard op parked on its Surface. Allow and `cancelConfirmIfOwnedBy` clear `on_cancel` first, and since hideOverlay's callers go on using the Host, an `on_cancel` must never destroy it. Measured A/B on Debug portable copies on a hidden WinSta0 desktop with an OSC 52 write of a fake payload from cmd's `prompt $E]52;c;...$E\`: main showed "Allow clipboard write?" with focus on Allow and the preview as a Pane named after its contents; the fix showed the new wording with focus on Cancel and the preview as a read-only Edit "Preview" labelled by the title (F168), and an Enter posted to the focused Cancel declined it; `GetClipboardSequenceNumber` was the same before and after. Five traps. (1) The BUTTON class clicks on Space's RELEASE, so taking Space away from Allow means swallowing its keydown: falling through to the button would still accept. (2) Every route to Allow (keys, the BN_CLICKED of a click, UIA Invoke, Enter in the preview pane through `submitOverlay`) ends in `invokeConfirmAccept`, which is where the arming check lives; a check in the key handler alone would leave the click. (3) A delay counted only from when the prompt opened is not enough (independent review): Cancel has focus, but Tab or Right moves it to the preview pane, Left or Shift+Tab to Allow, and the fresh Enter of someone finishing `cd pro<Tab>...<Enter>` a second later would accept. (4) A program request must be declined BEFORE the requester clears its own `pending_clipboard_op`: that op may be what the open prompt delivers. (5) The OSC 52 read path cannot be driven live here without touching the maintainer's clipboard: the clipboard belongs to the window station, and `CreateWindowStationW` fails with `ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED` for this account, so a private one is unavailable; the read prompt is unit-tested only (the inert `TestSession` core must not be reached, so tests open and cancel prompts but never accept a clipboard op, and pin `armed_at_ms` rather than trust the clock). Also from F056: `OpenClipboard` is retried 10 times with a 5 ms sleep between (`openClipboard`; each sleep rounds up to the ~15.6 ms timer tick, so a clipboard held throughout costs up to about 140 ms, not 50), a lone surrogate on the clipboard or in a drop becomes U+FFFD (std's WTF-16 decode plus its in-place lossy pass, `clipboardTextToUtf8`), and CF_UNICODETEXT is written with CRLF (`crlfLineEndings`; CF_HTML is untouched, its header holds byte offsets). F053: dropped text and HTML get CRLF to LF in `formatTextPayload`, as clipboard pastes already did; file paths are untouched.
- 2026-10-15: `close_tab:this` with `undo-timeout = 0` (CLOSE-01; supersedes "`close_tab:this` still never asks" in the PKG-18 entry). Undo off means the detached tab is pruned by the next prune timer (`oldest + undo-timeout + 1` ms), so the close ended running programs with nothing to bring them back and no prompt. `App.closeTab(.this)` now counts the tab's panes with `tabsCloseConfirmCount` when `undoTimeoutMs() == 0` and asks through `askBeforeClosing(.this_tab, ...)`, userdata the Surface (its destruction cancels the prompt) and the same Close/Cancel rules as the other-tabs prompt; Close runs `closeTabUndoable`, which is the old `.this` body and asks nothing. With undo on, or nothing running, it closes at once as before. Its Close re-finds the tab through the Surface, so `commitTabDropSplit` (a tab dragged into a split of the front tab) cancels a prompt the dragged tab's terminals raised, as `detachTabForUndo` already does; otherwise Close would find the target tab through the same terminal and close all of it (found by review, from the code; the drag path is not covered by a test). `closeTab` returning true now also means "a prompt is open", so `Host.closeTabInPlace` moves focus to the strip only when a tab actually left; before that check, Delete on a focused background tab sent the keys away from the prompt it had just opened. Removing a tab raised no UIA structure event: `notifyActiveTabUiaSelectionChanged` stays silent when a background tab goes (no selection change), so a reader's tab list went stale. `Host.notifyTabRemovedUia` now raises `children_invalidated` on the host's root provider (`RootProvider.raiseChildrenInvalidated`; the tab buttons are child windows of the host, not children of the Selection container, which the buttons only name as their selection container) from `closeTabUndoable` once the close has committed (a close that rolled back raises nothing), from `windowDestroyed` when a whole tab leaves (a pane's last close, a background tab's shell exit), and from the close redo, the split-transfer redo and the drag of a tab into a split; adding or restoring a tab still raises none. Whether a screen reader re-reads its tab list on this event was not measured (no reader was run). Test traps: `UiaRaiseStructureChangedEvent` with a UIA client attached to the machine kept the test's provider alive past its `Release` (the first version of the test leaked it here; the holder is inferred, not measured), so under `builtin.is_test` `events.raiseStructureChanged` only counts (`testing_structure_changes`) and never calls UIA, whatever is listening; and a fixture surface's `core_initialized` must be false whenever `activateSurface` can run for it, so the tests set it true only around the call that reads `needsConfirmQuit`, as the other-tabs test does.
- 2026-10-15: A test that runs key messages through `win32/input.zig` must not trust the thread's keyboard state (FLAKY-02). A run with the keyboard in use saw `expected NumLock bit 0, found 32` and a stray LEFT_CTRL in the real-layout tests: the readers reported a state other than the one the test had set just before. Why was NOT found, and it could not be made to happen on its own: a full suite with NumLock on passed (4948/5022, 74 skipped), the test process was never the foreground window during a filtered `win32` run, and a thread that sets zeros kept them across `ActivateKeyboardLayout` and `SetFocus` (probed from a PowerShell thread). So the dependency is shown by injection, not by a repro of the cause: changing the thread's table between `TranslateMessage` and the dispatch (NumLock on) makes the real-layout test fail with exactly `...;32;1_` where `...;0;1_` is expected. `TestingKeyboard` (input.zig) now pins all 256 states for the calling thread, toggles included: `currentKeyboardState`, `keyPressed` and `keyToggled` answer from the pin under `builtin.is_test`, and the table is set on the thread as well for what Windows reads itself. `testingTypeKeys` pins it, `testingDeliverKeyMessage` perturbs the live table after translation on purpose (the three real-layout tests fail without the pin), and the unshifted-codepoint and `keyMessage` tests pin an empty keyboard (their assertions did not depend on it, measured by disabling the pin: they still passed). The settings Ctrl+S test owns the whole table through `holdKeys` (PKG-21), which saw the same thing from the other side: a new thread's key table starts as a copy of the real keyboard's. Not covered: `TranslateMessage` reads the thread's real table, which the test sets right before it, so only a change between that set and the translate could alter the characters a layout test types (never seen); a test that must exclude even that has to run its thread on a desktop of its own, as the settings dialog tests do.
- 2026-10-14: Typing latency after #335 (LAT-01, reported on #299). termio's `drainMailbox` woke the renderer after every mailbox message, pty writes included (upstream's "if we have a message we always redraw"), and on Win32 every renderer wake is a full `updateFrame` plus a `SwapBuffers` on the UI thread. Since Win32 input mode every key release and modifier press writes a record, so each release presented an unchanged frame. Measured over 240 keys (`lat-echo` echoing 55 ms late, the existing render trace): presents 747 on v1.3.131, 747 on 4b3c9b4, 993 on main and 732 with the fix; pty writes 242, 242, 482 and 482. The extra wake carries no `WakeSource`, so the render trace counted it as `unattributed_wakeup_count` (10, 16, 264, 2): a jump in that counter is the signature of this class. A write now asks for no frame (`messageNeedsRedraw`). The callers that change the screen before writing must queue their own render: typing, the text/csi/esc bindings and paste already did, through `scrollToBottom` or `queueRender`, and three paths that dropped the selection and then only wrote (alternate scroll, reported wheel ticks, reported clicks) relied on the drain wake and now call `clearSelectionForChildInput` (independent review caught these). Four traps. (1) On a hidden desktop `SwapBuffers` returns in about 0.03 ms on the 240 Hz and the 60 Hz monitor alike: nothing composes it, so an extra present costs nothing there, and keystroke-to-present latency was the same on v1.3.131, 4b3c9b4 and main (local echo 1.2-1.6 ms median, Debug builds too, bundled and in-box ConPTY). The regression appears only once a present can wait for vblank. With a measurement-only emulation (one frame queued per 60 Hz vblank; a second present in the same refresh blocks until it and shows a refresh later), main's display latency for echoes 0-70 ms late (typed steadily or in bursts) was 1.1-6.8 ms above v1.3.131's at the median and 4.7-8.7 ms at p95, and the fix returned it to v1.3.131's. That is a model, not a measured display. (2) A child started with a null `lpDesktop` by a driver running on a private desktop landed on the Default desktop (probed with a helper that prints `GetThreadDesktop`); always pass `WinSta0\<desktop>` to every child, as `hidden-win11-console-key-records.ps1` does. Two of this task's first smoke runs showed noctty on the interactive desktop before this was measured. (3) `STARTF_USESHOWWINDOW` with `SW_HIDE` hides a GUI target's first `ShowWindow`, and its renderer then never presents: a latency driver must not pass it for noctty. (4) Posting only WM_KEYDOWN/WM_KEYUP (no WM_CHAR) types each character exactly once, because the target's own `TranslateMessage` produces it, on v1.3.131 and on main. Still open, older than v1.3.131 (RESOLVED by the 2026-10-15 LAT-02 entry): every typed key presents a frame before its echo (`keyWriteIsTyping` queues a render even when the viewport did not move and no selection was dropped), and a coalesced wake presents a second, identical frame through the repaint retry, about one per keystroke, after the press frame or after the echo frame. Under the same emulation the first one costs local echo about one refresh.
- 2026-10-14: ReleaseFast's default `-Dstrip` also dropped the exe's unwind tables, and on x64 a debugger takes a function without one for a leaf, so a stack in a release minidump went wrong at the first noctty frame (#299's reporter found noctty's stacks not walkable). `src/build/GhosttyExe.zig` now keeps them whatever the strip setting, +132,096 bytes (0.5%) on noctty.exe. No PDB ships, so frames are module offsets, and cdb labels them with the nearest export (`noctty!onig_unicode_define_user_property+0x...`), which is not the function: read those as offsets. Reading a dump offline: `cdb -z <dump> -y <dir> -i <dir>` with `<dir>` holding the exact released noctty.exe (the unwind data comes from the file, not the dump), then `.reload /f noctty.exe`, `.ecxr` and `~*k`. Measured on a stripped build against this one: an idle dump's main thread went `GetMessageW -> noctty+0x24707a -> 0x113 ...` stripped and reached `RtlUserThreadStart` with the tables, and in noctty's own crash dump (remote thread at address 0, as in the 2026-10-09 entry; both builds still write it) 18 of 22 threads walked to their thread start stripped, 22 of 22 with the tables. Symbols were left out: with debug info on, noctty.exe grows about 1 MB even with frame pointers omitted (`.text` +0xB0200), and the PDB is 56.7 MB (14.5 MB deflated).
- 2026-10-15: Three default-terminal handoff fixes (PKG-34), none measured through a real handoff: that needs the machine's default terminal changed, which a worker must never do, so they rest on unit tests and on reading the core. (1) `createAdoptedWindow` set `config.title` to the startup title. `config.title` is the static-title setting: `Surface.handleMessage(.set_title)` returns early while it is set, so every title the console sets afterwards (cmd `title`, a prompt, a TUI) is dropped, and `Surface.zig` re-applies it on every config update. It now leaves the config alone and calls `Surface.setTitle(pending.title)` right after the surface exists; the surface's mailbox is only drained by a later `App.tick`, so that cannot overwrite a title the console already set (inferred from the code, not traced); a `title` the user configured is static, already applied by the core, and still wins. The window is shown inside `Surface.init`, before that call, so its first caption can read `noctty` for a moment. The window text passed to `createHost` does not survive: `syncWindowTitle` rebuilds the caption from `effectiveTitle`, which is null until a title is set. `cloneStartupTitle` used the strict UTF-16 conversion, so one unpaired surrogate in a client-chosen title failed the whole handoff; it now goes through `std.unicode.fmtUtf16Le` (U+FFFD). (2) `TERMINAL_STARTUP_INFO.wShowWindow` and `dwFlags` were never read. `PendingSession` now carries both by value (the struct is COM-owned and gone when `EstablishPtyHandoff` returns), and `adoptedInitialShow` maps them to `InitialShow`, which replaces `SurfaceInitOptions.passive_show`: `wShowWindow` counts only with `STARTF_USESHOWWINDOW` (the field is garbage otherwise), `SW_SHOWNOACTIVATE`/`SW_SHOWNA` show without activating, the minimized variants and `SW_HIDE` show `SW_SHOWMINNOACTIVE`, `SW_MAXIMIZE` maximizes and takes focus. `SW_HIDE` is deliberately not a hidden host: conhost delegates those consoles too, and a hidden host nobody can bring back is worse than a minimized one. A minimized host reaches `refreshSurfaceVisibility` through `WM_SHOWWINDOW`/`WM_WINDOWPOSCHANGED`, so its surfaces are occluded as for any minimized window. Position, size and the count-chars/fill-attribute fields are still ignored. (3) Unregister left empty `Interface\{IID}\ProxyStubClsid32` keys. `RegDeleteKeyW` deletes a key together with its values, so "only when empty" is a `RegQueryInfoKeyW` check (no subkeys, no values) before the delete, and it is not atomic. Registration now records a `KeysCreated` mask beside `Present` (which of the proxy key and its IID key did not exist), unregister removes only those and only once empty, and state saved by an earlier build has no mask and keeps its keys. Cleanup failures are logged, not fatal, so they cannot trigger the unregister rollback. The registry code resolves every key under `test_registry_prefix` in tests, so the round trips run in a scratch `HKCU\Software\noctty-test-handoff-*` subtree that the test deletes (with an assert on the prefix before the `RegDeleteTreeW`) and never in the real classes; a test build panics on any registry call made without that prefix. The real `Console\%%Startup` values and `startup-attempts.json` were unchanged afterwards.
- 2026-10-14: Git Bash discovery and its bash integration (PKG-17, F095, G021, F104, G029, F139, G026). (1) `lookupGitBash` now tries the install `git.exe` belongs to (`<root>\bin\bash.exe`, the root being the parent of the `cmd` or `bin` that holds `git.exe`, or the grandparent for a MinGW environment's `bin`; any other folder, a scoop shim for one, is not recognised), then the Git installer's `HKCU` / `HKLM` `SOFTWARE\GitForWindows` `InstallPath`, and only then a PATH `bash.exe` that is not under `%SystemRoot%` or `WindowsApps`; there is no later PATH entry to fall through to, because `os.path.expand` returns the first hit, so a machine with only WSL's launcher gets no Git Bash profile. `setup()` also refuses bash injection for those launchers (a bare `bash` is resolved through the child's PATH first), since `--posix` and the `ENV`/`GHOSTTY_BASH_INJECT` variables do not cross into WSL. Measured with the Machine+User PATH (the one Explorer hands the app): the first PATH `bash.exe` is `C:\WINDOWS\system32\bash.exe`, the new lookup gives `C:\Program Files\Git\bin\bash.exe`. Trap: `scripts\dev-windows.cmd` puts `Git\usr\bin` first on PATH, so no test run through it can show the bug; run the compiled `ghostty-test.exe` directly with the registry PATH. The registry tier is stubbed under `builtin.is_test` (a machine with Git would otherwise add a Git Bash profile to tests that expect none) and covered by a scratch-key test of `registryString`. (2) Git Bash reports `OSTYPE=cygwin`, not `msys` (MSYS2's own bash says `msys`), so `ghostty.bash` tests for both. Under either: the `path` block is skipped (MSYS splits the Windows `GHOSTTY_BIN_DIR` at its colon into a relative `C` entry and a stray tail, and Exec already put the directory on the PATH the shell inherits); OSC 7 is `/C:/Users/me` (the one shape `isWindowsUriPath` reads as a Windows directory; `/c/Users/me` is a WSL path) from a pure-bash rewrite of `/c/...` and `/cygdrive/c/...`, with `cygpath -m` only when the directory changes to one off a drive mount, and nothing is sent when that is not a drive path (a UNC path would be refused anyway); and a Windows-form `PWD` from `working-directory` is replaced by a logical `cd` to its `/c/...` form before any startup file runs, with OLDPWD put back as it was (`cd -P .` was the first try and resolves a junction or symlink in `working-directory`, so the harness starts in a junction; in bash `builtin declare -a x=()` is a syntax error, assign the array on its own line) (MSYS bash keeps the inherited string, so the first prompt showed `C:\Users/me` and the first OSC 7 glued the host to the path). (3) `D;0` for every command was `__ghostty_precmd` ignoring `$1` (upstream 4dfa44ecd), not hook order; a scalar `PROMPT_COMMAND` also clobbers `$?` before the hook (591ccacbd), and `declare -ax` arrays (ed7f046ee) and re-sourcing with an array (a4031c8e3) needed their own fixes. The four were applied from `gh api repos/ghostty-org/ghostty/commits/<sha> -H 'Accept: application/vnd.github.patch'`: `git fetch` of an upstream commit by abbreviated SHA fails, and a depth-1 fetch makes every commit a root. `test/windows/bash-shell-integration.ps1` (a CI step in `test.yml`) drives Git Bash over a pipe with a scratch HOME; each of its assertions fails against a copy of the script with just that fix reverted. Harness traps: a PowerShell parameter named `$Input` is the automatic pipeline variable; a hosted runner's TEMP can be an 8.3 name (`RUNNER~1`; inferred, not measured here), so compare a start directory by its tail; and a `-Dtest-filter` run that adds a test constant named like an existing local (`git_bash`) fails the whole build with `shadows declaration`.
- 2026-10-14: The NVIDIA crash on every monitor unplug and Win+P (#299, R580 32.0.16.1742, inside `nvoglv64!DrvPresentBuffers`) followed noctty's choice of a half-float window format, and both causes are in `src/apprt/win32/pixel_format.zig`; measured with a table dump on an RTX 5070 Ti with the same driver. (1) NVIDIA's only accumulation-free window formats are RGBA16F (`WGL_TYPE_RGBA_FLOAT_ARB`, 16 bits a channel), and every RGBA8 window format carries a 64-bit accumulation buffer. Both rankers put accumulation-free first, validation only asked for `cColorBits >= 32`, and a descriptor has no float type (`DescribePixelFormat` reports these as `PFD_TYPE_RGBA` with 64 colour and 16 alpha bits), so the classic enumerator picked one: index 91 here, 123 on the reporter's laptop. Every path now requires exactly 32 colour and 8 alpha bits, and a deep format is refused rather than ranked lower. The extended path already rejected a non-RGBA WGL pixel type inside an inline boolean; that check now belongs to the candidate's classification (`pixel_type`, deliberately without a default so the wiring from the attribute query cannot go missing silently), where a test pins it. (2) The extended inventory bound was 1024 and the reporter's laptop exposes 1172 formats, so the sRGB selector failed on every surface and the classic enumerator ran. The same driver exposes 670 on this desktop, so a local run passes either way: the count differs between machines on one driver version (these two also differ in GPU and display setup). Both inventories are now bounded at 4096, and extended candidates are ranked as they stream (16 KiB of stack, against 24 KiB at the old bound or 112 KiB buffered at the new one). To exercise the classic path on hardware whose table fits, lower the bound below the measured count in a scratch build: main then logs the reporter's two warnings and selects index 91 (64/16, accumulation 0), the fix index 14 (32/8). Trap: `selected WGL pixel format` is a `log.debug` line, so a ReleaseFast build never prints it; read the selection from the benchmark trace (`NOCTTY_BENCH_MEMORY_STAGE_TRACE_FILE`: `wgl_pixel_format_index`, `wgl_selection_source`, `wgl_total_format_count`). The unplug itself was not reproduced here.
- 2026-10-11: PKG-32, the Windows default keymap (G055, G013, G056, G061; decision D5). (1) Shift+Insert did nothing: `Keybinds.init` bound it to `paste_from_clipboard` and, a block later, unconditionally to `paste_from_selection` for every non-macOS target; a trigger maps to one action, so the later `put` replaced it, and Win32 has no selection clipboard, so the key was swallowed. The second block is skipped on Windows. (2) The focus-region cycle moved from F6 / Shift+F6 to Ctrl+F6 / Ctrl+Shift+F6, so a bare F6 reaches htop (nice), Midnight Commander (move) and vim. The chrome controls never reach the keybind table, so their fixed handler (`Host.handleFocusRegionKey`, shared by the tab strip, the host banner, the docked search controls and the overlay) answers the same chords through `focus_region.chordDirection`; a bare F6 in the chrome now does nothing. (3) Fullscreen is F11 and Ctrl+Enter is unbound on Windows (since PKG-72 conhost tells Ctrl+Enter from Enter, so a program can use it); Alt+Enter was never bound and stays free. (4) Windows defaults for undo, redo and `navigate_search`, all performable: Ctrl+Shift+Z undo, Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Z redo, F3 and Shift+F3 step through the search the way Enter and Shift+Enter do in the docked bar (a test in `labels.zig` ties them to `dockedSearchEnterDirection`). The audit's redo, Ctrl+Shift+Y, is NOT used: it is `copy_last_command_output` on Windows (windows.md), so redo takes undo's letter with Alt added. Unmeasured: Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Z on a layout whose AltGr produces a letter there, where the chord may type instead of redoing. (5) A performable binding only lets the key through if the action returns false, and Win32's `.undo` / `.redo` returned true after showing "Nothing to undo." for every surface-targeted request, which a key press and the command palette both are. A surface-targeted request with nothing to do now returns false and shows nothing; an app-level request (a host with no surface, a global binding) keeps its banner and true; and `invokePaletteRow` shows the banner itself when undo or redo did nothing, since nothing was freed. (6) The "Tab closed" banner names the key (`Tab closed. Press Ctrl+Shift+Z to undo.`), found by scanning `Set.bindings` in `labels.tabClosedMessage`: performable triggers are not in `Set.reverse` (Binding.zig), so `reverse.get(.undo)` finds nothing, and for the same reason the palette rows for undo, redo and `navigate_search` show no shortcut. With undo unbound the banner keeps the generic text. (7) `noctty +list-keybinds --default` prints no flags (no `performable:`), so the golden file `src/cli/testdata/default_keybinds_windows.txt` cannot pin them; `default Windows keybinds` asserts the action and the flag of every changed trigger and that the freed chords stay unbound, and the golden pins the rest. Regenerate the golden from the failure output or from `noctty +list-keybinds --default --plain` after reading every changed line. Measured: with main's Config.zig and the new table test the test fails at the first case (`shift+insert` found `paste_from_selection`) and so does the golden; with the surface-targeted early returns removed the new undo test fails. The accessibility matrix's measured rows keep the chords they were measured with and say so; `interactive-win11-accessibility.ps1` now sends Ctrl+F6 / Ctrl+Shift+F6 but was not run (it needs the foreground).
- 2026-10-14: PKG-32 Windows defaults (G055, G013, G056, G061; D5): keep Shift+Insert on paste_from_clipboard by skipping the later selection-clipboard put on Windows; move focus cycling in both the pane and native chrome to Ctrl+F6 / Ctrl+Shift+F6; use F11 fullscreen and leave Ctrl+Enter / Alt+Enter free. Undo / redo are performable Ctrl+Shift+Z / Ctrl+Shift+Y; copy_last_command_output remains in the palette with no default chord (maintainer confirmed the collision resolution). Surface-targeted undo/redo with no history must return false silently so the key reaches the program; the palette supplies its own no-history banner. Performable bindings are absent from Set.reverse, so the last-tab hint scans the live direct leaf bindings and falls back when undo is unbound, modifier remaps exist, or physical/translated overrides make the chord ambiguous. Unicode shortcut hints require the exact US HKL (0x04090409), not just LANGID 0x0409; Turkish Q can produce Unicode i from physical quote and shifted İ, so an ASCII key guess is insufficient. F3 / Shift+F3 must map consistently through the pane defaults, docked F3 handlers, buttons and Enter: core search is newest-first, so visible down/next is core .previous. An empty host has no surface and routes through CoreApp.keyEvent with target .app; preserve its host ID during that call so undo/redo cannot select another window, and resolve the ID only after pruning because pruning may destroy the host. The default-table test pins flags as well as actions; the CLI snapshot alone cannot prove performable fallthrough. The updated interactive accessibility harness requires foreground input and is not safe for this campaign; use private-desktop probes with copied portable binaries and isolated AppData.
- 2026-10-11: Focus after a close follows the shell model (PKG-28, F037, F038, F039, F077, and X-gpt-arch-09 from the audit addendum; decision D7). `windowDestroyed` used to give the first leaf of the remaining split tree the focus whichever pane closed, kept the active tab's index rather than the tab when a tab before it closed, and always called `activateSurface`, which restores (`SW_RESTORE`) and raises a minimized or background window. Now native keeps the focused pane by identity (the handles are renumbered, so `findHandle` again) and the active tab by `activeTabAfterRemoval`, and after the shell close commits, `followShellFocus` adopts the model's choice by stable ID, which picks the first pane of the closed pane's sibling, as `reducer.closePane` does, when the focused pane itself closed. Teardown activates with focus only when the closed pane was the window's focused one and the window is in front (`tabSeenByUser`), otherwise `activateSurfaceNoFocus`. `detachTabForUndo` and `redoClosedTabEntry` had the same index bug (closing any tab moved the selection to the closed tab's place) and use the same helper, so a tab's X, middle click or Delete now closes it in place (`Host.closeTabInPlace`) without selecting it first; the right-click menu still selects the tab it opens on, because Rename and Move act on the active tab. `goto_split` out of a zoomed pane unzooms, or with `split-preserve-zoom = navigation` zooms the pane it moves to, lays out before focusing and drops the redo branch like `toggle_split_zoom`. Measured on Debug portable copies on a hidden desktop, typing `exit` into one pane with posted key messages: on the PKG-18 head a background tab's exit restored the minimized window and a background pane's exit moved focus from the bottom-right pane to the left one; `goto_split:left` out of a zoomed right pane focused the hidden left pane; middle-clicking the first of three tabs made the second active and logged `shell/native invariant failed context=focus err=TabCountMismatch`. With the fix each kept the state the user had, the focused pane's exit gave focus to its sibling, and `goto_split` showed both panes. Two traps. (1) DestroyWindow sends WM_KILLFOCUS to a child that still has the keyboard focus AFTER its WM_DESTROY, and `Surface.destroy` freed the Surface inside WM_DESTROY without clearing `GWLP_USERDATA`, so `windowProc` ran `deferred_char.clear()` on freed memory (0xC0000005, cdb: `clear` <- `windowProc` <- `NtUserDestroyWindow` <- `requestClose`). It was masked because teardown always moved focus elsewhere during WM_DESTROY; once a background close stopped doing that, the focused pane's exit crashed. `destroy` now clears the window data before freeing. (2) Posting key messages types every character twice: the loop's `TranslateMessage` makes a WM_CHAR from the posted WM_KEYDOWN, and the posted WM_CHAR follows. Send the triple with SendMessage (as `interactive-win11-shader-pacing.ps1` does), or post only key downs for keys without text (F-keys). On a hidden desktop noctty is never the foreground window, so the focus-moving branch was never measured end to end; a unit test pins only the predicate that chooses it (`tabSeenWithForeground`), because `windowDestroyed` activates nothing while `App.running` is false, as in `TestSession`. Two more from review: activation that shows no window (`activateSurfaceNoFocus`) must leave `windows_hidden` alone, or a pane exit while `toggle_visibility` hides the windows makes the next toggle a no-op; and closing a background tab leaves the active tab as it was, which `activateSurface`'s fast path does not lay out, so `close_tab:this` lays out the tab strip itself then.
- 2026-10-11: Scrollback search with many matches (PKG-36, G051 and G059). (1) `PageList.pointFromPin(.screen, pin)` walks the page list from the first node on every call. The search thread's scrollbar match rows called it once per match, under the terminal lock, on every refresh after any output (`reloadActive` always bumps `match_rows_revision`, which also covers rows that shift when the oldest page is pruned) and after every page fed during the initial scan: O(matches x pages). `ScreenSearch.collectMatchRows` records every node's first screen row in one walk (`NodeRows`) and gives exactly what `pointFromPin` gives, including no row for a node no longer in the list. Two tests compare it with `pointFromPin` across pages, pruning and a cleared scrollback; they pass on the old code too (they pin equivalence, the bench is the evidence for the cost), and fail with a row off by one or a missing node given a row. Measured with `zig build bench:search-stream` (ReleaseFast, 100k lines that all match, 140x45, 200 MiB scrollback, 118k matches over 358 pages; medians): the initial search 3,294 -> 265 ms, one refresh after 32 KiB of output 27.7 -> 3.8 ms, and with 8 MiB written at 2 MiB/s under the lock the writer waited for it 2,668 -> 565 ms of 4 s. What a refresh still costs is mostly upstream code that is O(matches) too: `reloadActive` copies every history result to prepend new ones, and `pruneHistory` reads every result's serial. (2) `PageList.grow` reuses a pruned standard-size first page as the NEWEST page (same node pointer, new serial), so until `pruneHistory` runs a stale history result maps to a row near the bottom, in `pointFromPin` and `NodeRows` alike; a pruned page stays in the list, and only a destroyed one (a cleared scrollback, a larger-than-standard page) leaves it. Rows of matches on destroyed pages can coincide with remaining rows, so a test of the missing-node case must check pins one by one, not the deduplicated row set (a first draft passed with missing nodes given row 0). (3) `Thread.Search.init` creates no screen searchers, so until the first `feed` `isComplete()` is vacuously true: a driver loop that does not feed first measures nothing (the first bench draft reported a 0.0 ms initial search). (4) The search thread sends its events to the shared 64-slot app mailbox, which only the app thread drains, and the app thread joins the search thread in `Surface.Search.deinit` (end search, clear the query, close the pane). With the mailbox full, both waited forever: the test "ending a search does not wait for room in a full app mailbox" fills it but for one slot, and on the old code the join was still waiting after 10 s. `Search.deinit` now sets `search.stopping` before the stop notify (the same shape as PKG-02's `io.stopping`): the callback then sends nothing more, `pushSearchMessage` drops and frees a message it cannot deliver, and a push already waiting for room gives up (`.forever_unless`). The test leaves one slot so the push that waits is the viewport matches, whose arena the testing allocator checks is freed. Nothing is lost: `endSearch` and `setSearchQuery("")` clear the search state themselves under a newer generation, which rejects the ended search's messages (the dropped quit-time `search_clear` duplicated that clear), and `Surface.deinit` has already left the app; a search thread that exits on its own error still waits for room, because nothing is joining it. Not covered: `Thread.send` pushes the app thread's own messages into the search thread's 64-slot mailbox with `.forever`, so a full search mailbox while that thread waits for room in a full app mailbox would still hang. That needs 64 queued query, focus or visibility messages from an app thread that is not draining its own mailbox; not measured.
- 2026-10-11: EOL-01. A `core.autocrlf=true` checkout (Git for Windows' default and this machine's system gitconfig) turns every text file `.gitattributes` does not pin into CRLF, and the release job's Windows runner evidently checks out the same way (inferred from its package, below). Measured on the v1.3.131 portable ZIP (hash checked against `SHA256SUMS.txt`): `noctty.exe` embeds `src/config/config-template` with CRLF (one copy, none with a bare LF), `config-template.ghostty` beside it has 52 CRLF lines, and the shell-integration scripts for bash, zsh, fish, nushell, elvish and Clink are all CRLF (`ghostty.bash` 335 of 335 lines), while `integration.ps1`, pinned by `*.ps1`, and `bash-preexec.sh`, pinned by `*.sh`, are LF. The config loader trims a trailing CR (`cli/args.zig` `LineIterator`), so the CRLF template was harmless. For the scripts the harm is measured only for a strict bash: WSL's `bash -n` rejects the shipped `ghostty.bash` with a syntax error at a `do` that carries the CR, but noctty never injects into WSL or ssh (`shell_integration.zig` returns null for them), and Git Bash's own bash, the one it does inject into, accepts the same file and a CRLF probe (`bash -n` exit 0), so a check from Git Bash proves nothing. zsh, fish, elvish, nushell and Clink were not measured. `.gitattributes` now pins `src/config/config-template`, `src/config/testdata/*`, `src/shell-integration/**` and `src/font/res/*.bdf` (`text eol=lf`); unit tests fail when the embedded template or a shipped shell-integration script holds a CR; and a flagship contract pins the four rules. NOT reproduced: the lead's trial-merge failure of "default config files: an edit to the edit-target takes effect on reload" (expected 77, found 12), which was put down to a fresh autocrlf=true clone. On main 6d42d0bd1 that test passes in a fresh autocrlf=true clone, alone (70/71 passed against a 69/70 no-match baseline) and in the full suite (4864/4938, 74 skipped, no failure), and an independent review got the same alone-run, so a CRLF template is not enough to make it fail on that code; the trial merge's code was different and the cause of its failure is unexplained. 12 is the `font-size` default, so the reload never applied the appended line. Unmeasured hypothesis: `loadOptionalFile` keeps the defaults for any error but FileNotFound, so a transient sharing violation on the just-written file (antivirus, the indexer, under `.zig-cache\tmp`) would give exactly that; the trial log's `error reading optional config file, not loading err=` lines (anything but `FileIsEmpty`) would tell. Three traps. (1) `git ls-files --eol` shows `i/lf w/crlf attr/` for exactly the files a CRLF checkout converts and no attribute pins; run it in a fresh `-c core.autocrlf=true` clone to list them. (2) Pulling a pin does not fix an existing autocrlf=true checkout: git sees no blob change, so the working files stay CRLF (`git status` clean, `w/crlf attr/text eol=lf`) and a local build still embeds CRLF, which the new tests catch. Delete the files and `git checkout -- <path>`, or, with nothing uncommitted, `git rm -r --cached -q . && git reset -q --hard`; fresh CI and release checkouts are not affected. (3) A filtered test run needs a token from the fully qualified test name, which has spaces; a hyphenated guess matched nothing and reported the 70-test no-match baseline.
- 2026-10-11: Closing a window (WM_CLOSE: the caption X, Alt+F4, the taskbar; `close_window`), `quit`, `close_all_windows` and `close_tab:other`/`right` now ask once when any terminal they would end needs `confirm-close-surface`'s confirmation (`Surface.needsCloseConfirm`, core's `needsConfirmQuit`), on one host overlay (`Host.askBeforeClosing`, `closeScopePrompt`) whose accept runs the unconfirmed path (`Host.closeConfirmed`, `App.quitConfirmed`, `App.closeTabsBeside`) (PKG-18, F036, F092). `close_tab:this` still never asks, because it is undoable, and neither does a sign-out (`session_ending`). The window comes up first when it is minimized or not in front (`presentForPrompt`), a repeated request keeps the prompt that is open, a prompt that cannot open closes nothing, the last-host session save runs only on accept, and quit with unsaved Settings edits raises the Settings close prompt first and resumes the quit after Save or Discard (`holdQuitForUnsavedChanges`, `takeQuitAfterClose`; Keep editing or a failed save drops it). Measured on Debug portable copies on a hidden desktop with `ping -t localhost` in the terminal, A/B against main: main ended the process at once for SC_CLOSE (also minimized), `close_window`, `quit`, `close_all_windows` and `close_tab:other`; the fix showed the prompt (restoring the minimized window), Cancel kept everything, Accept closed with exit 0, and a window whose cmd sat at its prompt still closed at once. Four traps. (1) Core's `closingAction` names only `close_surface`, `close_window` and `close_tab`: after `quit` or `close_all_windows` returns, `maybeHandleBinding` goes on using the terminal whose key asked for it. The Win32 apprt destroyed every window inside that call, so a quit keybinding freed its own terminal: 0xC0000005 in `endKeySequence` with a minidump on main, idle shell or not. Both now post `WM_WINHOSTTY_QUIT` to a host and run from the message loop (`App.postQuit`). A failed post declines the quit: falling back to running it in place is the same crash (Greptile and CodeRabbit on #343). Any new app-scoped action that can destroy the calling terminal must do the same or be a core closing action. (2) `close_tab:other`/`right` walked each tab's split tree while closing its panes, and each close replaces and frees that tree, so the next iterator step read freed arena memory (inferred from the code; Debug did not crash because the freed pages stayed readable). Stage the panes first, as `Host.closeConfirmed` does. (3) `Host.deinit` never freed an open confirm payload; a window-close prompt whose last terminal exited leaked its strings. (4) Test seam: the inert `TestSession` core answers `needsConfirmQuit` from `readonly` before it reads the terminal, so `core_surface.readonly = true` plus `core_initialized = true` stands in for a busy terminal; clear `core_initialized` before anything else reads the core. Harness notes: a hidden-desktop driver that starts noctty with `-WindowStyle Hidden` sees every surface as invisible (`IsWindowVisible` checks ancestors) until it shows the host; the smoke harness passes `--confirm-close-surface=false` because its WM_CLOSE can beat the shell's first prompt. Not measured: real Alt+F4 and taskbar input (posted SC_CLOSE only) and a Restart Manager close.
- 2026-10-11: PKG-27, three cwd and notification reports that were silently dropped. (1) `os.hostname.isLocal` compared an OSC 7 host byte for byte with `GetComputerNameA`, which is the NetBIOS name: upper-case and cut at 15 characters. Git Bash (`$HOSTNAME`), WSL, fish and nushell send the DNS host name in its own case (the audit measured an all-caps NetBIOS name against the same name in mixed case from `$HOSTNAME` in Git Bash and `hostname` in WSL on the maintainer's machine, whose fully qualified name equals its DNS host name: no domain), so their reports were refused and new tabs and splits did not inherit the directory. Host names are case-insensitive, so `hostname.eql` compares case-insensitively and ignores one trailing dot, and on Windows `isLocal` accepts the DNS host, fully qualified and NetBIOS names from `GetComputerNameExW`. The whole name has to match, never a prefix or a label, because the check exists to keep an ssh session's cwd out; `127.0.0.1` and `::1` are NOT accepted (the audit's optional extra). The tests derive every name from `computerName`, so none names a real machine; a machine whose names are all short ASCII cannot tell the DNS lookups from the NetBIOS one (a NetBIOS-only `isLocal` passes the suite there), so the DNS-name test skips on a machine with no DNS name and `eql` pins that a 15-character NetBIOS name does not stand for a longer DNS name. (2) `OSC 9;9`, the form Windows Terminal documents and oh-my-posh's `osc99` and nushell send, carries a plain path (`C:\Users\me`, oh-my-posh quotes it), not a URL, and went through the same `file://` parse as OSC 7: `C:\...` read as scheme `C`, a quoted one failed to parse. The parser now marks the report (`Command.report_pwd.conemu`, `stream.ReportPwd.conemu`) and `StreamHandler.reportConemuPwd` takes a drive-absolute path (`X:\` or `X:/`, one pair of quotes stripped, a stray quote refused) or the `\\wsl.localhost\Distro\...` / `\\wsl$\...` path `wslpath -w` prints (the recipe Windows Terminal documents for WSL; `normalizeWslPath` maps it to the distro's own `/home/...`, as in an OSC 7 URL) through `windows_shell.conemuPathToLocal`, then `screenWindowsPwd`: the same `isSafeWindowsPath` and `os.path.isNetworkOrDevicePath` screening as an OSC 7 path (2026-10-06 entry). Any other UNC value, and every device path, is not taken at all: it falls through to the URL parse, which refuses it (the log then says "invalid url in OSC 7"). The screening itself therefore only ever stops a control byte, and the OSC parser drops or ends the sequence on most C0 bytes: only DEL (0x7F) reaches it, which is the case the test pins (with the screening removed, `ESC]9;9;C:\a<DEL>b` set the pwd). Anything that is not a path falls through to the URL parse, so noctty's own `kitty-shell-cwd://localhost/%P` form works as before, and OSC 7 never takes the plain-path branch (`OSC 7 ; C:\Windows` stays refused). There is no host check for 9;9, as in Windows Terminal, so a remote Windows session that prints a drive path is believed; OSC 7 never stopped a hostile remote either, since any remote can send `file://localhost/`. (3) `showDesktopNotification` cut the title (63 bytes) and body (255) at a byte, which splits a multi-byte code point; `buildToastXml` then fails with `InvalidUtf8` and the toast falls back to the banner. `StreamHandler.utf8Prefix` backs up over continuation bytes to the lead byte. A program that itself writes invalid UTF-8 still fails the toast; that is untouched. Measured: with the old behaviours put back (case-sensitive compare, no 9;9 branch, byte cut), nine of the new tests fail, and with `screenWindowsPwd` reduced to `return path` the 9;9 screening test and three older ones fail. Two test traps. A `.?` on `term.getPwd()` or a popped message panics, and a panic ends the whole test process, so the first failing test hid the other eight in the mutation run; `TestStream.expectPwd` and `expectSurfaceMessage` fail with an error instead. And `apprt.surface.Message.deinit()` takes no allocator where the app mailbox's `deinit(alloc)` does.
- 2026-10-11: Settings file safety (PKG-21: G004-G006, G008, F083), re-verified on d2a23196b before changing anything: G004's `-e` half had been fixed by #305, F083's wrong label survived #324, the rest held. (1) The save target scanner and the loader disagreed beyond `-e`, found by feeding the loader's own `loadIter` the same argv. `--config-file x` is NOT an option (the loader gets `ValueRequired` and an "invalid field" for `x`, and loads nothing), `--config-file=` clears the list, `--config-file=?x` and `="x y"` carry an optional marker and quotes that `Path.parse` strips, and a bare `--` ends nothing (`--config-file=b` after it is still read). `cliConfigFileOverrideArgs` now runs each value through `RepeatablePath.parseCLI`, stops at `-e` and at `-Embedding` like `Config.parseManuallyHook`, and a differential test asks the loader; this supersedes the separated-form statements in the 2026-04-17 `cliConfigFileOverride` entries. `win32_elevation.buildRelaunchParameters` still stops at `--` and reads the separated form; it was left alone. (2) A save writes where the configured name leads (`win32/save_target.zig`): `CreateFileW` with no access plus `GetFinalPathNameByHandleW`, stripping `\\?\` and `\\?\UNC\` (kept when the plain path would pass 230 characters, since `ReplaceFileW` and `MoveFileExW` take the path as written and no manifest here opts into long paths, and kept when a component ends in a dot or space or is a device name such as `nul` or `com1`, because Win32 normalization turns `config.` into `config`: stripped, a save through a link to `config.` replaced the sibling file; found by Greptile, tested on disk). A symlinked config keeps its link, a chain of links is followed, and a first save is the name itself. `nNumberOfLinks > 1` marks a hard-linked file, which is rewritten in place because a replacement would leave the other names on the old bytes. That rewrite opens without truncating (creating over a hidden or system file fails with access denied), is not atomic, and keeps the synced temp copy, with its path in the log, if it fails after the first byte. The open fails with not-found for both a first save and a dangling link, so `GetFileAttributesW` on the name (it does not follow links) tells them apart, and a dangling link is refused (`DanglingLink`, with its own status note) instead of replaced. Anything the final-path query cannot answer (some redirector and RAM-disk volumes, a name with a lone surrogate) leaves the name as it was, so a save that worked before still works (which also means a link on such a volume is still replaced). The hard-link rewrite writes before it truncates, so a disk-full failure in the middle of a longer text leaves new bytes followed by the old tail, with the complete copy kept and logged. Tests make links with `CreateSymbolicLinkW(.., SYMBOLIC_LINK_FLAG_ALLOW_UNPRIVILEGED_CREATE)` and skip on `ERROR_PRIVILEGE_NOT_HELD`; std's `Dir.symLink` was not tried (it creates the link through NT, which wants the privilege enabled). (3) Windows PowerShell 5.1 writes UTF-16LE with a BOM for `>` and Out-File. `Config.loadReader` parsed it as NUL-separated bytes (nothing set, nothing said) and a save appended UTF-8 lines to it. The loader now decodes a FF FE or FE FF file (a warning is logged; an odd trailing byte is dropped); a file that is not valid UTF-16 is skipped WITH A DIAGNOSTIC instead of returning an error, because `loadRecursiveFiles` does `try loadFsFile` and one bad included file would otherwise end the whole config load. A save refuses (`UnsupportedEncoding`, nothing written) any file that starts with a UTF-16 BOM or contains a NUL byte, which also catches UTF-32 and BOM-less UTF-16; ASCII-compatible text (UTF-8 with or without BOM, a legacy code page) is still patched line by line, byte for byte. (4) Same-key external edits: the window only learns of one through a reload (`externalConfigChanged`), so a save wrote over it. `AppHandle.reconcileDisk` runs first in `SettingsWindow.save`, loads the file as a reload does and compares EFFECTIVE values for the edited keys only: disk differs from `original` and from the draft, the rule `externalConfigChanged` itself applies, so a changed include counts and an edit to another key does not (at key granularity: the two notification toggles share `app-notifications`, so an edit to one applies the file early, the window then finds no conflict, and the save goes on). The file is compared with the app's conditional state applied, as `CoreApp.updateConfig` applies it to the config the window's snapshots are cloned from: on a dark system `theme = light:A,dark:B` picks the dark half, a theme can set editable keys, and compared raw an untouched file looked edited and every Save stopped (found in review; the masked check after a save still compares a raw reload and can have the same false positive). On a conflict it applies the file with `updateConfig`, and the window, not the check, decides whether anything is in conflict (`conflictCount`), so the two cannot disagree into a Save that never goes through. A file that cannot be read or applied stops the save with `ReloadFailed` instead of writing blind, and that includes a target that exists but will not open (held without read sharing, access denied, an offline share): `Config.load` logs such a default file and loads the defaults as if it were absent, which the check would have taken for an edit and applied to every terminal (found in review; `settingsFileOpenable`, tested with a file held with no sharing; std's `.lock = .exclusive` is a byte-range lock on Windows and does not stop an open). The same holds for every file in the loaded `config-file` list, checked before the conflict verdict (`settingsDiskVerdict`, `settingsIncludesOpenable`): a locked include is skipped with a diagnostic and everything it set is missing from the load, which would make a false conflict and, with no conflict, make the save's own reload apply the same partial config (found by Greptile and CodeRabbit). The theme file the config selects is held to the same rule (`settingsThemeOpenable`, the loader's own search over `themepkg.LocationIterator`, both the light and the dark name; it stops at the first directory that has the name, as the loader does, so a locked bundled copy shadowed by a user copy is not refused; a theme that is simply absent is not refused, since the running config lacks it too); Greptile found it. Limit: any open error other than not-found on a theme directory or include refuses every Save, also when the failure is permanent (an offline share), because the check cannot tell it from a lock. The ordinary reload still does that; only Save refuses. The check is a second full config load on the UI thread before every Save that edits something, on top of the reload after the write. The check must run BEFORE `transaction.dispatch(.apply)`: while an apply is pending `externalConfigChanged` returns at `ApplyAlreadyPending`, so a check inside `saveAndReload` would never show the conflict. The palette's theme save calls `settingsSaveAndReload` directly and does not check; its `original` is the live config, and the pick is the user's own act. A file hash taken at open was the first idea and was dropped: it needs refreshing at every reload and save, and it flags edits to other keys. (5) `App.loadConfigAtStartupCwd` holds the startup-cwd dance (2026-04-17 entries) for both Settings paths (the save and the disk check); the `.reload_config` handler still carries its own copy, which also runs `updateConfig` in the startup cwd, while here the cwd is restored right after the load, so `updateConfig` runs in the sanitized cwd. A relative `--config-file=custom.conf` still reloads the file that was saved (measured on a portable copy). (6) The Ctrl+S accelerator test (#320) failed 2 of 6 runs on a reviewer's machine. A hidden-desktop thread's key table starts as a copy of what the real keyboard holds (inferred; not measured), and the test copied it and set only Ctrl, so a held Shift or Alt (or Ctrl, for the "without Ctrl" half) failed it. It now replaces all 256 entries per case and reads Ctrl, Alt and Shift back through `GetKeyState`. Not reproduced here (old and new bodies passed 40 of 40 runs with no key held; injecting a real key would have gone to the maintainer's session); the old body does fail with only Shift set in the table. (7) The Terminal page label is "Scrollback limit (bytes; 0 = off; new terminals only)": the value is a byte budget, 0 disables scrollback and there is no unlimited setting. It stays within the narrowest lane (a test measures every label at 96, 144 and 192 DPI); "Scrollback limit" stays its first words, which is the accessible name the UIA harness looks for, so a screen reader still does not announce the unit. (8) Second review round (all four confirmed or measured). `std.fs.Dir.makePath` rejects the `\\?\` spelling with `BadPathName` (measured; a drive root too), and `save_target.plain` keeps that spelling on purpose, so `persistSettings` called `makePath` unconditionally and a config reached through it could never be saved; the parent is now created only when it does not open (tested with a `config.` file). The palette's theme pick (`commitPaletteTheme`) calls `settingsSaveAndReload` directly and so skipped the disk check; it has no Keep mine / Use disk prompt, so it now runs `settingsReconcileDisk(.., apply = false)` and a theme that changed on disk comes back as `DiskChanged`, refused with its own message and the running config left alone (applying the file under a pick in progress would be a second surprise); the checks for an unopenable file apply there too. The status line names causes: `FileUnopenable` (the config file or an include, with its path, through `FailureDetail` and `AppHandle.failureDetail`, because a `StatusNote` is a static sentence and a path needs an owned one, `SettingsWindow.note_text`), `BinaryConfig` (NUL bytes without a byte order mark; `UnsupportedEncoding` is now only the mark), and `IncompleteWrite` (the hard-link rewrite failed after its first byte: the file may be half old, half new, and the sentence says where the complete copy is). The failing-write path is tested by handing `rewriteOpened` a read-only handle, since a full disk cannot be staged. A UTF-16 file that does not decode is skipped whole by `loadReader` (`Config._undecodable_file` records which; `finalize` with a `theme` rebuilds the config by replay in `loadTheme` and the record has to be carried across by hand, or the check passes for every user who has a theme: found by Greptile's re-review, tested with a theme), and its defaults looked like a conflict that `settingsReconcileDisk` then applied to every terminal (found by Greptile): `settingsDiskVerdict` now refuses with `UnsupportedEncoding` naming that file, and the encoding sentences take a file name like the others (`StatusNote.takesDetail`). Harness traps: `Dir.iterate()` needs the directory opened with `.iterate = true` (a `testing.tmpDir` is not); the Git Bash tool turns `\\` into `\` inside heredocs, which silently turned `'run\appdata'` into a BEL character in a Python-written PowerShell file, so write files with backslashes through the file tool; an error inside a REPL scenario discards the output printed before it, so wrap steps in `try`.
- 2026-10-11: Win32 input mode (DEC 9001, PKG-72) is implemented: while ConPTY's `CSI ?9001h` is set and the active screen has no Kitty flags (Windows Terminal's rule, `terminalInput.cpp` `HandleKey`; modifyOtherKeys is no exception there either, and review found why it must not be: once conhost has seen a record its input parser holds a lone ESC, or ESC plus one byte, back until more input arrives, `stateMachine.cpp` `EncounteredWin32InputModeSequence`, so a switch back to VT bytes for modifyOtherKeys would delay every Escape by a keystroke), the Windows apprt attaches a `KeyEvent.Win32Record` (vk, scan code, raw control-key state, repeat count, the UTF-16 units Windows typed) to every key event and `key_encode` writes `CSI Vk;Sc;Uc;Kd;Cs;Rc _` records instead of VT. Rules that were measured, not assumed, against the same console-API observer in a real `conhost.exe` window on the private desktop (`test/windows/hidden-win11-console-key-records.ps1`; 37/37 cases pass on the branch, every record identical except a latched dead key's release, and 32/35 of the original cases differ on main): (1) conhost's key-UP records carry the key's character, because its input thread calls `TranslateMessageEx(TM_POSTCHARBREAKS)`: Shift+Enter's key up is `uc=13`, AltGr+7's is `{`; a `WM_SYSKEYUP` gets no break, so an Alt chord's key up is `uc=0`; the first draft sent `uc=0` for every release and `uc=97` for Alt+a's. (2) A latched dead key's release: `ToUnicode` with `TO_UNICODE_NO_STATE_CHANGE` reads the accent back TWICE at that point (`''` on US-International), conhost itself emits two break records and attributes one to the next key (`kd=0 vk=69 sc=0 uc=180` for an `e` not yet pressed), and Windows Terminal keeps the first unit; noctty tracks the latched key (`DeferredCharState.dead_record_vk`) and sends the accent once. conhost also zeroes the scan code of the accent a dead key flushes before Enter ("fake character", `HandleKeyEvent`); noctty keeps the key's. A Shift tap between the dead key's press and release leaves the accent latched (measured: `e` still composes and conhost still emits two release records), so the latch is dropped only by a press that typed something, not by any press (the first version dropped it on a Shift repeat, and on a VK_PACKET, whose message is marked composing). Windows posts the left Ctrl of AltGr with `lParam` bit 25 (`FAKE_KEYSTROKE`) and conhost ignores that message, down and up (`HandleKeyEvent`: "if alt-gr, ignore"), as it ignores nothing else; measured by posting the fake messages to both, noctty makes no record for it either, nor for `VK_PROCESSKEY`. (3) The record's control-key state is the RAW keyboard state (an AltGr chord reports LEFT_CTRL|RIGHT_ALT = 9), not the AltGr-normalized mods the encoder uses. (4) Typed text no longer defers to WM_CHAR while records are active (same gate as Kitty `report_all`), the accent-before-control commit is skipped (the record carries both units), and child exit clears the mode and the Kitty flags at the TOP of `childExited`: the first version cleared them in the terminal-message branch, which Win32 never reaches because `show_child_exited` returns true and `break :terminal` skips it, so a modifier press or a key release would have been the key that closes the pane (review caught it). The mode survives a `reset` keybinding, which the pty never sees (ConPTY asks again only after a RIS it parsed, `adaptDispatch.cpp` `HardReset`); `key-remap` rewrites the record's modifier bits (`Win32Record.setModifiers`; the virtual key and the typed unit stay the physical chord's); the synthesized releases on focus loss carry no record; a release that writes a record is not typing for the viewport (`keyWriteIsTyping`: before, letting go of a held key dropped a selection made meanwhile and scrolled to the bottom, as a Kitty release event already did; Greptile caught it); and the typeahead submission count is read from the records (`key_encode.win32RecordSubmissions`), never from the Enter key: only an unmodified Enter's key-down counts, because a line editor that reads records continues the line on Shift+Enter (PSReadLine's `AddLine`) and inserts one on Ctrl+Enter, and counting those consumed the shell's input mark mid-edit (Greptile caught it); a cooked read's Shift+Enter submission goes uncounted on purpose. Harness traps: (5) `ConsoleWindowClass` windows report the console CLIENT's pid and a fake tid from `GetWindowThreadProcessId`, so `AttachThreadInput`/`GetKeyboardLayout` against that tid do nothing; conhost's real input thread is the one owning its `UAC Input Indicator`/`Touch Tooltip Window` windows (`InputThreadCandidates`), confirmed by a Shift+F3 probe whose record must carry SHIFT. (6) The key-barrier marker must be posted with the chord's modifiers still held; conhost translates a posted key only when it dequeues it, and its WM_CHARs land behind everything already queued, so wait for the record stream to settle instead of expecting the marker last, and space strokes like typing (120 ms) or dead-key records interleave. Windows coalesces a posted autorepeat into one message with `rc=2` when the target has not dequeued the previous one; the record is faithful, compare expanded. (7) Post only WM_KEYDOWN/WM_KEYUP: the target's own TranslateMessage posts the WM_CHAR, so a posted WM_CHAR types the character twice under records. (8) `conhost.exe <program>` started explicitly runs `StartConsoleForCmdLine` and never hands off to the default terminal, and a `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` child gets a headless conhost; but a console program started from a console-LESS driver without `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` allocates a new console, which on a machine whose default terminal is delegated (this one: `HKCU\Console\%%Startup` points at Windows Terminal) goes through the handoff; run console helpers with `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` and captured std handles. (9) `-NoExit -Command` is PowerShell history entry 1: PSReadLine's Shift+Enter check must expect `(Get-History).Count` 2 (3 when the line ran) and read the `>>` continuation off the buffer (`console-attach-info.c`). G019 (herdr ignores mouse) is herdr's, measured with `test/windows/hidden-win11-herdr-mouse.ps1` under noctty and WezTerm on the private desktop: (10) herdr 0.8.2's default client/server mode runs with console input mode 0x298 (its client sets `ENABLE_VIRTUAL_TERMINAL_INPUT` and writes `CSI ?9001h` itself), reads records through `ReadConsoleInputW`, and its pane focus follows clicks under BOTH terminals (`pane current` w1:p1 -> w1:p2 -> w1:p1 under noctty, w1:p1 -> w1:p2 -> w1:p3 under WezTerm, where the split's pane is reported as p3; its `HERDR_WINDOWS_INPUT_TRACE=1` log shows `Mouse { kind: Down(Left), column: 136, row: 21 }` under both). Its `--no-session` mode runs with 0x98 (crossterm's `EnableMouseCapture` on Windows REPLACES the console mode with `ENABLE_MOUSE_INPUT|ENABLE_WINDOW_INPUT|ENABLE_EXTENDED_FLAGS` and emits no `CSI ?1000h`: `is_ansi_code_supported()` is false there) and reads stdin as bytes (`raw_input::spawn_input_reader`), which never carry `MOUSE_EVENT` records, so it ignores clicks under both terminals; the audit measured that mode. The records themselves reach a 0x98 reader under noctty (six per two clicks) because the 1.24 ConPTY turns `ENABLE_MOUSE_INPUT` into `CSI ?1003;1006h` (`getset.cpp`, `WriteSGR1006`) and decodes noctty's SGR replies; WezTerm 20240203's own OpenConsole delivers none, in mode 0x98, in 0x298, and to a 0x98 child that writes `?1003;1006h` itself (its focus records arrive, so it is attached). The first version of that positive control passed 12 to `WriteConsoleW` for the 13-unit request and dropped the `h`, so it requested nothing and still "proved" the same thing; review caught it, and the request is now 13. Count the units of a literal you pass with an explicit length, or pass the length from the literal. (11) Harness traps: `herdr pane split --current` needs `HERDR_PANE_ID`, so pass `--pane <id>` from `pane list`; herdr's detached server survives the client's job, so stop it through `herdr server stop` on its own `HERDR_SOCKET_PATH` and otherwise only by a parent chain the run started; herdr's config and socket follow `APPDATA` (`%APPDATA%\herdr`), its state `LOCALAPPDATA`, which it restores on the next start, so every phase gets its own `LOCALAPPDATA` or the previous phase's layout comes back; WezTerm honours STARTUPINFO's `SW_HIDE` and ignores posted clicks on a hidden or never-activated window, so launch it `SW_SHOWNOACTIVATE` on the private desktop and send `WM_ACTIVATE`/`WM_SETFOCUS` first; WezTerm gives every window the `--class` name, so take the visible one with a client area. A hidden desktop keeps the user's input out, but not the user's MODIFIERS: the terminal reads the live keyboard state for a posted click, and one run measured `CLICKS-IGNORED` under noctty with herdr's trace showing only `Mouse { kind: Moved, ..., modifiers: 1 }`, because someone was holding Shift at the real keyboard (the machine was in use) and Shift+click is a selection, not a report; the same afternoon two real-layout unit tests read LEFT_CTRL the same way. Sample `GetAsyncKeyState` for Shift/Ctrl/Alt around every posted click and mark the phase `INTERFERED` instead of reading a result. (12) On this machine the default terminal is delegated to Windows Terminal (`HKCU\Console\%%Startup`): a console program started from a console-less driver without `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` gets a new console whose in-box conhost hands off to Windows Terminal via DCOM (watched: `pwsh.exe` -> `conhost.exe` -> `OpenConsole.exe` and `WindowsTerminal.exe` under `svchost.exe`, from another agent's tree at 05:46:55), so every console helper is started with `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` and captured std handles, and `proc-watch.ps1`-style process watching is the way to attribute a `WindowsTerminal.exe` launch.
- 2026-10-11: Host banners and the update notice are on screen now (BANNER-01, from the PKG-33 finding; this supersedes that entry's "expect no visible banner change"). `Host.banner_hwnd` (control 1913) used to be a layered STATIC at alpha 0 that only carried the UIA live region while the host painted the text under the terminal; it now paints itself (`paintBanner`, for `WM_PAINT` and `WM_PRINTCLIENT`), sits at the right end of the content area's top rows, below a docked search frame that starts there, sized to its line and buttons (`placeBanner`, `chrome_layout.hostBannerWidth`/`hostBannerRect`), and is raised above the terminal whenever it is not `GetWindow(host, GW_CHILD)`. It overlays rather than reserving a row, because a reserved row resizes the terminal each time a banner comes or goes and every resize makes ConPTY and the shell redraw (#328); right-anchored, so a short message leaves a fresh prompt at column 0 uncovered. Its actions are real BUTTON children of the banner (1914 Open Release/Install, 1915 close/Dismiss) subclassed with `hostButtonProc`, with Button + Invoke providers whose names carry the message ("Close: Nothing to undo."), because F6 lands on a button, not on the text; `WM_DRAWITEM` and `WM_COMMAND` (and UIA Invoke, which posts the same `BN_CLICKED`, possibly after the button went away, so a hidden button ignores it) arrive at the banner's own subclass, and the banner needs `WS_CLIPCHILDREN`. A banner button handles its own press (`bannerButtonMouse`): the BUTTON class takes focus on `WM_LBUTTONDOWN`, which pulled focus, and the focus reports a program asked for, off the terminal on every click (render trace: 2 terminal focus changes per click before, 0 after) and left focus on the button after a press dragged off. Enter presses the focused button (a repeat never does: a held Enter must not press what the first press left focused), Tab/Left/Right move between the two, Escape closes a message but on the update notice goes back to the terminal (Dismiss records that version as dismissed). Focus goes back to the terminal when a keyboard close leaves the update notice behind, and before a focused button is hidden (a message replacing the notice). `.info` messages count down (`syncBannerExpiry`) only while shown, not pointed at (the banner or a button) and not focused, and restart in full when either ends or the same message is set again; the duration is `SPI_GETMESSAGEDURATION` (Settings > Accessibility > Visual effects > "Dismiss notifications after this amount of time", 5 to 300 s, 5 by default), the time Windows gives its own notification pop-ups. The cached `surfaces_visible` starts out true and only window messages refresh it, so the countdown also asks the window (`IsWindowVisible`, not `IsIconic`), and `refreshSurfaceVisibilityObserving` re-syncs it on every refresh, not only on a change of the flag: CodeRabbit on #336 caught a banner set before the window was first shown counting down unseen, and the first show of such a window is no change of the flag. A `WM_TIMER` is honoured only once `banner_expires_at` has passed, so a tick from an older countdown cannot clear a newer message. Failures that used to be `.info` are `.err` so they do not vanish: updater apply and startup failures (`showUpdateInfo` takes a kind), the ConPTY fallback reason, and "Theme saved, but a later config layer masks it.". Measured A/B on ReleaseFast portable copies on a hidden desktop (harness and raw results on the branch `evidence/banner01-visible-banners`). Before: the banner child was visible but the terminal was the topmost child and its GDI reached all of the banner rect, `PrintWindow(banner, 2)` was solid black, the host capture there was the GL child's white, and the update notice never showed the child at all. After, at 150% and after a move to a 96-DPI monitor: the banner is the topmost child, the terminal's GDI reaches none of its rect, `PrintWindow(banner, 2)` shows its line, 48 px tall at 144 DPI and 32 at 96, an `.info` message went after about 5 s while an error stayed, F6 reached its close button, Escape and a click hid it with focus back on `noctty.win32`, and a split made under it stayed below it. Hiding it cost the terminal one paint retry and one swap; showing it cost none. Traps. (1) `PrintWindow(child, 0)` returns FALSE and prints nothing for a child window; flag 2 or `PrintWindow(host, 0)` works, and a self-painting child must answer `WM_PRINTCLIENT`. `PrintWindow(child, 2)` makes the child repaint to capture it, so about 1 capture in 30 caught the banner mid-paint (background, no text) and looked like flicker; `BitBlt` from `GetDC(child)` reads the pixels without asking for a paint and was steady over 320 samples, before and after a split. (2) The manifest-less test binary cannot create a layered child (`CreateWindowExW` fails with 87), so `ensureChromeButtons` fails at the tab container in a unit test; give it a plain one first (`BannerTestWindow`). Test pixels need a 32-bit DIB: `CreateCompatibleBitmap(GetDC(null))` quantizes on a 16-bit display. (3) `NOCTTY_RENDER_TRACE_FILE` is written at the first swap; with `NOCTTY_RENDER_TRACE_LIVE=1` a fresh snapshot is written only when the surface receives `WM_WINHOSTTY_RENDER_TRACE_SNAPSHOT` (`WM_APP + 8`), so reading the file without posting it shows `swaps=1` forever. (4) Never press the update notice's buttons in a test: Dismiss writes the real update state and Open Release starts a browser. (5) A `-dev` build is on the `tip` channel and never checks automatically, so no source build or interactive harness shows the update notice; build with a stable `-Dversion-string` (0.0.1 here) to see one. (6) One of about 20 hidden-desktop launches lost a posted F10 two seconds after the first surface appeared (no banner, empty UIA name, the action never ran); rerun before reading a missing banner as a regression. High Contrast was checked in a unit test with `highContrastThemeFromSysColors`, not live, because turning High Contrast on is session-wide; 200% only in unit tests, because this machine's monitors are 150% and 100%.
- 2026-10-11: The Settings form lives in a child viewport window (`noctty.win32.settings.viewport`, EXT-06).
