Before it, about 70 controls sat on the top-level window, were scrolled by moving each one and clipped with `SetWindowRgn`, and the stock EDIT/STATIC/BUTTON/COMBOBOX classes carry `CS_PARENTDC`, which paints through the parent's clip and ignores a window region: after three wheel notches 23,744 header pixels and 10,935 action-bar pixels changed at 96 DPI (52,887 and 25,538 at 144), and with no `WS_CLIPCHILDREN` plus a full `RDW_ERASE | RDW_ALLCHILDREN` per layout, 7-27 of 14-27 text controls were blank right after every section switch, wheel notch and resize step (window-DC BitBlt on a hidden desktop, portable copy). The viewport's client area is the clip now; both windows have `WS_CLIPCHILDREN`, layout moves children in `DeferWindowPos` batches, scrolling is one `ScrollWindowEx(SW_SCROLLCHILDREN)`, and section switches and size steps paint before returning: 0 changed and 0 blank in the same probes. Seven traps. (1) A CBS_DROPDOWNLIST combo that is resized (every layout gives it its open-list height) puts its selection field back to the font's height, so `CB_SETITEMHEIGHT(-1)` sent before the layout is lost: measured in the test binary with a probe (set 26, `SetWindowPos` to any other height, read 19). The audit had blamed `SetWindowTheme`; `fitComboFields` runs after the layout instead, and measures the window's frame rather than assuming it. (2) `GetNextDlgGroupItem` walks into a `WS_EX_CONTROLPARENT` child and ignores `WS_GROUP` on the child itself (ReactOS documents the same), so the rail's arrow-key group ran on into the viewport's labels until the viewport's first child (the help text) got `WS_GROUP`. (3) Owning a text field's non-client area (`WM_NCCALCSIZE`/`WM_NCPAINT`, for the focus stroke and vertical centring) while the EDIT kept `WS_BORDER` made the themed (comctl32 v6) EDIT draw its 1 px border a second time inside the text area; the unmanifested test binary's user32 EDIT did not, so only the real app showed it. Fields have no border style now. (4) `ShowScrollBar(..., FALSE)` clears `WS_VSCROLL` from the window style, so a test that reads the style must first make the form overflow. (5) With `WS_CLIPCHILDREN` the parent no longer paints under a themed push button's rounded corners or a checkbox, so `DrawThemeParentBackground` relies on the parent painting its background for `WM_PRINTCLIENT`; both windows do (and still return 1 from `WM_ERASEBKGND` without painting, so a normal paint fills once). (6) The viewport is a raw-view UIA node only: a `layout_pane` `SettingsControlProvider` keeps it out of the control and content views, where the node set (names, types, count) is unchanged per section (dumped before and after); the field labels now follow the rail in sibling order, as the fields always did. `WM_COMMAND` and `WM_CTLCOLOR*` from the fields are forwarded to the Settings window, `postButtonClicked` posts to the viewport, the closed-dropdown subclass hands the wheel to the viewport (whose `DefWindowProc` passes it up), and the UIA and text-field subclasses find the window through `recoverOwner(GetParent(...))`, so the viewport carries the same `GWLP_USERDATA`. Capture what a window shows with a window-DC BitBlt: `PrintWindow` repaints the window into its bitmap first, so a missing repaint cannot show in it. (7) Moving a window keeps what it showed, and the Static class has no `CS_HREDRAW`/`CS_VREDRAW`, so a static resized while the Settings window keeps its size keeps its old picture. A resize of the window itself hides this: the frame's class has `CS_HREDRAW | CS_VREDRAW`, which redraws the whole window, children included, whenever its size changes, so removing those styles to save paints brings the trap back on every resize. Two paths resize a static inside a window of one size: a status message that needs a row of its own (85 stale pixels in a 900x560 window), and a DPI change on a window already at its capped minimum, where `WM_SETFONT` with redraw draws each static at once in the new font at its old size (923 stale pixels in the summary at a simulated 288 DPI on a 1008x689 client). A text static is now moved with `SWP_NOCOPYBITS` when its size changes (`ChildPlacements.addText`). The DPI path failed only on CI, the same box and count on both runners, because a runner's 1024x768 screen caps the window's minimum size (`WM_GETMINMAXINFO`) to the work area, so the window kept one size across the test's DPI steps; locally it grew with each DPI and the class styles redrew everything. Sizing the test window to the runner's screen did not reproduce it, because the cap reads the real work area; making `windowWorkArea` return 1024x728 did, with CI's box and count. My first theory, that the window reached past the screen, was wrong: a window half off a hidden desktop's screen captured cleanly.
- 2026-10-11: VT parser caps and print conformance (PKG-24). The limits: a kitty graphics APC payload stops at 65 MiB (`apc.Protocol.defaultMaxBytes`), an allocating OSC capture (52, 66, 5522) at 8 MiB (`osc.Parser.MAX_ALLOCATING_BUF`; 8 MiB of base64 is a 6 MiB clipboard. The cap applies to a program setting the clipboard, not to noctty's replies, which the parser never sees; the largest clipboard measured going through a program and back here was a 933 KB reply, and writing that back is far below it. Past the cap the write is dropped with one warning), OSC 7501 keeps its own 4,087-byte body limit, and a fixed capture (OSC 133 and the rest) is still 2048 bytes and dropped whole past it. One byte over drops the sequence and frees its buffer; the rest of it is ignored. Four traps. (1) A variation selector now applies to the codepoint just before it, not to the cell's first one (upstream 3f11e695d), so `U+263A U+0301 U+0301 U+FE0F` no longer widens the cell: PKG-03's grapheme-transfer test relied on that and now widens with ZWJ + U+2620. (2) A failing-first test that unwraps an optional with `.?` panics on the unfixed tree and ends the whole test binary, so every later test reports nothing: the first run against main showed 29 failures and stopped at the termio OSC 22 test; with `expectEqual(@as(?T, x), v)` it showed all 35. (3) The C API tests in `src/terminal/c/` run only in `zig build test-lib-vt -Demit-lib-vt=true`, never in `zig build test`, so a filter that names one of them runs nothing (the bare count of about 70). That step does not compile on main once it reaches `src/terminal/search/screen.zig` (an unfiltered run): in the lib-vt build `SearchError` is only `OutOfMemory` (no oniguruma), so three `else` prongs there are unreachable; CI does not run the step. A filter that names only C API tests never analyses that file and runs (no-match baseline 41 tests). With those three switches widened to `anyerror` locally (not committed), the whole step passed 4230 of 4322 with 92 skipped. (4) C0 bytes 0x10-0x1F in the ground state now execute, and DLE through US (CAN and SUB too) are not logged, because a binary dump is full of them; UTF-8-decoded C1 (U+0080-U+009F) is dropped as xterm does, while a raw C1 byte still prints U+FFFD.
- 2026-10-11: LIFE-01, two lifetime fixes. (1) `src/terminal/search/Thread.zig` keeps `ScreenSet.generation` next to each `ScreenSearch` (upstream `73ac36fa5679`, adapted): a searcher is valid only while the generation and the Screen pointer both match, `select` reconciles under the terminal lock before it selects (the fork stops the refresh timer while the surface is unfocused or hidden, so without that a select could run on a destroyed screen indefinitely), and a searcher whose screen is gone is released with `deinitScreenInvalid`, which must not untrack its pins: they were freed with the PageList. `Search.deinit` takes the terminal and runs under the lock, because `Surface.deinit` stops search while the IO thread still runs. Test trap: with the testing allocator handing the destroyed Screen block back (same address), `select` on the old code PASSED, because the stale searcher's only reads of the dead screen were its tracked selection pins and they landed in reused memory; the test asserts what a refresh keeps (the old screen's match and selection) instead of waiting for a crash. (2) `ShellExecuteW` dispatches window messages on the calling thread. Measured with a probe that mirrors the UI thread (`OleInitialize`, one window, `ShellExecuteW` open, `SW_SHOW`) on a hidden desktop: opening a URL scheme whose `shell\open\command` is a harmless program dispatched a WM_CLOSE and a WM_KEYDOWN posted before the call, a message posted during it and a cross-thread SendMessage, the first within 4 ms of a 290 ms call (the same with a `ddeexec` key); opening the program's path directly dispatched nothing in 75 ms, so measure with the target kind you care about. A pumped close then freed the Surface under `processLinks`: on a hidden desktop with Debug portable copies and a temporary HKCU `gemini` handler, a Ctrl+click on a `gemini://` link with a WM_CLOSE posted to the same surface crashed main 3 of 3 times with 0xC0000005 (cdb on the minidump: `free` from `processLinks` from `mouseButtonCallback` from `windowProc`) and the fix 0 of 3, the link opening every time. `App.openUrl` now checks the target (a refused one still fails at the caller) and queues it; `App.run` calls `drainDeferredOpens` at the top of each loop iteration, with no window procedure on the stack: it checks each target again right before `ShellExecuteW`, then does what the loop does after a dispatched message (hotkey sync, core tick), because the pumped messages can include `WM_WINHOSTTY_WAKE`, which every window procedure swallows, and repeats while that tick queued more opens. Never call anything that pumps from a window procedure or core callback whose window a pumped message can destroy. Still called in place, and not measured (launching them would leave the hidden desktop): `showOnScreenKeyboard` (`osk.exe`), the update installer's `runas`, and the `ShellExecuteExW` in `win32_elevation.zig`. Also still open in the search code (fork-only, found in review): a regex runtime error in `tickHistory` calls `failSearch` from the unlocked `Search.tick`, which untracks pins from the primary screen's PageList without the terminal lock (alternate screens have no history, and `tick` never sees `.active` because `initWithOptions` runs `tickActive` itself).
- 2026-10-11: Windows codepoint fallback (F121, PKG-31) opened every installed face again for each codepoint no font had. `filterRecordsLocked` probed each record with `initFace` + `getCharIndex` and kept a charset only for faces that HAD the codepoint, so a face that lacked it kept nothing and was reopened by the next one: 568 faces here, about 40 ms per such codepoint on the renderer thread under `SharedGrid`'s write lock, and a bold, italic or explicit-presentation lookup of the same codepoint is another `SharedGrid` key and paid again. Measured with ReleaseFast `noctty.com +show-face --string=<100 codepoints from U+10FE00>` from a portable copy: 3.7-4.6 s on main, 140-185 ms now. A face's coverage is now read once per record set and kept as sorted runs (`CodepointRange`; 126,000 runs for 3.1 million codepoints here, about 1 MB, against 12 MB as a codepoint list), lazily: `discover` ranks without coverage and `DiscoverIterator.next` reads it down the ranking only until a face has the codepoint, and a record already known to lack it is not a candidate at all. The cost moved: the first codepoint no font has reads every face's coverage once, roughly twice the old probe pass (about 50 ms here), and every later one is binary searches. Four traps. (1) `std.heap.page_allocator` maps a 4 KiB page per allocation. Discovery used it for thousands of short record strings: a scan committed about 11 MB of private memory for about 0.6 MB of data (14.7 MB against 4.3 MB after a scan, measured with `K32GetProcessMemoryInfo`; a GUI process is 148.4 MB against 137.6 MB). Never use it for many small allocations; discovery now takes `c_allocator`, as `global.zig` does in release. (2) `+show-face` calls `CodepointResolver.getIndex` directly, past `SharedGrid`'s per-key cache, so each codepoint in `--string` pays a full fallback lookup: the right tool to time fallback cost. CLI actions turn stderr logging off; set `GHOSTTY_LOG=stderr` to see discovery logs. (3) The startup prefetch held `SharedGridSet.lock` for the whole scan (F123). It now only scans, behind `Windows.scan_mutex`, and `collection()` calls `refreshExisting`, which never starts or waits for a first scan, so a grid that names no font is built without the scan and `discover` waits for it when a grid names a font. A fix that only skips the refresh while the prefetch holds the scan mutex depends on the prefetch thread winning a race (review caught this). On a warm machine the lock cost little (process start to first swap, medians of 9 on a hidden desktop: 359 against 343 ms with the default config, 375 against 360 ms with a font-family, one GetTickCount64 quantum apart) because the scan ended during window setup; if the prefetch thread cannot be spawned, the first fallback lookup now scans on the renderer thread. (4) A fail-first test that counts face opens needs the counter on main too; with `coverage_reads` added there, the new tests fail with `expected 3, found 9` and `expected 1, found 6`.
- 2026-10-11: Upstream memory-safety fixes adapted into `src/Surface.zig` (PKG-23). (1) `processLinks` takes the renderer state mutex itself and releases it before `openUrl`. Never call it with the mutex held, and never hold the mutex across the opener, which runs `ShellExecuteW` and can pump messages. An OSC 8 URI (`osc8URI`) is a slice of page memory, so copy it under the mutex. Measured on a hidden desktop with Debug portable copies and `NOCTTY_WIN32_OPEN_URL_TRACE_FILE`, Ctrl+clicking row 0 while `cmd` streamed numbered URLs with `scrollback-limit = 0`: main opened 94 of 300 clicks and 73 of those URLs were torn (`https://exa`); the fix opened 300 of 300, all whole. (2) `Terminal.fullReset` destroys the alternate screen with every pin it tracks, and the next switch recreates it under the same key. Anything outside the terminal that keeps a tracked pin must also keep `ScreenSet.generation` and, once the generation moves, drop the pin without untracking it; `Mouse.left_click_pin` now does. `src/terminal/search/Thread.zig` still compares Screen pointers, which allocator address reuse can defeat (upstream `73ac36fa5679`; adapted by LIFE-01, see that entry). (3) `TempDir` always owns its parent handle: without `TMP` it opens `.` again, because `std.fs.cwd()` on Windows is the process's own current-directory handle. `close(.retain)` closes both handles and keeps the directory. Before this, every `write_*_file` action leaked two handles (measured: +40 for 20 `write_scrollback_file:paste`, 0 after). A handle test must not trust `GetHandleInformation` on a closed value, which Windows hands out again at once (upstream removed its fd checks as flaky in `1ffdd7415`), so the TempDir tests compare the file identity the value names.
- 2026-10-11: PKG-26: every right press must reach the core with its matching release before a context-menu modal loop. A hidden-desktop portable SGR recorder on a0e45e976 measured unpressed motion as `ESC[<34;11;5M` after one menu click; the release path now clears that state and the press's consumed result gates the configured menu. Wheel lParam is signed SCREEN coordinates, so refresh only the cursor cache through ScreenToClient before scrollCallback; calling cursorPosCallback there adds a motion report. Keep the inherited report count (D21); `mouse-scroll-multiplier = discrete:1` is the user override. Surface mouse moves arm the existing TME_LEAVE tracker, and leave reports (-1,-1) to clear core link/renderer hover, except during captured drags. This supersedes the 2026-04-17 inline IDropTarget storage rule: DropTarget is independently allocated, the Surface owns its initial reference, and teardown clears the nullable Surface context before revoke and releases that reference; the final COM Release frees the target. IDataObject calls can reenter teardown, so DragEnter and Drop temporarily retain themselves and re-read the context after extraction. Callable tests cover retained references, reentrant GetData, and Unicode HDROP bytes without accessing the clipboard. CoInitializeEx(STA) alone failed RegisterDragDrop on an unshown test HWND; OleInitialize succeeds and must pair with OleUninitialize only after S_OK/S_FALSE, preserving the STA and RPC_E_CHANGED_MODE rules of the 2026-04-16 entry.
- 2026-10-11: `App.init` no longer starts the D3D11/DirectComposition/DirectWrite shell compositor (PKG-33, F003): `Backend.init(.{}, null)` leaves it in fallback, so attach, resize and render are no-ops, and `attachShellCompositorWindow` stays silent for `error.FallbackOnly`. The device-graph code (`win32_compositor_native.zig` and friends, still covered by `test.yml`'s `zig test src/apprt/win32_compositor_native.zig`) stays until the D3D11 renderer decision says whether it is reused; nothing may start it again without a consumer that pays for it. Measured on an RTX 5070 Ti, ReleaseFast portable copies on a hidden desktop, 22 interleaved runs per side (two batches of 11, the order flipped in the second) on a machine at 65-100% CPU from other builds, as medians before and after: main to first swap 484 and 328 ms, launch to first swap 687 and 516 ms, private bytes at first swap 143 and 89 MiB, idle private bytes 148 and 94 MiB, 100 and 21 threads (79 of the 100 start in `nvwgf2umx.dll`, NVIDIA's D3D11 user-mode driver, and none of the 21), 119 and 40 idle context switches per second. `d2d1.dll` and `nvwgf2umx.dll` are no longer loaded; `dwrite.dll` still is (font discovery, `discovery_windows_dwrite.zig`), and `d3d11`, `dcomp` and `dxgi` are loaded by something other than the shell compositor that was not attributed. `probeNativeCapability` is gone from the start-up path too: its 4 LoadLibrary/GetProcAddress/FreeLibrary pairs took about 6.5 ms in a fresh pwsh process (a lower bound, since pwsh has some of them loaded already). Expect no visible banner change, because host banners and the update notice are NOT visible today, with or without the compositor. (Superseded by the BANNER-01 entry above: they are on screen now.) `paintChrome` places them at `banner_y = bands.content_top + 2 DIP`, inside the terminal pane (`splitSurfacePlacement` makes `pane_rect` the whole content rect and `statusBarHeight()` is 0, so no row is reserved), and the host's `WS_CLIPCHILDREN` DC does not reach a band that child windows cover: `GetDC(host)` plus `PtVisible` on a live ReleaseFast host with a banner raised reads True over the tab strip and False over the whole banner band (7260 sampled points, 0 reached) in the default layout, with the tab bar hidden, with `window-decoration = none`, with 40 px padding, with the docked search bar open (the search frame child covers the band) and with the inspector panel open. The banner's own UIA child (`Host.banner_hwnd`, control 1913, a visible STATIC at alpha 0 while a banner is set) sits on exactly that rect too, so moving the terminal down would probably not be enough on its own (inference, the child was not isolated). The DirectWrite path is no better by the documented layering: `CreateTargetForHwnd(..., topmost = FALSE)` composes the visual tree behind child windows (MS docs, "four layers"), so the opaque GL child covers it as well (documentation, not pixels). Banners still reach screen readers through the live-region provider; "not visible" means on screen. Banner visibility is its own package. Traps. (1) A hidden desktop cannot show the composite: `PrintWindow` (flags 0 and 2) returns the tab strip and a white GL child and no DComp content, so it cannot tell "drawn under the child" from "not drawn"; the DC clip probe is the usable measurement. (2) WMI `Win32_PerfRawData_PerfProc_Thread` takes about 5 s a query, which stretches the window you divide by; use `NtQuerySystemInformation(SystemProcessInformation)` (per-thread `ContextSwitches` at +0x40 of the 0x50-byte thread record, threads from +0x100) with `QueryPerformanceCounter`. (3) `SYSTEM_THREAD_INFORMATION.StartAddress` is `RtlUserThreadStart` in ntdll for every thread; the entry point per thread needs `NtQueryInformationThread` class 9 and `THREAD_QUERY_INFORMATION` (0x40), `THREAD_QUERY_LIMITED_INFORMATION` is refused. (4) `GetTickCount64` steps by 15.6 ms and launch-to-first-swap carries 100-500 ms of process-start noise on a busy machine; compare main-to-first-swap (`process_start_to_first_swap_ms`) and take medians of interleaved runs. (5) A `-Dtest-filter=compositor` run reports 95 tests, the 70 unnamed reference tests plus 25 that match, two of them new; the new `App.init` test fails on the old code at `isStarted()`, the paint test passes on both (it pins the GDI path, not the start-up).
- 2026-10-11: A TUI that exits without popping Kitty keyboard flags leaves cmd and PSReadLine receiving literal CSI-u, including for Enter and Ctrl+C. Recovery belongs in noctty's own cmd/PowerShell prompt integrations (`CSI <99u` and `CSI >4;0m`), including the host-only reader fallback after a replaced PowerShell prompt. Never reset on generic OSC 133 A: Kitty-aware shells legitimately use those marks. Keep alternate-screen state unchanged (D11). Windows bracketed paste needs LF converted to CR through the existing `MutableRequired` copy path; queued CR bytes must still reach `submissionsInBytes`. Hidden-desktop portable checks measured Enter and Ctrl+C recovery after normal exit and exact-PID kill on cmd and pwsh. Three harness traps: a hidden desktop shares WinSta0's clipboard, so it does not isolate clipboard data; a managed `Process.ExitCode` can remain null and a native query needs a handle opened before exit; and after Ctrl+C, wait for PowerShell's next prompt rather than typing after a fixed short sleep, which can lose the next command while the shell unwinds the interrupted pipeline.
- 2026-10-11: `std.unicode.utf8ToUtf16Le(dest, src)` has no capacity check ("Assumes there is enough space for the output"; its only error is `InvalidUtf8`), so the old `drawPaletteRowText` comment promising truncation at 256 units was false: a `command-palette-entry` title or description of 300 ASCII bytes, or 300 copies of U+65E5, panicked with an index out of bounds in Debug (measured on the old call shape through the new tests) and would write past the stack buffer in ReleaseFast. `win32/text.zig` `utf8ToUtf16LeBounded` validates the whole input first (invalid UTF-8 anywhere still draws nothing, as before) and then writes at most `dest.len - 1` units without splitting a surrogate pair; it does not allocate, because the row painter runs per frame. Every other fixed-buffer `utf8ToUtf16Le` call in `src/` takes bounded input (the environment block in `Command.zig`, sized in bytes, which is never fewer than UTF-16 units, the pipe names in `pty.zig` and `Exec.zig`, one codepoint in the DirectWrite fallback, `utf8ToW` in Settings which cuts at its own capacity, and buffers sized by `calcUtf16LeLen`). The overlay paint-text cache (label, feedback, badge) freed the old slice before allocating the new one, so a failed allocation left a dangling pointer that the next paint freed again; `replaceCachedUtf16` allocates first and keeps the old text on failure, and the dirty bit stays set so the paint retries. The unit tests drive that helper and `freeCachedChromeStrings` with `FailingAllocator`; none reaches the call sites in `paintChrome`, which need a device context. `App.setLauncherQuickSlotPreference` had the same free-then-allocate order for the other slot's key and now goes through `appendOwnedString`.
- 2026-10-11: PKG-40a, the words and feedback of the Settings window (`src/apprt/win32_settings.zig`). Five things to know before touching it again. (1) A dropdown's item TEXT is free, its POSITION is a contract: the `...labels` tables, the `...FromComboIndex` / `comboIndexFrom...` pairs and `writeSettingValue` (the diff) read the same positions, and `settings dropdown text changes while the index and the saved token stay put` pins every position to the raw config token (`false`, `block_hollow`, `detect`) that is still what gets written. Do not reorder an item to tidy the list. `window-theme` `auto` and `system` do the same thing in `resolveTheme` (and `Config.finalize` turns `auto` into `system` for a light/dark theme pair), so both stay in the list and both say "Match Windows"; `ghostty` is the dark palette plus the config's title bar colors. `shell-integration = detect` is what injects the PowerShell and cmd integrations (`detectShell`), so its item says both. Labels were checked against what the runtime does, not the option's name: `confirm-close-surface = true` asks unless the cursor is at a shell prompt (`needsConfirmQuit`), which needs shell integration to know, so without it it always asks ("Unless at a shell prompt"); `copy-on-select` `true` and `clipboard` differ only in the selection clipboard, which Windows lacks, so both copy to the one clipboard; the updater only checks `stable` by itself, so `tip` reads "no automatic checks". Control NAMES (`settings_control_specs`, `annotateAccessibleControls`) and field labels were left alone on purpose: `test/windows/interactive-win11-accessibility.ps1` looks controls up by those exact names. (2) The transaction's `live_preview` flag over-promises. Font size, padding, cursor style, padding balance and blur are written into `app.config` by the preview thunk, but no open terminal reads it (EXT-04), so "Previewing now" would be false for them. The status hint therefore keys on `previewEffect(field)` (moved here from win32.zig; only `window_theme` and `background_opacity` have one), and everything else says "Applies when you save." (The unsaved value still reaches terminals created while the edit is unsaved; that is PKG-39.) (3) The status line is one `StatusNote` plus the validation and conflict state, never an ad-hoc `setStatus` string: `updateSaveEnabled` re-derives the idle text on every call, so a message set with raw `setStatus` after a failure is overwritten at once. Use `setNote`, which shows an outcome at once even while a conflict or a validation error holds the line (review caught a version that queued it behind the conflict and showed it later, about a conflict that was gone); a failure note only shows while edits remain to save, and typing in a font family, theme or command field (`markOwnedTextChanged`) replaces a stale "Settings saved." the moment Save enables. The idle text doubles as the disabled-Save cue: a disabled Save is nearly the same shade as an enabled one in the dark theme (measured by the audit, SUX-13), and owner-drawing it was refuted by the impact lens, so the line beside it says "No changes to save." in words. (4) A status message that does not fit on one line beside the buttons now gets a row of its own above them, the whole pane wide and wrapped (`statusRowGeometry`, measured with `DT_CALCRECT` in the font it is drawn in). Before, its width was `status_right`: 127 px of a 471 px pane with the three conflict buttons showing, and 0 when they stack, so "Choose Keep mine or Use disk" was cut off. Two traps: the font has to be set before the text is measured (`surfaceNextValidation` calls `syncStatusFont` first, and `setStatus` invalidates the cached measure), and a row change has to re-run `layoutChildren`, which `setStatus` does only when `statusRowChanged` says the geometry differs, because every layout ends in a full `RDW_ERASE` redraw (EXT-06's flash). The overdraw of the action bar by a scrolled form on a short window (SUX-01) is still there and untouched. (5) The palette's setting rows keep their id and payload (the field tag; saved recents and dispatch key on it) and show `SettingField.label()` with the config key in the keywords, built in one place (`paletteDescriptor`). Tab completion had returned the payload, which would now be a tag that matches nothing, so `paletteCompletionText` returns the title for a setting. The blur checkbox is disabled with "not supported on Windows" (D6), so it is not a tab stop: `settings Tab visits the fields in form order` skips disabled controls. Harness trap: pwsh 7.6.6 split `System.Drawing` (`Graphics` now lives in `System.Private.Windows.GdiPlus`), so the audit's `sux.ps1` stopped compiling with `CS0012: IGraphics is defined in an assembly that is not referenced`; add `System.Drawing.Common` plus that assembly's path (forward slashes) to `-ReferencedAssemblies`.
- 2026-10-11: `session-state.json` has one owner per user profile: the process that was eligible to restore it (`sessionRestoreEligible`), took `session-state.json.lock` (an exclusive byte-range lock held for the process lifetime, so a crash frees it) and then got its first window. `sessionStateEnabled`, the gate every save goes through, is `session_owner != null` plus the restore policy. Before this, every process that ran `terminate` saved, and with no hosts `saveSessionState` deletes the file. Measured on 88fef4f3f with portable copies on a hidden desktop: a second plain launch that forwarded to the running primary deleted the file while the primary ran; an idle `-Embedding` handoff server deleted it on its 5 s idle exit; a `--single-instance=false --working-directory=C:\Windows` window (the installer's "Open noctty here" verb) overwrote a 2-window session with its one `C:\Windows` window; and a second `single-instance = false` launch restored the same 2 windows again. Sign-out, shutdown and reboot never saved, because Windows may end the process after WM_ENDSESSION(TRUE) without `GetMessage` returning, so `terminate` never runs. `hostWindowProc` now saves on WM_ENDSESSION(TRUE), as Windows Terminal's `WindowEmperor` does (WM_QUERYENDSESSION stays with DefWindowProc, TRUE), and checkpoints on WM_DISPLAYCHANGE through a posted `WM_WINHOSTTY_SESSION_CHECKPOINT`, at most once a second. Six rules. (1) Ownership is set only after the first window exists (`run`), so an error out of restore or the first window leaves the file alone and no save runs while a restore is half done. A restore that returns false (unreadable, oversized, every window skipped) still makes the process the owner, which overwrites the file at its exit, as before. (2) A `.checkpoint` never deletes and skips once a last-host close has saved (a committed `Surface.init` clears that flag again, never `createHost`: a window whose init fails leaves the last-host-close snapshot standing); only the owner's `.final` save may delete, because closing every window ends the session. (3) A real end of session latches `session_ending`, after which nothing saves: the sign-out ends the shells too, their panes close, and those closes would otherwise shrink the snapshot or, through `terminate` with no hosts, delete it. `App.init` raises the process to shutdown level 0x2FF so it is asked before its shells (default 0x280; one level is ended in no set order). That ordering is from the `SetProcessShutdownParameters` documentation, not measured with a real sign-out. A Restart Manager close (`ENDSESSION_CLOSEAPP`) checkpoints without latching, since an installer may leave noctty running. (4) The WM_ENDSESSION save runs inside a sent message, which can arrive while the UI thread waits inside a call made under a surface's renderer mutex (the core's copy path holds it across `EmptyClipboard`, which sends to the previous clipboard owner), so `captureSessionScrollback` only `tryLock`s it and skips a surface without a live core. (5) An explicit-directory, `-e`, profile-picker, `--launch-layout` or `initial-window = false` process is not the owner, so it neither restores nor saves: what it ran was never loaded from the file, and writing it back replaced the session the next plain launch restores. The cost: when such a process is the first one running (a jump-list recent folder, `noctty --working-directory=X`), it becomes the IPC primary and nothing forwarded into it that day is saved; a reload from `window-save-state = never` does not start saving either. This reverses the 2026-09-01 test line "Startup-only restore bypasses must not disable later session saves". (6) A plain launch that starts while the previous owner is between `stopIpcServer` and the lock release in `terminate` becomes a non-owner primary; a bounded wait was tried and dropped, because the lock spans the profile while the IPC namespace is per class and elevation, so it would also stall launches that sit beside an unrelated owner. To exercise the end-session and display-change saves without signing out, send WM_QUERYENDSESSION, WM_ENDSESSION(1, lParam) and WM_DISPLAYCHANGE to the host windows with `SendMessageTimeoutW`. A unit test cannot reach `saveSessionState` itself: `localAppDataPathAlloc` returns null under `builtin.is_test`, so test the gate (`sessionStateEnabled`), `removeEmptySessionState` and the lock with explicit temporary paths.
- 2026-10-11: The native PowerShell bootstrap fixture passed locally but timed out on hosted ARM64 under `powershell.exe` with no stdout markers or stderr at its 30-second deadline (run 38110080357). Cold host startup and first-run module/assembly work need a generous liveness bound: warm each available host with `-NoProfile -NonInteractive -Command 'exit 0'`, then give both warm-up and each policy case 90 seconds. A timeout or nonzero warm-up exit still fails; only an absent optional `pwsh.exe` omits its cases, with a diagnostic. Retain all integration, policy, error-history, scope and prompt assertions; elapsed time is not an acceptance criterion. Keep the exact-handle watchdog/reaping test's deliberately short deadline separate.
- 2026-10-11: PowerShell UNC OSC 7 reports must be omitted, even with the correct filesystem `ProviderPath`. The first ProviderPath fix emitted `file://localhost/c%24/Windows` on both hosts for `\\localhost\c$\Windows`, but code review traced the receiver accepting the local authority and treating `/c$/Windows` as a WSL-shaped cwd; the network-path guard sees only that single-slash path. Noctty deliberately does not inherit network cwd, so the encoder returns null for UNC instead of handing the receiver an ambiguous URI. Regression cases on both PowerShell hosts cover the encoder, normal prompt, and replacement-prompt reader fallback while retaining OSC 133 marks. A test of URI syntax alone misses this receiver mismatch.
- 2026-10-11: `test/windows/interactive-win11-conpty-sync.ps1` (PKG-13) compares noctty's visible rows (UIA `TextPattern`) with ConPTY's own buffer (a compiled helper that runs `FreeConsole`, `AttachConsole(shell pid)`, `CONOUT$`, `ReadConsoleOutputW`; never in the driver, since `AttachConsole` is process-wide) on a hidden desktop, and `src/terminal/resize_replay.zig` replays the same shapes headless from `src/terminal/testdata/replay/*.vt`. Measured on 17a6edc76 with the bundled ConPTY, 80x20 at 150%: growing rows with cmd's prompt on the bottom row (enlarge 20->29, maximize, search bar 20->18->20, split down then closed) leaves 19-43 rows different and the next typed text lands on an old output row; narrowing 80->47 then widening differs on all 20 rows; leaving a full-screen program (vim, or a bare `?1049h`/`?1049l`) after a maximize differs on 46 rows; a rows shrink 20->8 or a cols shrink 80->43 with 15 rows below the cursor differs on 7 and 9 rows, ConPTY keeping the cursor's row as its top row while noctty snaps the cursor to another line. In sync on the same build: minimize and restore (#262), a rows shrink with the prompt on the bottom row, maximize then restore (the pull on maximize is undone by the push on restore), and a PSReadLine ListView through a split or a narrowing (PSReadLine repaints it). Five traps. (1) PSReadLine keeps its history under the known folder, not `%APPDATA%`, so a harness that types into a "sandboxed" Windows PowerShell or pwsh appends every line to the user's real `ConsoleHost_history.txt` (it happened here: 5 lines, removed again). Point it away with `Set-PSReadLineOption -HistorySavePath <sandbox>` before the first prompt; that needs `-Command`, which turns off noctty's automatic injection, so dot-source `integration.ps1` the way `buildCommandValue` does. `-HistorySaveStyle SaveNothing` also stops PSReadLine reading the file, so a seeded history (for ListView) needs `SaveAtExit`. (2) PowerShell variables are case-insensitive: a local `$history` silently overwrote the `[string[]] $History` parameter and the path became `System.String[]`. (3) A "wait until this text is on screen" needle must not occur in the command typed to produce it; the echoed command line satisfies it at once (`ALT SCREEN` in `'[HALT SCREEN'`). (4) After conhost unwraps lines on a widen, PSReadLine still draws typed input on the row its prompt used to be on (row 19 while the prompt moved to row 10): that is ConPTY's own buffer, so the harness's expectation, not a noctty bug. (5) Git for Windows' vim can drop `:q` typed while it redraws for a new size; retry with Escape first.
- 2026-10-11: PKG-14 makes noctty agree with ConPTY across resizes. Every Windows pty is a pseudo console, so `Exec.initTerminal` turns off `Terminal.flags.resize_pull_scrollback` (a backport of upstream c55f213aa, C API option 40 included): growing rows appends blank rows below instead of pulling history back into the active area, and a column reflow keeps the old top of the active area. On Windows, a maximize at a prompt therefore shows blank rows under the prompt rather than more history, as Windows Terminal does. With the flag off, a shrink that would push the cursor's row into history also drops what does not fit below the cursor instead (`PageList.keepRowActive`, rows and reflow alike), which is what conhost does; before, the cursor snapped to an unrelated row. That part is gated on the flag because upstream's own tests pin the old behaviour for other ptys (`PageList resize less rows and cols cursor near top pushed to scrollback`, `Screen: resize resets a pending-wrap cursor pushed into scrollback`), and a pty that repaints on SIGWINCH loses nothing that way. Tracked pins in the dropped rows (a selection end, a kitty placement) move to the new last row. `interactive-win11-conpty-sync.ps1` went from 8 of 17 scenarios in sync to 17 of 18 (bundled ConPTY, 80x20 at 150%). Two differences remain, measured. (1) After narrowing until lines wrap and widening back, ConPTY's row 0 holds only the tail of a line whose head scrolled off its top while narrow; noctty still has the head in scrollback and unwraps the whole line back into row 0. Every other row agrees, so later absolute writes land right; the scenario leaves row 0 out. (2) A command line that wraps, a full-screen program started from it (`?1049h` saves the cursor), a maximize that unwraps the line, then leaving the program: noctty restores the saved cursor on its row as moved by the reflow, conhost at its old coordinates one row lower, so the next prompt lands one row apart (2 rows differ; `altscreen-wrapped-maximize-powershell` keeps it marked). Started from a short command, as vim usually is, the same sequence stays in sync. Also measured: F108's suggested trigger, a resize after the child exited under `wait-after-command`, does not fail on the bundled ConPTY (the grid reflowed on main as of 45bf675ab too); a pty resize that does fail (a handoff session whose signal pipe closed returns `error.ResizeFailed` by construction) is now logged instead of skipping the terminal and renderer resize. `Termio.resize` also publishes `self.size` under the renderer lock now, since the stream handler reads it there (DECCOLM's mode 3 reset, size reports). `test-lib-vt` is not a way to run the C API test: CI does not run it, a filter that reaches `src/terminal/search/screen.zig` fails to compile there on main (three unreachable `else` prongs under the C ABI build), and a filter naming any C API test runs 41 tests, the same as a filter that matches nothing. The option was checked by building `-Demit-lib-vt=true`.
- 2026-10-10: `Surface.init` set `destroy_on_wm_destroy` as soon as the core started, while `refreshChrome`, `layout`, `requestRepaint`, `inheritWindowStateFrom` and the shell commit could still fail under its errdefers. A failure there reached `errdefer DestroyWindow(hwnd)`, whose synchronous WM_DESTROY ran `destroy` and freed the Surface; the remaining errdefers then read it and `createWindowSurface` freed it again (a stale shell revision at commit segfaults the test). The flag now means "init has committed" and is init's last statement, and an errdefer stops the core first, while GL and the HWND are alive. Rules: arm a hand-off to a window message (WM_DESTROY freeing its owner) only once nothing after it can fail; do not leave an errdefer armed past the point where what it frees has a new owner (`restoreSessionTab` kept `errdefer restored_tree.deinit()` after giving the tree to the tab, so a failed `layout` freed it twice); and since `destroy` and `windowDestroyed` never run for a failed init, its errdefers must release what they would have: the title and other strings the core sets as it starts, the new tab's button and UIA provider (`Tab.deinit` asserts the provider is gone), the quit timer `addSurface` stopped, and a new host's provisional shell id, which the Debug shell audit otherwise reports while the host window is destroyed. (Labels the core's `set_title` gave the other tabs of an existing host stay until the next chrome sync.) The core has the same trap one level down: its `Surface.init` starts the renderer and IO threads and then ran a fallible initial `set_title`, while its errdefers free state those threads use and nothing stopped them. Once a failed init no longer ends the process, that is a live-thread use-after-free (access violation 0xC0000005 after a forwarded `+new-window --title=x` whose title action fails, measured on a fault-injected portable build). Nothing may fail after both threads start, and an errdefer stops the renderer thread if the IO thread's spawn fails (and hands the older errdefers what the thread allocated, since they free copies taken before it started). The same function kept `errdefer env.deinit()` armed after handing `env` to `termio.Exec.init`, whose `deinit` frees it, so any later failure freed it twice; `Subprocess.init` now owns `env` from the call on, also when it fails, because its puts can grow the map and leave the caller's copy stale; and its font grid reference had no errdefer, which Debug reports at exit through `App.deinit`'s no-grid-left assertion. Its renderer errdefer deleted GL objects on whatever context was current, which after `finalizeSurfaceInit` or the renderer thread is no longer this surface's; it now rebinds the context first and abandons the objects if that fails, as `deinit` does. Also, one failing core-app mailbox message used to end `App.run` and close every window; `drainMailbox` now drops it with a warning, and a failed `new_window` shows a banner because a forwarded request was already acknowledged. `App.test_surface_init_stage` stands in for the GL context and core surface so the rest of `Surface.init` runs for real in a unit test; `destroy` then leaves the core-app registration, because it unregisters only a surface whose core it stopped.
- 2026-10-11: The Settings window is a plain `RegisterClassExW` window that the App pump feeds to `IsDialogMessageW`, and the dialog manager asks its window for things a plain class never answers (EXT-05). Measured on a Debug portable copy on a hidden desktop, with posted and sent messages only, before then after. (1) Enter on a focused push button did nothing (Save stayed enabled, nothing written; after, the config has the edit): for Enter the manager sends `DM_GETDEFID` to the window, and with no answer it sends `IDOK` with no control. Answering `DM_GETDEFID` with the focused button looked enough, but the manager also asks it for the OLD default after focus moves, to strip `BS_DEFPUSHBUTTON`, so the previous button would keep the default style (review finding). `handleAccelerator`, asked by the pump BEFORE `IsDialogMessageW`, takes the key instead: Enter `BM_CLICK`s a focused enabled push button (`WM_GETDLGCODE` has `DLGC_DEFPUSHBUTTON` or `DLGC_UNDEFPUSHBUTTON`; an auto-repeat is consumed without clicking again); rail radio buttons, checkboxes and fields fall through to the manager and Enter in a field still does nothing (tested with Save enabled and a save counter). Ctrl+S goes through the same hook and the Save button's own gate, and consuming the key means no `WM_CHAR` 0x13 follows. An auto-repeated Escape is swallowed there too, so holding it does not flap the close prompt. (2) Escape did nothing outside the close prompt, and the earlier comment that it arrives as `IDCANCEL` with `lParam == 0` held only while the prompt was open: the manager sends `IDCANCEL` only when the control that owns the ID is ENABLED, and the prompt's hidden "Keep editing" button owned it and was disabled while hidden (a unit test saw `IsDialogMessageW` return true yet no close request happen until the button was enabled; the missing `WM_COMMAND` is inferred from that). `closePromptActions().keep_editing` is `!close_posted` now, and `cancelRequested` runs the normal close path, which prompts when dirty, unless the prompt is open (then it is "Keep editing", with focus restored to where the prompt took it from). (3) After an activation round trip focus was on the frame, because `DefWindowProc` gives it there on `WA_ACTIVE` and a non-dialog class has no `DefDlgProc` to hand it back. Reproduced without `SendInput` by `AttachThreadInput` plus `SetForegroundWindow` between the host and the Settings window of the same process (it returns False there and the activation still changes): before, focus came back on the Settings frame; after, on the Font size edit. `WA_INACTIVE` keeps the focused child only if it is a usable descendant (live, visible, enabled), and the frame's `WM_SETFOCUS` forwards focus to it (the `DefDlgProc` pattern; with nothing usable, to the section rail). It is `WM_SETFOCUS` and not `WM_ACTIVATE` because restoring a minimized window focuses the frame with no second `WM_ACTIVATE`, and click-activation must not scroll: the forward runs under `restoring_focus`, which keeps the `EN_SETFOCUS` handler's `ensureControlVisible` from moving the form under a click that was already hit-tested (a test fails without that guard, scroll 0 became 199). (4) The wheel: `delta / 120` truncated every touchpad delta to zero (four deltas of -30 scrolled 0 px, one notch 96) and a closed focused combo took the wheel (Cursor style 1 to 2, Save enabled, page not scrolled). `scrollByWheel` accumulates in 1/120 px (`wheelScrollPixels`; a reversal drops the leftover) and scales by `SPI_GETWHEELSCROLLLINES` around the old 64 px at 3 lines (`WHEEL_PAGESCROLL` is one page); after, four -30 scroll 96 px at 144 DPI, one -120 scrolls 96, the combo keeps its selection and the page scrolls. `settingsComboProc` forwards the wheel to the window unless `CB_GETDROPPEDSTATE`; it keeps the previous procedure in the combo's own `GWLP_USERDATA` (a stock COMBOBOX leaves it unused) so it reads no `SettingsWindow` state, and detaches itself in `WM_NCDESTROY`. It is installed BEFORE `initializeControlUiaProviders`, whose subclass records whatever is installed then and unwinds through it. (5) Tab followed creation order, not the visual order: with a pending change Save was second, right after the rail. Tab, `GetNextDlgTabItem` and the UIA sibling order all follow z-order (`EnumChildWindows` lists topmost first), which is creation order, and a new child lands at the BOTTOM. `applyTabOrder` sends each control to the bottom in `formItemsForSection` order, then the action bar (Keep mine, Use disk, Save, then the close prompt's three), leaving the rail on top. Only the Appearance rail button started a dialog group, so the rail's arrow-key group would have run on into the fields and Save: each section's first field and the first action button get `WS_GROUP` (`GetNextDlgGroupItem` from the last rail button wraps to the first; Shift+Tab, tested over all seven sections, still walks the exact reverse). After: rail, then the fields as drawn, Save last. A UIA ControlView dump of all seven sections (pwsh 7 `TreeWalker.ControlViewWalker` from a hidden-desktop driver) has the same node set before and after (`sort` diff empty) and only the sibling order moved, to the visual one; PrintWindow captures of the top and bottom bands at three scroll positions hash identically between builds, so the z-order change repaints nothing differently (the caption icon is the one pixel difference). (6) Correcting one invalid scalar no longer calls `surfaceNextValidation(true)`: it teleported the user to another invalid field, possibly in another section, in the middle of typing. Save and close attempts keep the focus-moving variant. (7) The window had the generic application icon: the class `hIcon`/`hIconSm` were null. `AppHandle.appIcon` hands over the same icon the terminal windows load, for the class and for `WM_SETICON`; `WM_GETICON` went from 0 to the host's icon. (8) The `u31` overflow EXT-04 left open is fixed: `@max(0, x)` alone is a `u31`, so `height - 3` in `closeActionRowGeometry` (and `normalActionRowGeometry`) wrapped or panicked below 3 px, and `closePromptLayoutGeometry` asks `closeActionRowGeometry` with a height of 0 for an unprompted bar on a narrow pane on every paint; `width`, `height` and `available_height` are typed `i32` now. Harness traps. A `SettingsWindow` registers its class once PER INSTANCE (`class_atom` lives on the struct), so a second instance in one process fails `RegisterClassExW`; the dialog tests unregister the class after each fixture. A test that opens the window shows it, so it runs on a hidden desktop on a fresh thread (`SetThreadDesktop` fails on a thread that owns a window). Focusing a control scrolls it into view, so a wheel test must measure from the scroll position after `SetFocus`. The `interactive-win11-*` harnesses (accessibility, pr-smoke) launch `zig-out\bin\noctty.exe` on the visible desktop with `SendInput`, which the hidden-desktop and portable-copy rules forbid, so they were not run; the UIA dump above stands in for the tree check.
- 2026-10-11: Every Settings live-preview keystroke ran `App.reconfigureTheme` (every host's brushes, chrome fonts and DWM attributes, a forced `RDW_ERASE | RDW_ALLCHILDREN` redraw, then a full `SettingsWindow.themeChanged`), and every `WM_SETTINGCHANGE` / `WM_SYSCOLORCHANGE` / `WM_THEMECHANGED` ran `themeChanged` again, so typing in Font size or Opacity and an unrelated "Environment" broadcast each cost 80-200 ms of UI thread and blanked every Settings control (EXT-04). Measured on a hidden desktop with a ReleaseFast portable copy (1 host, 8 rounds; `SetText` on the EDIT, which sends `EN_CHANGE` synchronously, or a posted combo `WM_COMMAND`, around `SendMessageTimeout`): font size 89 ms median, opacity 91, cursor combo 92, window theme 93-105, `WM_SETTINGCHANGE` to Settings 100, `WM_SYSCOLORCHANGE` 102, `WM_THEMECHANGED` 91, against 1 ms for a non-preview field. After: font size 1.4-2.1, opacity 1.1-1.4, cursor 0.8, the three broadcasts 0.2-0.5 ms to Settings; a real light/dark flip still re-themes Settings (42 ms, background `#202020` to `#F3F3F3`). Those are `SendMessageTimeout` durations on a one-host session: the host's own `WM_SETTINGCHANGE` handler still runs the full `reconfigureTheme` for every broadcast and defers its paint cost to `WM_PAINT`, so only the Settings half of the broadcast cost is gone (the host half is PKG-57). The rules it left. (1) `settingsPreviewEffect` says what a preview must refresh: `window_theme` runs `reconfigureTheme` (`resolveTheme` reads `window-theme` and nothing else a preview edits), `background_opacity` runs `Surface.applyRuntimeConfig` (the host's layered alpha is the only thing that shows it), everything else only writes `app.config`. Font size, padding, cursor style and blur have no consumer on a live surface, which reads its own copy of the config: the written value reaches terminals created afterwards (`applyRuntimeConfig` reads none of them), so the 250-300 ms quiet period the audit proposed for font size and padding would have debounced nothing. A previewed field that gains a live consumer must be added to that switch. (2) `themeChanged` is keyed on its inputs, not on the broadcast: it returns when the resolved `SettingsColors` (High Contrast state included), the DPI the UI fonts were built for and the existing brushes and font all match what was applied. Do not whitelist `lParam` strings: system font and High Contrast changes arrive under several names. The host-side `WM_SETTINGCHANGE` handler still calls `reconfigureTheme` for every broadcast (PKG-57). (3) The opacity floor (0.2, `previewValue`) is applied to the preview EFFECT of an edit in `trackEdit`, never to the draft or the transaction, and never to a rollback (a config reload re-previews each dirty draft through the same `previewValue`): typing `0.85` passes through `0`, which made the host fully transparent (alpha 0) until the next digit, but a saved opacity of 0.1 must come back as 0.1 after Discard, and a saved value below the floor lowers it so a preview is never more transparent than the window the user already has. Flooring in the App thunk would have been wrong because the thunk also carries the rollback. (4) A latent overflow found on the way, NOT fixed here (fixed by EXT-05, see the entry above): `closeActionRowGeometry` computes `const height = @max(0, available_height)`, which Zig types as `u31`, and `height - 3` on the stacked path (reached when the pane is narrower than the three buttons, for example a 884 px client at 120 DPI) panics in safe builds for the 0 that `closePromptLayoutGeometry` passes for an unprompted bar. ReleaseFast wraps instead and keeps going, so shipped builds do not show it; a test that lays out a small window in Debug does. (5) Harness traps: a pwsh 7.6 / .NET 10 `Add-Type` of System.Drawing code also needs `System.Private.Windows.GdiPlus` and `System.Private.Windows.Core` in `-ReferencedAssemblies`, and a script parameter value that starts with `-` (a nested `-Bin x -Run y` string) has to be passed as `-Name:'-Bin ...'`. `cmd.exe` splits `-Doptimize=ReleaseFast` at the `=` in `%1`, so hand zig arguments to a batch file through an environment variable.
- 2026-10-11: The policy bootstrap must not assume GetExecutionPolicyCommand is already loaded: portable pwsh 7.4.13 reproduced the hosted x64 failure (TypeNotFound, no helper), while 7.6.6 passed. Assembly.Load('Microsoft.PowerShell.Security').Location resolves the binary on both pwsh and 5.1; a direct PSHOME DLL import fails on 5.1, whose assembly lives in the GAC. All nine host/policy probes pass with the assembly-name loader. The native fixture now adds -NonInteractive only after building the production payload, to forbid host startup/trust input; prompt/scope/policy assertions and the exact-child deadline remain. ARM64 CI previously timed out in powershell.exe AllSigned before the post-load markers, with the host startup warning on stderr; that stall cause is not established by the x64 reproduction.
- 2026-10-11: PowerShell auto-injection now dot-sources UTF-8 script text inside the existing child scope under Restricted and RemoteSigned, but keeps file signature checks under AllSigned (D9), and skips ConstrainedLanguage before installing hooks. Two measured traps on pwsh 7.6.6 and Windows PowerShell 5.1: the caught `$_` is a different ErrorRecord from the one stored in `$Error` (their Exception is the same), so `$Error.Remove($_)` removes nothing; preserve the old head by identity and remove only records added by the failed load. On this pwsh installation the unsigned Microsoft.PowerShell.Security manifest itself cannot autoload under AllSigned, so load Microsoft.PowerShell.Security by assembly name and import that binary before calling the real policy cmdlet; do not infer effective policy from a process environment variable or skip Group Policy precedence. The child harness now quotes the actual `-Command` through ProcessStartInfo because 5.1's native binder strips embedded double quotes in assertions. A failing-first foreign-ssh test must stub native command resolution even when it expects the profile's function: the old integration overwrites that function and a bare call would start real SSH. Filesystem PSDrive roots can add a trailing slash to ProviderPath; compare equivalent directory URIs accordingly. UNC emission now uses ProviderPath, but the terminal's existing refusal of network cwd paths remains deliberate.

- 2026-10-10: Do not read a key's text from a `ToUnicode` probe in the `WM_KEYDOWN` handler. The pump runs `TranslateMessage` before `DispatchMessageW`, so by then the layout has already latched a dead key: measured on US-International, German and French, the probe (`TO_UNICODE_NO_STATE_CHANGE`) sees the dead key as its accent typed twice (count 2, never -1) and the key after it as its bare letter, while the queue already holds the real result (`WM_DEADCHAR`, then `WM_CHAR é`, or `'` and `x` when they do not combine). The `dead_key` branch was unreachable live and the tests that set `dead_key = true` by hand could not see the two bugs that followed: each composition left one `WM_CHAR` unit authorized, which let the next Enter, Backspace, Tab, Esc or Ctrl chord through a second time, and under Kitty `report_all` the accent went out as a quote key and `é` was dropped. The surface proc now takes this key's `WM_CHAR` / `WM_DEADCHAR` from the queue (`win32_input.takeTypedChars`; posted messages are retrieved before the next input message, so nothing from a later key can be there) and that is the key's text. Five traps. (1) A filtered `PeekMessageW` still returns `WM_QUIT` and removes it (measured): re-post it. (2) `PeekMessageW` runs cross-thread sent messages even with `PM_QS_POSTMESSAGE` (measured), so drain before resolving the surface pointer. (3) `WM_SYSCHAR` stays queued: Alt+Space has to reach `DefWindowProc` for the window menu. (4) The interactive harnesses send `WM_KEYDOWN`, `WM_CHAR`, `WM_KEYUP` straight to the surface, never through `TranslateMessage`, so a key with nothing queued keeps the probe and a per-key allowance for the `WM_CHAR` that follows; that allowance is set by each press, never added to, or a leak comes back. (5) To test this for real, load the layout for the test thread with `KLF_NOTELLSHELL`, restore the thread's layout and key state, and unload only a layout the session did not already have (`src/apprt/win32/input.zig` `TestingLayout`). Two consequences of taking the queue at face value: after a dead key, Enter, Backspace, Tab, Esc and Ctrl+[ queue the accent and then their own control character, so the accent is typed before the key the core encodes (dropping both loses it, and main typed it after the key); and a dead key's press sends nothing, so its release is dropped too, and a dead key held down reports its first repeat that types text as its press, or a Kitty `report_events` client sees a key it never saw pressed. Separately, `std.unicode.utf16LeToUtf8` asserts that its destination fits instead of returning an error, so the IME's fixed 512-byte buffer overran in ReleaseFast for any composition over 170 CJK characters; size it from the source (`imeTextUtf8`). And a key or IME commit into a pane whose child has exited closes it from inside the core's `keyCallback`, which on Win32 frees the surface before `close` returns: everything after that call (the core's own inspector `defer`, the runtime's accessibility note, the next queued character) must not touch it. A ReleaseFast build hides this because teardown nulls `inspector` before freeing; a Debug build with the inspector open faults on it (0xC0000005 at the `defer`, measured).
- 2026-10-10: A fixed-drive path can still traverse a directory symlink or junction onto a network share. The WSL probe now validates cwd, search directories and executable candidates with native `NtCreateFile` and `OBJ_DONT_REPARSE`, refusing reparse traversal at any component before ordinary lookup or launch. A reparse candidate stops executable search rather than authorizing a later PATH copy that CMD would not choose. Local ancestor/final symlink fixtures cover this; the probe stays off the UI thread.

- 2026-10-10: WSL's default distribution can change while noctty runs, without changing the implicit launch argv or environment. Such terminfo probes now bypass the process-lifetime cache; only explicit `-d`/`--distribution`/`--distribution-id`/`--system` selections reuse answers. The probe remains off the UI thread, but each implicit launch now pays its probe cost and possible timeout. Regression cases distinguish selector values and command arguments from real distribution selectors. This qualifies the older WSL cache notes below.

- 2026-10-10: A synchronous (`update_now`) renderer paint inside `App.drainMailbox` can starve the UI thread for seconds. The drain pops until the mailbox is empty, and an `update_now` paint blocks in SwapBuffers until vblank before it releases the surface's repaint reservation. Whenever a present takes longer than the renderer's frame interval (a 60 Hz monitor against the 8 ms animation timer or the output follow-up burst, or heavy output at any refresh rate), the renderer usually queues the next frame before the drain looks again. Drains then run back to back. Every mailbox push also posts a `WM_WINHOSTTY_WAKE`, and posted messages outrank input and WM_TIMER in `GetMessage`, so the WAKE backlog grew faster than the pump retired it. Sent messages still got through whenever a drain lost the race (WM_NULL probes were answered every 50-300 ms), but posted keys, WM_CLOSE and WM_TIMER waited behind the backlog for seconds. Settle mode (`resize_settle_*`) is the only state that makes renderer frames `update_now`, and it ended only on its timer's last WM_TIMER, so it sustained itself. Measured: 1-22 s stalls after a resize, maximize or drag under heavy output on a 240 Hz panel. A local reproduction of the startup freeze described in #297, with no user resize: an animated custom shader on a 60 Hz display never drew typed input, because a WM_SIZE during window creation starts settle mode (about 40 synchronous paints at launch on a 240 Hz panel). The reporter's battery hardware and nu shell case remain unverified. Two bounds now hold it. The hard one: settle mode also ends at a deadline (`resize_settle_until_ms`), so a starved WM_TIMER cannot extend it. The practical one: a core-app tick spends at most one synchronous renderer paint (`rendererTickRepaintMode`), and later frames in that tick queue an invalidate, whose WM_PAINT ranks below input, so the WAKE backlog cannot grow. Bounding the drain to the messages present at entry would not have been enough on its own, because the WAKEs from the renderer's pushes still outrank input. This corrects the 2026-04-29 entry below that says renderer `.render` actions must paint `update_now` outside live resize: since #8 they queue except during settle. Measured generation-advancing presents for 30 and 60 fps TUIs were comparable before and after: their count was near the emitted-frame count at 240 Hz, and the count/emitted-frame ratio was 0.87-0.98 for 60 fps at 60 Hz. This is a proxy, not a unique-frame survival measurement: the observation window includes command and prompt output, and multiple presents can accompany one emitted frame. The run-to-run spread was measured; a refresh-phase explanation remains inference. Two harness traps. A surface on a `CreateDesktop` desktop does get keyboard focus (render trace `surface_focused=true`), so it takes the focused 8 ms cadence even though `GetForegroundWindow` never returns it there. And `pwsh -File` does not treat single quotes as quoting: a config line with spaces passed through a hidden-desktop driver arrives split (`'unfocused-render-fps`) and is silently ignored, so pass `key=value` without spaces.
- 2026-10-10: Bundled ConPTY in source builds. Releases since 1.3.125 require and ship `conpty.dll` + `OpenConsole.exe`, but a plain `zig build` never staged them (build.zig's run-step comment already claimed it did), so every source build ran Windows' in-box conhost, and the fallback banner was ReleaseFast-only, so a Debug build fell back silently: measured on `a4de01394`, a portable copy of `zig-out\bin` reported `ConPTY : inbox` with no reason. That path is a different terminal, not a cosmetic difference: the 2026-10-10 rootcause-io audit measured conhost answering DA1/DA2/DA3/DSR/DECRQM itself (mode 2026 reported `0` before and after `CSI ?2026h`) and OSC 4/10/11/12 and XTGETTCAP never coming back, so the OSC 7501 entry's "a dev run from `zig-out` has no bundled ConPTY" no longer holds: it has one unless the build printed a staging warning, and `noctty +version` names the reason when it does not (`inbox (conpty.dll is not next to noctty.exe)`). `src/build/ConptyRedist.zig` re-hashes the installed pair on every build (about 4.5 ms in the Debug build runner) and otherwise runs the release helper `Install-ConPtyRedist` through `scripts/stage-conpty-redist.ps1`, so a source build and a package are staged by one implementation from one pin. Only a download that cannot happen is tolerated (no network, `zig build --system`, no Windows PowerShell: warn, delete any installed pair the pin does not vouch for, build on); a hash or PE mismatch fails the build, and `-Dbundled-conpty=false` skips the step. Seven traps on the way. (1) TLS is compiled out of Zig 0.15's build runner (`.http_disable_tls = true` in `lib/compiler/build_runner.zig`), so `std.http.Client` panics `reached unreachable code` on an https URL inside a build step; a build step that downloads has to use a child process. (2) `zig fetch` rejects the NuGet URL (`unknown file type` for `.nupkg`), so the package cannot be a `build.zig.zon` dependency, and a lazy dependency would make an offline build fail instead of falling back anyway. (3) `std.zip.EndRecord.findBuffer` does not compile in 0.15.2: it returns `error.EndOfStream`, which is outside its declared error set, and the error points into `std/zip.zig`. (4) 0.15 has no `Build.pushInstalledFile`, and `zig build uninstall` is a `@panic("TODO")`. (5) A `.cmd` wrapper that forwards arguments with `%1` and `shift` splits `-Dtest-filter=x` at the `=` (zig then reports `Expected -Dtest-filter to be a list, but received a flag`); forward `%*`, and put a filter containing spaces in a `.cmd` file as one quoted argument. (6) A PowerShell 7 parent (CI's default `pwsh` shell, or a developer's prompt) puts its own module directories first in `PSModulePath`; a `powershell.exe` 5.1 child then loads pwsh 7's `Microsoft.PowerShell.Utility` and has no `Get-FileHash`, so the helper threw `The term 'Get-FileHash' is not recognized` and the build failed, while the same build from Git Bash passed (measured). The step removes `PSModulePath` from the child's environment, which each PowerShell rebuilds when unset, and starts `%SystemRoot%\System32\WindowsPowerShell\v1.0\powershell.exe` by path, since `std.process.Child` searches the current directory before PATH. (7) `Install-ConPtyRedist` moves a finished download into the cache with `Move-Item`, so two first-time stages sharing the Zig global cache raced: review measured four concurrent runs with two failing `Cannot create a file when that file already exists`. The entry script serializes on a named mutex instead of changing the hash-pinned helper; the wait is bounded at two minutes (then exit 3, a fallback), and a build that a mutex created by an elevated build refuses runs unserialized.
- 2026-10-10: Two Windows build traps that made portable auto-update dead on arrival (F209) and left every shipped rename on the strict path (F165). (1) `dist/windows/noctty.rc` named its block `VS_VERSION_INFO`, an undefined macro, so the block did not get resource ID 1, and FILEVERSION, PRODUCTVERSION and the version strings were commented out: the staged `noctty.exe` had no version, and `readWindowsFileVersion` (the portable downgrade guard) failed with `InstallerVersionInfoUnavailable`. The version now comes from `GhosttyExe.addWindowsResource` as `/DNOCTTY_VERSION_*` flags beside the icon-hash stamp; flags are part of Zig's resource cache key (0.0.2, 0.0.3, 0.0.2 on one tree read back 0.0.2.0, 0.0.3.0, 0.0.2.0), the fixed version is `major.minor.patch.0` (what `installerVersionAtLeastClaim` compares), a component above 65535 fails the build, and a dev build stamps its base version (`1.3.2.0`, string `1.3.2-dev+windows`). The updater test builds an empty exe with the same helper rather than compiling the whole app, and `package-windows.ps1` checks the real staged exe. (2) An explicit `-Dtarget` carries the `win10` floor, and `posix.renameatW` takes FILE_RENAME_POSIX_SEMANTICS only when the target guarantees `win10_rs5`, so `Config.zig` now raises the minimum for every Windows target. Measured with a probe on Zig 0.15.2: below the floor `Dir.rename` returns `AccessDenied` for a destination held open whatever its share mode; at the floor it succeeds when the holder shares delete, and returns `error.Unexpected` (NTSTATUS 0xC0000043, a sharing violation) when it does not, so a test must open the destination WITH `FILE_SHARE_DELETE` (a first draft without it failed at both floors, and an earlier audit note that said "without FILE_SHARE_DELETE" had the share mode backwards). `std.fs.renameAbsolute` is `MoveFileExW` and returns `AccessDenied` for the same open destination at either floor, so a caller that must replace an open file renames through `std.fs.cwd().rename` (`saveState`); other `renameAbsolute` sites are unchanged. std 0.15.2 falls back to the legacy rename only on `INVALID_PARAMETER`, so a filesystem answering `NOT_SUPPORTED` would now fail (inferred from the std source and a later upstream fix, not measured: no such share here). Harness trap: `pwsh -File x.ps1 -Filters a,b` hands a `[string[]]` parameter one string, so both filters became one token that matched nothing and the run reported the 69/70 no-match baseline.
- 2026-10-10: The second WSL identity review found five holes in the optional terminfo probe. Its cache now hashes the full launch environment in sorted key order plus the native cwd, because a forwarded `HOME` changes `~/.terminfo` and a forwarded `PATH` can change `infocmp`. The probe preserves `--cd` and uses the launch's sanitized native cwd so relative databases are checked in the right directory. A shell-form command gets automatic TERM only when it is a single unexpanded command; one Ubuntu answer must not become TERM for Kali after `&`, or for a later Windows process. An empty or option-shaped `term` is refused (`infocmp -V` succeeds without checking an entry). Zig's `std.process.Child.spawn` searches the parent PATH, not `env_map`, and does so before its wait timeout: resolve a local absolute `.exe` first, screening network/device shapes and mapped remote drives before file access, or skip the optional probe. Bare shell-form `wsl` without `.exe` and unsafe/ambiguous search paths therefore keep the fallback. Existing WSLENV flags still win, including `/w` preventing a variable from traveling into WSL. The original implementation notes below describe the first review round; its `--cd` omission and limited environment cache key are superseded here.
- 2026-10-10: A WSL shell saw a different terminal than cmd and PowerShell in the same window: native children had `TERM=xterm-ghostty`, `COLORTERM=truecolor`, `TERM_PROGRAM=ghostty` and `TERM_PROGRAM_VERSION`, bash in Ubuntu had `TERM=xterm-256color` and the other three unset, because `wsl.exe` hands a Linux process only what `WSLENV` lists and nothing in noctty listed anything. `termio/wsl_env.zig` now merges `COLORTERM`, `TERM_PROGRAM` and `TERM_PROGRAM_VERSION` into the child's `WSLENV` for a WSL launch, after the configured `env` overrides: the user's entries, flags and order stay, names are matched exactly (Linux names are case-sensitive), only names are added, and no path (`TERMINFO`) is ever exported. Each added name carries `/u`: without it a Windows program started from inside WSL gets the Linux value back (review measured `cmd.exe` from WSL printing `linux` for a plain `FOO` entry and `win` for `FOO/u`), for example `git.exe` under tmux receiving `TERM=tmux-256color`. A WSL launch is `isWslArgv` on the final argv, or the first word of a shell-form `command = wsl.exe ...`, which `execCommand` runs as `cmd.exe /C <line>` (the documented way to make WSL the default, and the first version missed it). `TERM` is listed only when the distribution has a terminfo entry for the configured `term`, asked of the distribution itself: `wsl.exe <-d/-u/--system/--distribution-id as launched> --exec infocmp <term>`, run in `Subprocess.start` (never `init`, which runs on the UI thread). The probe scans wsl.exe's options only up to the first word that is not an option, `--`, `-e` or `--exec`, skips the value of `--cd` and `--shell-type`, and gives up on any other option (`--shutdown`, `--update`): wsl.exe treats everything after the first word as the command, so `wsl.exe -d Ubuntu sudo -u postgres psql` would otherwise probe as `postgres` and `ls -d /etc` would fail with "distribution name has already been set". The Windows-side `TERM` is not consulted: `putWindowsTerm` downgrades it to `xterm-256color` when the Windows home lacks the entry (portable mode), which says nothing about the distribution, so the probe asks about `cfg.term` and, on a yes, puts that into the child's `TERM` before listing it. The probe runs in the launch's environment (an inherited `WSLENV` can forward a private `TERMINFO`, and the answer is keyed on `WSLENV`, `TERMINFO` and `TERMINFO_DIRS` as well as the command line), and it gives up on a `-d`/`-u` value that holds `%` or `^`, which `cmd.exe` would expand and the probe cannot. The cache lock covers the table only and a tab waits on a pending probe of its own question alone, so one slow distribution does not hold every other WSL tab (or a tab close, which joins the thread). A `TERM` set through `env` is listed without a probe. Answers are remembered per probe command line, including a timeout (15 s) as a no, so a hung WSL service stalls one tab and not each in turn; the probe runs inside `threadEnter`, before the event loop exists, so closing a tab joins that thread and waits for it (up to the timeout). Measured on a hidden desktop, portable copies with `LOCALAPPDATA`, `APPDATA` and `HOME` inside the run directory: before, Ubuntu bash had `TERM=xterm-256color` and the three others empty; after, `xterm-ghostty`, `truecolor`, `ghostty`, the version, `WSLENV=COLORTERM/u:TERM_PROGRAM/u:TERM_PROGRAM_VERSION/u:TERM/u`, with a direct `-e wsl.exe`, with a shell-form `command = wsl.exe -d Ubuntu`, and with no per-user terminfo in the Windows home. kali-linux (no `xterm-ghostty`; `infocmp` exits 1) kept `xterm-256color` and got the other three; a user `WSLENV=GOPATH/l:USERPROFILE/p:TERM_PROGRAM/u` came out with the missing names appended; `env = TERM=xterm-kitty` arrived as given. A starship prompt with `env_var` modules for `TERM_PROGRAM` and `COLORTERM` (temp `STARSHIP_CONFIG`) rendered `ghostty truecolor >` natively and, before the change, only `>` in Ubuntu; after, byte-identical to native. Costs and traps: the probe is a `wsl.exe` start, 200-500 ms warm and 13 s for a stopped distribution (kali), which the launch would have paid anyway, and a 5 s timeout would have missed that case. The probe uses `--exec`, so a `TERMINFO` set in a login profile is not seen. Ubuntu's `xterm-ghostty` came from a `ghostty` package and kali's ncurses-term 6.5+20250216 has none, so do not tell users which package ships it. `ssh.exe` is unchanged and differs by design: the PTY's `TERM` travels by itself, the other variables only through the opt-in `ssh-env` feature and the server's `AcceptEnv`. In this agent's Git Bash, heredocs and `sed` halve every run of backslashes, which broke a Zig string and a Python harness three times; write such files with a file tool. `wsl.exe` output is UTF-16 (strip the NULs), and Git Bash rewrites `/usr/...` arguments to `wsl.exe` unless `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1`.
- 2026-10-10: OSC 7501 program status. Four traps, all measured on a portable copy on a hidden desktop. (1) Replies to OSC queries never reach a Win32 console program through Windows' in-box conhost: with VT input enabled, a pwsh reader got the DA1 reply but nothing for `OSC 7501 ; ?` and nothing for the long-shipped `OSC 11 ; ?` either, while the same build with the bundled `conpty.dll` + `OpenConsole.exe` beside it (`info(pty): using bundled ConPTY`) delivered both, exactly once. A dev run from `zig-out` has no bundled ConPTY, so an empty reply there is conhost, not the handler; test with DA1 as a control before blaming the reply path. (2) On a `CreateDesktop` desktop `GetForegroundWindow()` never returns the noctty host and `ITaskbarList3::HrInit` fails (`taskbar progress init failed err=error.InitializationFailed`), so anything gated on "the user is looking at this tab" (`tabSeenByUser`) and the whole taskbar path cannot be observed there: cover them with unit tests (`effectiveTaskbarProgress`, `Records.acknowledge`), not a hidden-desktop run that silently never takes the branch. (3) A tab status badge takes room from the label, so it is charged to the label budget through `cached_button_label_max_width`, which the tooltip gate already reads; charging it anywhere else reopens the 2026-09-14 label/tooltip mismatch. Charge it in cells AFTER `hostTabLabelMaxWidth`: `syncTabButtons` divides the tab area without the layout's 220 DIP button cap, so subtracting the badge's pixels first changed nothing for any tab wider than about 233 px, which is most tabs. (4) GDI `Pie` inside a 10 px badge renders a 40% wedge as an unreadable sliver; the progress ring is a muted track `Ellipse` plus an `Arc` with the same 2 px pen. Harness note: in PowerShell `Cli` is a built-in alias of `Clear-Item`, and aliases resolve before functions, so a helper named `Cli` "runs" `Clear-Item` on its first argument.
- 2026-10-10: Closing a pane, tab or window, or quitting, could freeze noctty for good (robustness audit F1): `Exec.threadExit` stopped the Windows pty reader with one `CancelIoEx`, which cancels only a read that is already pending. Whenever the reader was processing a chunk or about to call `ReadFile`, the cancel was lost (the audit measured this on 29% of busy-pane closes on the in-box conhost and 38% on the bundled ConPTY), and the next synchronous read never ended. This process holds the output pipe's write end until `Pty.deinit`, after the join, so no EOF came either. The IO thread then joined the reader forever, and the UI thread, inside `DestroyWindow`, joined the IO thread. `Surface.deinit` now sets `Termio.stopping` first thing (`threadExit` sets it too, for an IO thread that ends on its own), the reader checks it before every read (`ReadThread.readUntilStopped`), and `cancelReadUntilExit` repeats the cancel every 10 ms until the reader thread exits, as Windows Terminal does (microsoft/terminal#14544). The reader can stop before the child is killed: with nothing draining the full output pipe, `TerminateProcess` plus the wait still returned in 2 ms (in-box conhost, measured). Both halves are needed. The check alone misses a reader that passed it just before the flag was set and reaches `ReadFile` after the first cancel.
