The retry alone keeps a reader running for as long as output (for example from a descendant of the killed shell) keeps its reads completing at once, so no cancel finds one pending. The same deadlock had a second entry: the reader's `.forever` pushes into the app mailbox (titles, pwd, clipboard; drained by the app thread) and the termio mailbox (replies; drained by the IO thread) waited for threads that are both joining during a close. A stopped reader now drops those messages at the source, and a push already waiting gives up through `BlockingQueue.Timeout.forever_unless`, which waits like `forever` until the flag is set. Nothing is lost: `Surface.destroy` removes the surface from the app (`deleteSurface`) before `core_surface.deinit`, so the app would drop its messages anyway. The IO thread's own `child_exited` push (`processExitCommon`) gives up the same way, and `killCommand` waits at most 2 s for the terminated child. Not changed here (read in the code, not measured): the search thread's quit pushes still wait `forever` while `Surface.deinit` joins that thread (G059); `frameCompleted`'s `renderer_health` push waits `forever` too, and on Win32 it runs on the app thread, which draws, so a health change while the app mailbox is full would wait on that thread's own queue; and the in-box `ClosePseudoConsole` before Windows 11 24H2 may wait for attached clients (all measurements here ran on build 26300, where it returns at once). Three traps. (1) A sink that blocks in its first chunk holds the real reader outside `ReadFile` deterministically, which proves the pre-read check. Nothing can hold the real reader between its check and its read, so the retry is tested with a stand-in reader released after a 100 ms sleep intended to let the first cancel run. There is no acknowledgement of that cancel: under heavy load a single-cancel mutation could occasionally pass, although the recorded mutation failed. Each new test was run against the old code (one cancel, no pre-read check, `.forever` pushes) and failed with its own error. (2) In the robustness harness, the fixture producer's 10-minute cap ends the original pane's shell, which closes the window. A long run that ends at about 600 s with a failed probe, Win32 error 1400 and a zero app exit is that cap, not a hang; pass `-ProducerMinutes`. (3) Every pane leaked its child's process handle: `Command.pid` was never closed, and libxev's process watcher closes only its own duplicate. The subprocess now closes it when it drops the command.
- 2026-10-09: `src/crash/minidump_windows.zig` never produced a dump: its `MINIDUMP_EXCEPTION_INFORMATION` was naturally aligned (24 bytes, pointer at offset 8), but `minidumpapiset.h` wraps it in `pshpack4.h` (16 bytes, pointer at offset 4), so dbghelp assembled the exception pointer from the padding and half the real pointer. `MiniDumpWriteDump` then failed with `ERROR_NOACCESS` (998) on every crash, and the errdefer deleted the empty file. The filter itself was installed and ran (stderr showed `failed to write windows minidump err=error.Unexpected` after a remote thread was started at address 0 in a hidden-desktop portable copy of 1.3.131), so an empty `crash\` directory looked exactly like "the handler never fired" (#299). Two traps: a `MiniDumpWriteDump` smoke test that passes `null` for the exception parameter succeeds with either layout and proves nothing, and in a Zig `extern struct` every field whose natural alignment exceeds the pack value needs an explicit `align(N)`; check SDK structs for `pshpack` before mirroring them. Also, per the Zig 0.15 std source, Debug and ReleaseSafe builds install Zig's vectored segfault handler (`default_enable_segfault_handler` follows runtime safety), which ends the process before the unhandled-exception filter runs for access violations and the other faults it handles, so verify crash capture end to end on a ReleaseFast build.
- 2026-10-08: In `src/renderer/Thread.zig`, the custom-shader animation draw timer and the content path (`renderOnce`, then the render follow-up) share ONE present slot per paced interval, and any interval above 8 ms (power-saver pacing, `unfocused-render-fps` with `custom-shader-animation = always`) makes them compete. The draw timer's tick is draw-only, so when it wins it presents stale frame data and pushes the deferred `updateFrame` out by a full interval, and once it leads it tends to keep winning (inference: both timers expire on the same coarse kernel tick and the draw timer, re-armed earlier, sorts first). Measured on #297 with `power-saver-rendering = on`: with one typing round, 9 of 11 runs presented none of the typed output (0 frame updates over 6-30 s) while the shader presented ~22 fps, and the other 2 were fine, so a single passing round proves nothing; `test/windows/interactive-win11-shader-pacing.ps1` types three rounds and failed 8 of 8 runs on main. A deferred update now sets `frame_update_deferred` and the next animation tick runs `renderOnce` instead of `drawFrame`. Two traps: `renderOnce` drains the mailbox, whose focus / visibility / config arms call `syncDrawTimer` and may re-arm `draw_c` from inside `drawCallback`, so the callback must re-arm only a `.dead` completion; and a WM_CHAR with no preceding WM_KEYDOWN is dropped by `DeferredCharState`, so a harness that posts bare WM_CHAR types nothing and silently measures cursor blinks. `power-saver-rendering = false` is NOT a valid value (`auto`/`on`/`off`); it is rejected and the setting stays `auto`.
- 2026-10-06: A path a program reports (OSC 7 / 9;9 / 1337 pwd, a kitty graphics `t=f` / `t=t` file) must be screened for a network or device shape BEFORE anything opens it, with the one predicate `os.path.isNetworkOrDevicePath` (two leading separators of either kind: `\\s\x`, `//s/x`, `\/s/x`, `\\?\UNC\...`, `\\.\pipe\x`, plus the NT prefix `\??\UNC\...`, which `realpath` hands to the kernel unchanged). Link opening had its own copy that missed `\/` and `/\`. Windows `posix.realpath` and `std.fs.accessAbsolute` OPEN the path, so a check after them is too late. `reportPwd` refuses these in `decodeOsc7PathForPwd` (WSL paths are normalized to `/...` first and pass), which also covers relative-link resolution, session and layout files; the later layers stay as one-line second gates. Measured trap: `std.fs.path.resolve` does NOT always return an absolute path on Windows: `resolve(&.{"\\/server/share", "f"})` is `server\share\f`, and `accessAbsolute` asserts `isAbsolute`, so `windowsPathIsRepresentable` (the gate in front of it) now rejects non-absolute input.
- 2026-10-06: Three traps from the privacy-review fixes. (1) An Inno Setup `[UninstallRun]` entry's `Check` is evaluated at INSTALL time, not at uninstall time: measured with a throwaway installer, the Check ran once while installing, returned False because nothing was registered yet, and the entry was never recorded in the uninstall log, so uninstalling after the user chose noctty as the default terminal never ran `+unregister-default-terminal`. State that must be inspected when uninstalling belongs in `CurUninstallStepChanged(usUninstall)` (also measured: it runs in the uninstaller). The installer must not be installed on a real machine to check this; a throwaway script with `CreateUninstallRegKey=no` into a temp dir proves it. The uninstaller has no console, so a CLI action it runs must not fail on a console write. (2) `std.http.Client` writes its own `user-agent: zig/<version> (std.http)` unless `RequestOptions.headers.user_agent` is `.override`; naming `user-agent` in `extra_headers` sends BOTH. A loopback `std.http.Server` reading the real request head is the way to count headers. (3) Since #290 a filtered `zig build test -Dtest-filter=<x>` runs the tests it matches PLUS the unnamed `test {}` reference blocks, which every filter keeps, so a nonzero count alone does not prove the target ran. Measured on this branch: a filter that matches nothing reports 70 tests (69 passed, 1 skipped), `-Dtest-filter="canonical version strips v prefix"` 71, `-Dtest-filter=update` 157. Compare against a no-match baseline, or read the failure names. An earlier `All 0 tests passed` from a test exe on this branch did not reproduce with `zig build test` on the same tree; rebuild before trusting a stale exe.
- 2026-10-06: Entering fullscreen from a maximized window and leaving it left the window's normal rect at the maximized size, so un-maximizing afterwards kept it screen-sized. This refines the 2026-09-24 entry below, which put it down to `restore_rect` being captured while maximized: that was half of it. Measured with `GetWindowPlacement` after every step on a hidden desktop: `enterFullscreen`'s style write omits `WS_MAXIMIZE` and un-maximizes the window in place (showCmd 3 becomes 1), so the monitor-sized `SetWindowPos` after it lands on a normal window and replaces `rcNormalPosition` with (0,0,3840,2160). The old leave path then moved the still-normal window to its saved maximized rect, which recorded (-11,-11,3851,2099) as the normal rect, and only then called `SW_MAXIMIZE`. The fix captures the `WINDOWPLACEMENT` before the style write. A window that was maximized is maximized again with `ShowWindow(SW_MAXIMIZE)` and only then given its old placement through `SetWindowPlacement`, which on a window that is already maximized rewrites just the normal rect. Any other window keeps the exact `SetWindowPos` restore, with its topmost handling. For a snapped window that also keeps the snapped rect, because its placement holds the pre-snap rect; that part is inferred, since a hidden desktop takes no shell input and snap was never measured. Five traps. (1) On a maximized window, any `GWL_STYLE` write that lacks `WS_MAXIMIZE` un-maximizes it, and every `SetWindowPos` after that rewrites the normal rect. Restore state has to be captured before the write. (2) `restore_maximized` was never cleared, yet `setInitialSize` read it as "is maximized". With `window-width` and `window-height` set (otherwise `recomputeInitialSize` never calls it), after maximize, fullscreen, leave and restore, a font-size change stopped resizing the now-normal window. A maximized window that had never been fullscreen was resized by the same font change to 1735x1144, with `WS_MAXIMIZE` still set. Ask the window with `IsZoomed` instead of trusting a cached flag. (3) A unit test that maximizes a window shows it, so it must run on a desktop of its own: `CreateDesktopW`, then `SetThreadDesktop` on a fresh `std.Thread`. `SetThreadDesktop` fails on a thread that owns any window or hook (documented), and the test runner's thread is the one every other win32 test creates windows on. The work area is shared with the interactive desktop, so the maximized rect is the real monitor's. The same test fails against the old leave path with `rcNormalPosition.left` expected 100, found -11. (4) `SetWindowPlacement` with `SW_SHOWMAXIMIZED` does not set the normal rect and maximize in one step when the window is still normal. An out-of-context `EVENT_OBJECT_LOCATIONCHANGE` trace of the real app showed it first moving the window to the normal rect, (300,200) 1400x900, for about 10 ms, and only then maximizing it: 8 location changes where maximize-first takes 6. When the fullscreen window had been moved to a monitor with a different DPI, that detour crossed DPI. The host's `WM_DPICHANGED` handler then applied the scaled suggestion, and the normal rect came back as 2100x1350. Maximize first. The window then maximizes on the monitor it is on, as Windows Terminal's `IslandWindow` does. (5) `new_window` from a maximized window (`inheritWindowStateFrom`) had the same bug. It moved the clone, still a normal window, to the source's maximized rect and then maximized it, so the clone's normal rect was that maximized rect. It now takes the source's placement through the same helper. Its leg of the test fails without the fix, also expecting 100 and finding -11. Still open: after a monitor change while fullscreen, the normal rect is not moved to the new monitor. A later restore goes back to the old monitor with the size scaled by the DPI ratio (1400x900 came back 2100x1350, and 667x467 came back 445x311). The non-maximized leave path already did the same before this change. Windows Terminal offsets its restore rect by the work-area delta and scales it with `MulDiv` by the DPI ratio.
- 2026-10-11: Quick-terminal placement and tween rectangles are computed in physical pixels; a nested `WM_DPICHANGED` must update host DPI, chrome fonts and visible surface scales without substituting its suggested size. On the mixed-DPI hidden desktop, a nonanimated 96-to-144 DPI placement intended as 3840x522 became 5760x783 before the guard. `setQuickTerminalRect` scopes the guard to the Host, restores it with `defer`, and reapplies the computed rect after a DPI transition; between tween frames the animation itself guards the suggestion. Ordinary moves still accept the suggested rect. Fullscreen restore saves the enter-time monitor/work area/DPI, translates by the work-area delta and MulDiv-scales dimensions before restoring on the current monitor. `WINDOWPLACEMENT.rcNormalPosition` is in workspace coordinates for these hosts: convert it to screen coordinates before that transform and back afterwards, or a left/top taskbar adds the work-area offset twice. Keep maximize-before-SetWindowPlacement and same-monitor exact rect restoration. Clamp translated positions and oversized dimensions to the destination work area for smaller or portrait displays; preserve logical size when it fits. Retaining an oversized width can make the neighbouring monitor own the largest window intersection and select its DPI again. Maximizing tests stay on their own desktop; measuring threads use `SetThreadDpiAwarenessContext(-4)`. Client-area animation is disabled on this hidden desktop, so the native DPI-handler tween regression exercises the guarded path without changing the system accessibility setting; real slide motion remains unmeasured here. On the small CI display, the native DPI regression requested 1600x1000 but `DefWindowProc` capped its right edge at 1044; compare the ordinary handler with a directly measured native placement and establish/read back a smaller initial rect, since `CreateWindowEx` can also permit a rect that a later `SetWindowPos` caps.
- 2026-10-06: The D that 2026-09-24 point (4) called lost is recovered for the one prompt it matters for: the prompt drawn by the user's own function right after a command replaced `function prompt` (`. $PROFILE`, a theme re-init), which `__ghostty_readline` re-wraps at that read. After `__ghostty_wrap_prompt`, and only when the wrap left `__ghostty_prompt_is_ours` true, the reader works the code out with `__ghostty_exit_code` (now shared with `__ghostty_prompt_body`) and writes `133;D;<code>;aid=` ahead of OSC 7, P and B. Keep the call inside that gate: review measured it at 155-265 us on pwsh and 380-445 us on 5.1, which a draft that computed it before the wrap paid on every line under a ReadOnly prompt, every remote line and every script read. Computing it after the wrap is safe because a wrap that made the prompt ours adds nothing to `$Error`: its reads use `-ErrorAction Ignore`, and a failed assignment leaves the prompt not ours. Measured on pwsh 7.6.6 and Windows PowerShell 5.1.26100 through piped children with a stand-in reader, and through ConPTY traces of `. $PROFILE` with a plain, a themed, a Starship and an oh-my-posh prompt. (1) A prompt that stays unwrapped (ReadOnly or Constant, or deleted) deliberately gets no D. Replayed through the terminal, a D after such a prompt's text on every line ends each command on the prompt's row, and `lastCommandOutputString` then returns the prompt's text (`LOCKED> `) for every command where without the D it returns null: wrong content is worse than missing content. For the one replaced prompt that cost is not new: the command typed at it recovers `lastCommandString` null and `lastCommandOutputString` `RELOADED> ` with or without the D, because the prompt's text was written before any mark. The replacing command itself keeps `lastCommandString`, but `lastCommandOutputString` is null, because `highlightSemanticContent` stops the output zone at the P-marked row; its duration also runs to that D and so includes the prompt's draw time (inferred from C-to-D timing, not measured). Writing P before D was evaluated from the terminal code only, not measured: it would make a one-line prompt's replacing command copyable but put a multi-line prompt's upper lines into its output, and helps neither case above. (2) The host hands the line reader the `$?` the command left even though the user's prompt ran in between, so `$ok = $?` as the reader's first statement is the command's status. `$LASTEXITCODE` and the head of `$Error` need not be the command's there: a prompt that runs a native (every git-aware prompt) moves the code. So the reader passes `-AfterPrompt`, which drops the success arm's changed-code rule (without it, `Get-Date` under a prompt whose native exits 3 was marked D;3). Costs, measured on both hosts: `& { cmd /c exit 3 }`, whose `$?` stays true, is marked 0 by the reader and 3 by the wrapper; a failure reads what the prompt left, 1 under a prompt whose native succeeded or that native's code when it failed; and a prompt that adds to `$Error` turns a second `cmd /c exit 5` into 1. (3) Terminal side: `endCommand` ignores a D with no active command and `.stop_command` breaks on a null `command_timer`, so a second D after oh-my-posh's own shell-integration D, or a D with no C before it, is harmless. (4) The README's "`function f { cmd /c exit 5 }; f` marks `0`" was wrong: the `$?`-true path reports a changed `$LASTEXITCODE`, so it marks 5, and 0 only right after another exit 5 (measured on both hosts). (5) Harness traps. A piped `-NoExit` child with PSReadLine unloaded never runs the reader hook, because the host only calls `PSConsoleHostReadLine` when a function of that name exists; define `function global:PSConsoleHostReadLine { [Console]::In.ReadLine() }` after `Remove-Module PSReadLine` and the host calls our alias from its own call stack, without PSReadLine's per-prompt `$Error` record (`-StandInReader` in `test/windows/powershell-shell-integration.ps1`). A line that only defines a function leaves `$?` as the previous line set it, on both paths, so `cmd /c exit 5` followed by a `function global:prompt` line is marked D;5 twice; put the replacement on the failing line when a case is about the code. The harness throws at its first failed assertion, so to see each new check fail against the old script, run a copy whose `Assert-True` writes instead of throwing. And the 2026-09-21 zero-test trap still bites: `-Dtest-filter=integration.ps1` alone ran nothing, `-Dtest-filter=transport` alone ran 79, and the two together plus `-Dtest-filter=PowerShell` ran 100.
- 2026-10-06: The typeahead inference for cmd (#269, #273, `SemanticCommand.markPromptStart`) was gated on `output_mark_seen`, set by any C mark and never cleared, so a cmd tab that had ever run WSL bash, pwsh or Clink lost the inference for good and a shell started there from a typed-ahead line kept cmd's `redraw=0`. Four review rounds, three of which overturned the previous fix; the rules they left. (1) The handoff case, a line typed ahead while bash or PowerShell runs `exit` back to cmd, is fixed by no longer gating `new_prompt` on the flag; nothing else touches a line typed after the C. Every variant that instead dropped the count at the next prompt after a C (a per-cycle flag) failed it, and the first tests for it ran `ping -t x` in cmd before the typed-ahead line, so they exercised cmd's second prompt and passed on such a version; a test named for a handoff has to put the line in the handoff window (and `exit` typed, not Ctrl+D: bash writes no C for that, and the typed line closes its still-open prompt). Zeroing the count at the C itself only cost the lines submitted before it, `exit` and `nu` pasted at a nested pwsh prompt, so that zeroing is gone too. (2) "A D outside a prompt drops the count" on its own is a regression: noctty's PowerShell integration without PSReadLine (PowerShell drops it under a screen reader) writes D and `A;redraw=0` for every prompt and never a C, so that rule switched the inference off for a shell that needs it. The sticky C flag now only gates the D rule, which keeps a PowerShell prompt from opening presubmitted with nothing queued after `Read-Host` or a `>>` Ctrl+C, a state only a repaint that writes B before a theme's bare A (one non-string prompt object) makes visible; the gate holds until the tab's first C. (3) Once the count survives a C, a prompt that is not `redraw=0` must never be presubmitted: bash's first prompt after a line that `ssh` or `read` consumed would be, and readline's reprint of PS1's P marks after a completion listing then reads as a new shell, which narrowing makes visible (`redraw=true` clears the upper PS1 line). bash marks C for every line it records in history and zsh, fish and nu for every line, so the inference has nothing to do for them; `markPromptStart` takes the mark's `redraw=0`. A wrongly presubmitted `redraw=0` prompt (`Read-Host; cmd` in a cmd tab) shows nothing at cmd's own prompt, whose only mark is its `A;redraw=0` on Ctrl+C; the count it carries can reach a shell cmd starts from the queue, though, where a theme's bare A after B is misread until the next accepted line.
- 2026-10-06: The 2026-09-21 filter trap below now has a cause and a fix. #163 (`3b8c12ba5`) turned `src/main.zig`'s unnamed `test { _ = entrypoint; }` into the named `test "ConPTY transport probe child dispatch only"` so the early child-mode hook had a home. `-Dtest-filter` is applied at compile time (`b.addTest(.filters)`) as a substring match on the test's FULLY QUALIFIED name, `<path under src/ with dots>.test.<name>` (Zig 0.15.2 `Zcu/PerThread.zig`): a named test whose qualified name lacks every token is dropped before analysis, anything only that test referenced is never analysed, and an unnamed test is exempt from the filter. `_ = entrypoint` is the one reference that reaches the tree (nearly every other file's tests are pulled in through it), so after #163 it survived only for a token that also matched `main.test.ConPTY transport probe child dispatch only`. Measured on `1243ef0ba` before the fix: `win32`, `scroll`, `keybind`, `Screen`, `publisher`, `fuzz` and `bounded fuzz campaign` each gave `run test ghostty-test success` in 6-9 ms at 5M RSS with no count, and `ConPTY` ran 80 (76 passed, 4 skipped). The fix is an unnamed `test { _ = entrypoint; }` beside the probe test; the probe test keeps its child dispatch and only loses that reference. After, same tree: `win32` 1541 (1539 passed, 2 skipped), `scroll` 258, `keybind` 73, `Screen` 284, `ConPTY` 81, `publisher` 74, `fuzz` 83, `bounded fuzz campaign` 76. With `NOCTTY_RUN_CONPTY_TRANSPORT_TEST=1`, `NOCTTY_CONPTY_TRANSPORT_PROBE=1` and `NOCTTY_CONPTY_KEY_INPUT_PROBE=1` set, `-Dtest-filter=ConPTY` still completes the 24 round trips (80 passed, 1 skipped), also with the two tests in the opposite order, so the dispatch does not depend on declaration order. Two corrections to the 2026-09-21 entry. Its puzzle (`ConPTY` plus `zzzznomatchzzz` ran 79, `scroll` plus `zzzznomatchzzz` ran 0) was this same mechanism, the token `ConPTY` matching the one named test that anchored the tree, not "two filters disable filtering"; repeated filters are an OR (`scroll` plus `zzzznomatchzzz` now runs 258, the same as `scroll`). And "no count means no tests ran" is no longer the tell: the count is now present and still not proof that anything matched, because about 70 unnamed `test { ... }` blocks (70 measured: `-Dtest-filter=zzznomatch` reports 69 passed, 1 skipped) are exempt from every filter. Subtract that baseline: `publisher` is 74 - 70 = 4, equal to the four `publisher` tests in `src/update/github_releases.zig`. The qualified name also carries the file path, so `session_state` gives 99 - 70 = 29, every test in `src/apprt/win32_session_state.zig`, none of whose names contains the token; counting `test "` names with grep will not reproduce a count. Two limits remain. `src/pty_transport_probe.zig` is imported only inside the named probe test, so its two probe tests run only when the filter also matches that test (`survival` gave the bare 70 although two probe tests are so named; use `ConPTY`). Do not "fix" that by importing the file from the unnamed test: the probe's child inherits the parent's environment, so a build without the dispatch test could re-run the parent probe inside its own child (inferred from the code, not run). And a filtered build now compiles the whole tree, 40-75 s here against 4-6 s when it compiled nothing, so the local flagship contracts, which run ten distinct filters, took 9 minutes 15 seconds in one run. Side effect worth knowing: every command that ran a filter on trust now really runs its tests. That is `docs/signing-rotation-runbook.md` (`publisher`), `docs/security-audit-paste-paths.md` (`fuzz`, `bounded fuzz campaign`) and the local-only `Invoke-ZigFixture` filters in `test/windows/flagship/contracts`; the first two were measured at zero before, the fixtures by the same mechanism (none of their names contains `ConPTY`), and `test/windows/flagship/Test-VerificationContracts.ps1` passes with all of them running. CI keeps its full-suite runs; a filter is still only a subset.
- 2026-10-02: `'xterm-ghostty': unknown terminal type.` under `git diff` (#286) came from Git for Windows' ncurses, not from noctty's core: `GhosttyResources` skipped `tic` on Windows, so the package shipped only `share/terminfo/ghostty.terminfo` (source) and `less` (git's pager), `tput`, `vim` and `nano` had no compiled entry. `less` 704 calls `setupterm` without an error pointer, so ncurses prints the message and exits 1 and git shows nothing. The fix has two halves. (a) The Windows build compiles the entry itself (`src/terminfo/compiled.zig`, `ghostty-build-data +terminfo-database`) into `share/terminfo/78/xterm-ghostty` and `67/ghostty`, the hex layout that ncurses on MSYS2 and Cygwin reads (Git's ncurses 6.6.20251230 ignored a letter `x/` dir). The output is byte-identical (same SHA-256, same `infocmp -x`) to that `tic -x`, and three synthetic entries compiled by it are pinned as fixtures (canceled capabilities, `OT*` names past the SVr4 ones, every string escape, 32-bit numbers, every padding byte). tic writes booleans only up to the last TRUE one, so a canceled boolean never lengthens the section; Sourcery claimed otherwise and the third fixture shows it. (b) `App.init` installs that entry into `.terminfo` under the HOME Git for Windows gives its tools (`apprt/win32_terminfo.zig`), and TERMINFO is no longer exported on Windows. git.exe sets an unset HOME to HOMEDRIVE+HOMEPATH if that is a directory, else USERPROFILE (compat/mingw.c), but the MSYS2 runtime gives a directly started tool HOMEDRIVE+HOMEPATH unchecked: review measured that with HOMEDRIVE on a missing drive, a direct `tput` looked under `/q/...` while git's children used USERPROFILE. So the install, and the TERM check that runs after the configured `env` overrides against the child's HOME, use HOME, or HOMEDRIVE+HOMEPATH only when it is a local directory, or USERPROFILE only when neither is set; otherwise they give up and TERM falls back to `xterm-256color`. They also give up on a UNC or mapped network home (`GetDriveTypeW` reads the local drive table without contacting the server), because the check runs on the UI thread at startup and for every new terminal, where a disconnected share would block for a network timeout. Measured on a hidden desktop with HOME pointed at a scratch directory: git's pager, `less`, `vim` and `tput` found it from the install's drive, from a `subst` drive, and through Git Bash -> `cmd.exe` -> `less`. The install never replaces a file it did not write: it records the SHA-256 of what it installed in `.terminfo\.noctty-installed`, beside the entries so the record follows a roaming or shared home (a first draft kept it in LOCALAPPDATA, and review measured an entry stuck at its old version once that record was gone), and rewrites a file only while it holds that entry. It creates only `.terminfo` itself, never a missing home, and writes through an exclusively created, randomly named temporary file. It is skipped in portable mode, when `term` names another entry, and under `builtin.is_test`. `hasEntry` requires a whole compiled entry (every section its header declares), so an empty, foreign or cut-off file there also falls back. Why not TERMINFO, measured with release 1.3.131 and with review drafts of this fix: (1) That ncurses joins TERMINFO, `$HOME/.terminfo` and TERMINFO_DIRS and splits the result on `:`, so the old `TERMINFO=C:\...\share/terminfo` was searched as a directory `C` relative to the current directory, then as `\...\share\terminfo` on the current directory's drive. On 1.3.131 a directory holding a planted `C\78\xterm-ghostty` with `colors#8` made `tput colors` print 8: a cloned repository chose the escape sequences git's pager emits. And with a compiled entry in place, `less` still failed whenever the current directory was on another drive. (2) No TERMINFO value is absolute to every reader. `/proc/cygdrive/c/...` is absolute to MSYS2 (and, by Cygwin's documentation, to Cygwin), but native Windows programs that honor TERMINFO (Neovim's unibilium asks the database before its built-in `ghostty` entry; Helix's termini; neo-blessed) resolve it against the root of the current drive, `C:\proc\...`, which any account can create (`icacls C:\` grants Authenticated Users `AD`). `/c/...` works only in MSYS2, `/cygdrive/c/...` only in Cygwin, `\\?\Volume{...}` gave `Invalid argument` in MSYS2, `\\?\` paths do not normalize the `/` native readers join with, and `\\localhost\C$` needs administrator rights. MSYS2 also rewrites any POSIX-looking value into `C:/...` when it starts a native program (`MSYS2_ENV_CONV_EXCL` entries are prefixes of `NAME=VALUE`). Two reviewers flagged the `/proc/cygdrive` draft and the user chose the per-user install over it. What the install leaves: standalone MSYS2 and Cygwin keep HOME in their own `/home` and still lack `xterm-ghostty`; WSL starts distributions with `xterm-256color` unless WSLENV lists TERM; and `ssh.exe` sends `TERM` unchanged, so a host without the entry still needs the `ssh-env`/`ssh-terminfo` features. Harness traps: (3) `subst` fakes the cross-drive git case: Git for Windows resolves a work tree on a `subst` drive back to its real path on C:, so git's pager passed there while a direct `less` from the same directory failed. Test another drive with a direct `less`/`tput` or through `cmd.exe`, which keeps the directory. (4) In a PowerShell `Add-Type` harness, `$null` passed to a .NET `string` parameter arrives as `""`, so `CreateProcessW($null, ...)` failed with error 3; pass `[NullString]::Value`. And `-Repo "R:\"` on a command line ends in `\"`, which `CommandLineToArgvW` reads as an escaped quote. (5) `less -FX` waits for a key when the text does not fit on one screen: a harness that only finished because `less` failed hangs once `less` works. (6) To measure the install without touching the real profile, set HOME (and LOCALAPPDATA, for noctty's other state) for the launched noctty; portable mode skips the install entirely. One full-suite run failed `ssh config caps the number of include files read` with `GetLastError(32)` (file in use) and passed on the rerun; it touches no terminfo code.
- 2026-10-01: #285 lets an undecorated Windows 11 window (the 0 px caption row of the 2026-09-24 entry below) be dragged by its tab strip, and updates that entry's "top band with client below it": the band was 8 DIP (`edge_resize_width`) and is now 4 DIP, with the strip answering `HTCAPTION` below it. Measured at 144 DPI on a hidden desktop, window (266,266)-(1546,1066), by `WM_NCHITTEST` readbacks that ask the deepest child under the point and follow `HTTRANSPARENT` up to the parent. (1) `Host.handleNcHitTest` answers `HTCAPTION` for a point the layout calls client above the bottom of the tab strip, so DefWindowProc drags the window and a double-click maximized it (to (-11,-11)-(3851,2099)) and restored it. The strip has no caption buttons: it is caption up to the right resize strip. With `window-show-tab-bar = never` there is no drag handle. (2) The band is `Metrics.top_resize_height` (`size_frame_y` without a caption row; over the integrated titlebar it stays 8 DIP). The corners keep their 8 DIP square and the side and bottom strips are unchanged. Tab bar shown: y 266..271 `HTTOP`, 272..313 `HTCAPTION`, 314.. `HTCLIENT`, corners 12 px; hidden: 266..271 `HTTOP`, then client. It is scaled linearly, 6 px at 144 DPI where `GetSystemMetricsForDpi(SM_CYFRAME)` reads 5. (3) The tabs and the [+] / [▾] buttons are BUTTON children that begin `scaled(3)` below the client top, and Windows asks the child under a point before the host, so they cut the band short (4 of its 6 px reached the host) until `tabButtonProc` and `hostButtonProc` answered `HTTRANSPARENT` there. They do so only for `HTTOP`, `HTTOPLEFT` and `HTTOPRIGHT` (`Host.resizesFromTop`): the side strips are scaled linearly from the window rect while the client starts at DWP's frame (trap 2 of that entry), so a side code would take the first tab's outer 3-6 px at 200-300%, which the button kept on main. The terminal pane keeps `resizesFrom`, every edge code. At 96 DPI the two widths are equal and no client pixel is on a side strip, so the unit test can only pin the procedures' answer, at a point in the frame. The integrated titlebar's buttons are unchanged: its band is 12 px over the empty caption and 4 px over a tab, as on main. (4) A right-click on a caption that `WM_NCCALCSIZE` made client area (this strip, or the integrated titlebar's empty caption) opened no window menu: DefWindowProc turns `WM_NCRBUTTONUP` into `WM_CONTEXTMENU`, whose handler finds the caption from the stock frame's geometry and does not ask `WM_NCHITTEST`. Posting `WM_CONTEXTMENU` at the strip opened no `#32768` menu; `SC_KEYMENU` on the same window did, the positive control. The host now handles `WM_NCRBUTTONUP` `HTCAPTION` itself with `GetSystemMenu` + `TrackPopupMenu(TPM_RETURNCMD)` + a posted `WM_SYSCOMMAND`, as Windows Terminal's `IslandWindow::OpenSystemMenu` does. It also returns 0 for `WM_NCRBUTTONDOWN` and `WM_NCRBUTTONDBLCLK` `HTCAPTION` there (`captionRightClick`): on an active window with `WS_SYSMENU` (build 26200, hidden desktop) DefWindowProc given the press runs a modal loop that ends only when it removes a `WM_RBUTTONUP`, after which neither `WM_NCRBUTTONUP` nor `WM_CONTEXTMENU` reached the window procedure, so a real right-click would likely never have reached the menu. With the press swallowed no loop starts, so the release should arrive as `WM_NCRBUTTONUP`. A real right-click was still not performed: `SendInput` cannot reach a hidden desktop (6). (5) `TrackPopupMenu` does not refresh the system menu's item states, so `windowMenuState` sets them as DefWindowProc does. Read with `GetMenuState` and `GetMenuDefaultItem` on a synthetic `WS_OVERLAPPEDWINDOW` window while an `SC_KEYMENU` menu was open (build 26200): restored grays Restore; maximized grays Move, Size and Maximize; minimized grays Size and Minimize but not Move; the bold default is Close, or Restore when minimized. `WM_CONTEXTMENU` at a caption point, which DefWindowProc's caption right-click turns into, opened the same menu with the same default (ReactOS makes Maximize the default there; Windows does not). The right-click itself could not be finished there, because of the press loop in (4). Call `GetSystemMenu(hwnd, FALSE)` before the first menu opens, or the first read is of a fresh copy with every item enabled. (6) `SendInput` cannot reach a hidden desktop, so the drag and the menu were driven by posted messages, never real input. (7) `SWP_NOMOVE` does not keep a `WS_MAXIMIZE` window in place. The unit test sets the zoom bit with `SetWindowLongPtr` and applies the frame with `SetWindowPos(SWP_NOMOVE | SWP_NOSIZE | SWP_FRAMECHANGED)`; on a 1280x800 monitor at 96 DPI (work area 1280x752) a hidden window with the host's style and `WM_NCCALCSIZE` math went from (-1280,0)-(0,800), and from 1280x799, to (-1288,-8)-(8,760), the monitor's maximized rect (the work area grown by the frame on every side), and stayed put at 1279 px wide or at (-1254,26). So covering the work area appears to be the trigger; only those cases were measured. The host is created at 1280x800 and a GitHub-hosted runner's screen is 1024x768 (actions/runner-images#2935; inferred, not measured on the runners), so the test failed on the x64 and ARM64 runners with `expected 8, found 0` (the client top was 8 px above one computed from the window rect read before the frame change) and passed here until the hosts were created at that monitor's origin. Read `GetWindowRect` again after a frame change before measuring the client against it. Over Remote Desktop this machine's monitors were 2560x1440 and 1280x800, both 96 DPI, not the 144 DPI primary of the 2026-09-24 entry, so list them (`EnumDisplayMonitors` + `GetDpiForMonitor` on a per-monitor-aware thread) before relying on a layout. The unit test stands a plain BUTTON, subclassed with the real procedures, in for a tab, because the test binary has no manifest for layered children.
- 2026-10-01: The tab strip is the first consumer of the Windows accent (#278), behind `accent-follow-system` (default on). `resolveTheme` hands the accent `resolveSystemAccentColor` read to `withSystemTabAccent`, which keeps it as read in `ThemeColors.system_accent` and a readable version in `tab_accent`; the active tab's border, the underline and the strip above a separate tab row read the latter through `tabAccent`. `readableTabAccent` keeps the accent as chosen when the underline and `tabAccentBorder`, which every layout paints, reach 3:1 against `chrome_bg` (DESIGN.md's floor for essential non-text indicators), and otherwise blends it toward white (dark) or black (light) in 5% steps until they do. The strip does not take part in that decision: only a separate tab row paints it, never the integrated titlebar, so it would let a color nobody sees on default Windows 11 alter the accent. Where its shift would fall below 3:1, `tabAccentStrip` paints the followed accent itself instead. A custom `window-titlebar-background` band re-derives `tab_accent` from `system_accent` against the band's background and polarity, never from the already adjusted `tab_accent`: with `window-theme = ghostty` the base strip is always dark, so a dark accent such as `#0063B1` was lifted toward white for that strip and then stayed lifted on a light band, where the accent as read was already readable. High Contrast, a missing or zero accent and `accent-follow-system = false` leave `tab_accent` null and the strip as before. Everything else that reads `accent` (buttons, overlays, the scrollbar thumb, profile chrome, the Settings window) still uses the theme's fixed palette. This supersedes two statements in the 2026-04-16 `blendSemanticAccent` entry: `reconfigureTheme` on `WM_DWMCOLORIZATIONCOLORCHANGED` now has a visible effect (the tab strip), and the `accent-follow-system` knob now exists because it has a consumer; it also supersedes the 2026-04-16 "Do not add user-facing config knobs for planned-but-unimplemented features" entry's listing of `accent-follow-system` among the advisory fields that were silent no-ops and got removed — it is no longer a no-op and is not removed.
- 2026-10-01: Since #282 the search bar's six buttons draw VS Code's Codicons (`arrow-up`, `arrow-down`, `regex`, `case-sensitive`, `whole-word`, `close`) from the embedded Symbols Nerd Font with `DrawTextW`. This supersedes the 2026-04-17 entry for the search bar: there `win32_icons` is now only the fallback, and the `regex` / `case_sens` / `whole_word` shapes are no longer what users see. GDI cannot select font bytes held in memory, so `registerSearchIconFont` adds them once per process with `AddFontMemResourceEx` (private to the process, released at exit). The font is kept only when `fontHasSearchCodicons` finds every button's glyph with `GetGlyphIndicesW` on a memory DC, never by face name: `CreateFontIndirectW` does not fail on a missing face but returns a substitute (per the PR, an earlier draft drew the arrows from the titlebar's "Segoe Fluent Icons"; Windows 10 ships only Segoe MDL2 Assets, so it came back as Arial and the arrows drew nothing), and an older installed "Symbols Nerd Font" can shadow the registered one. Any GDI failure counts as missing; a failed registration or a rejected font is logged once at warn. Glyphs are drawn with `DT_NOCLIP` because whole-word's bracket spans its full advance, 16 px at 100%, while a 30x28 button's padded content box is 12x14: the overflow is horizontal (measured with `GetGlyphOutlineW`; every other glyph's ink fits the box). The font is created at the first search-button paint (`Host.searchIconFont`), not in `createHost`, since registering the 2.4 MB font costs milliseconds of cold start and most sessions never open the search bar; a DPI or theme change (`recreateTitlebarIconFonts`) drops it, and a failed creation is not retried until then.
- 2026-09-24: `window-decoration = none` on Windows 11 now keeps the system frame and collapses only its caption, the integrated titlebar's non-client path with a 0 px row (`undecoratedKeepsFrame`, `Host.clientCaptionHeight`). Measured at 150% on build 26200: the undecorated host is `0x16CF0000`, client 1258x789 in 1280x800 (the side and bottom frame are the invisible resize margins), `WM_NCHITTEST` gives the edges, corners and a top band with client below it, `toggle_maximize` puts the client at (0,0,3840,2088), exactly the work area, and Alt+Space opens the window menu (#250's `hasWindowMenu` sees `WS_SYSMENU`). The border is hidden with `DWMWA_BORDER_COLOR = DWMWA_COLOR_NONE`, which `DwmGetWindowAttribute` cannot read back (`E_INVALIDARG`). Windows 10 and the quick terminal stay frameless: the quick terminal is placed flush with a screen edge by its window rect, and the invisible margins would pull it in. Read the quick-terminal flag from the Host, never the Surface: only the surface `toggleQuickTerminal` creates is flagged, and a split or tab inside the quick terminal would otherwise write the framed style into its window (review caught this). #266's rule still holds, because the cached decoration state is set before `CreateWindowEx`, so the first `WM_NCCALCSIZE` already collapses the caption; the pty was 89x22 before and after a resize round trip. A live DPI change was measured by moving the window across monitors on the hidden desktop (the primary is 144 DPI, the two others 96): the frame, the resize edges and a maximize to exactly that monitor's work area all held at 96 DPI and after moving back. Six traps. (1) A hidden desktop cannot be screen-captured (`CopyFromScreen` fails with "The handle is invalid"), and noctty calls `SetForegroundWindow`, so it must not run on the real desktop. The visual evidence came from a synthetic window with the same style, NC math and DWM attributes, shown `SW_SHOWNOACTIVATE` on the real desktop: no top strip, a 2 px border at 150% drawn over the client's top row, shadow and rounded corners, and with the border colour set to none, no border and the rest unchanged. (2) `metricsDefault` scales the 96-DPI frame linearly (12 px at 144 DPI where Windows uses 5 + 6 = 11; 13 vs 16 at 192, 18 vs 24 at 288), and `calcNcClientRect`'s maximized top inset used it, so a maximized integrated-titlebar window had its client top at y = 1 (measured), a 1 px frame row across the top of the monitor. The inset now comes from the side frame DWP has just laid out. A unit test for it must be per-monitor aware (`SetThreadDpiAwarenessContext(-4)`): the test binary is DPI-unaware and runs at 96 DPI, where the two agree. (3) A top resize band inside the client area only works where no child window covers it. With `window-show-tab-bar = never` the terminal pane starts at y = 0, so the pane answers `HTTRANSPARENT` on the host's resize edges (`Host.resizesFrom`). A hit test sent straight to the host, as the first version of both the unit test and the live probe did, cannot see this; adversarial review caught it. (4) Rewriting the style with `SetWindowLongPtr` drops `WS_MAXIMIZE`, so toggling decorations while maximized left the window un-maximized inside its maximized rect (pre-existing for every toggle). On Windows 11 a toggle no longer changes the frame, and `applyWindowStyle` now skips the write when only state bits differ. Do NOT keep `WS_MAXIMIZE` across a frame change instead: the maximized rect is the old frame's, and a frameless window kept zoomed there sits 11 px past every screen edge (measured on the Windows 10 branch). (5) pwsh is system-DPI-aware by manifest, so `SetProcessDpiAwarenessContext` fails in a pwsh driver, and a system-aware process reads every monitor at the system DPI, both from `GetDpiForMonitor` and as virtualized window rects. I first recorded this machine as having one monitor scale for exactly that reason, and a window on a 96-DPI monitor measured at 1.5 times its real coordinates. Call `SetThreadDpiAwarenessContext(-4)` on the measuring thread. Measurements on the primary were valid anyway, because the system DPI is the primary's. (6) A `WM_GETMINMAXINFO` work-area clamp for the frameless Windows 10 window was tried and dropped. When `ptMaxSize` covers the primary monitor, Windows adds the size difference between the primary and the target monitor (review measured 3850x2170 become 1930x1090 on a 1920x1080 secondary), so on a secondary larger than the primary the clamp oversizes the window. The Windows 10 branch was exercised here by forcing `undecoratedKeepsFrame` to false in a scratch build, never on Windows 10 itself. Left as they were: after entering fullscreen from maximized, leaving it and then un-maximizing restores the window to its maximized rect, because `restore_rect` was captured while maximized; decorated windows do this too. Snap was not measured, because a hidden desktop takes no shell input.
- 2026-09-24: PowerShell's B mark now comes from the line reader, measured on pwsh 7.6.6 / PSReadLine 2.4.5 and Windows PowerShell 5.1.26100 / PSReadLine 2.0.0 through the ConPTY recorder. Five findings. (1) The host draws the prompt string, then runs `PSConsoleHostReadLine`, so B written there lands after whatever the host drew (multi-object and non-string prompts included); with every OSC 133 and OSC 7 stripped, the byte streams of 15 sessions per host (plain, several objects, 42, empty, Ctrl+L, Starship transient, oh-my-posh shell integration + transient, two `. $PROFILE` shapes, a Starship re-init, ReadOnly, no PSReadLine, three transcripts) were identical to the appended-B version, so the prompt text and every PSReadLine cursor move are unchanged. (2) `Start-Transcript` records the prompt string the HOST draws, and never a PSReadLine repaint: a B appended only during `InvokePrompt` (Ctrl+L, transient prompts) stays out of the transcript on both hosts. The "Turn on PowerShell Transcription" policy starts the same host transcription (inferred, not measured). Without PSReadLine there is no reader to write B, so it still rides in the string there. (3) Never wrap the call to the real reader in try/finally. PSReadLine throws when stdin is redirected. Without a try the wrapper runs on past the throw (both flags read False afterwards) and the host still reads the line itself; with the call inside `try { } finally { }` the host took the wrapper's return for end of input and the session exited after its first prompt. Measured on both hosts; why the host tells the two apart is not established. The harness's piped `-NoExit` children catch this. (4) A prompt the wrapper did not draw (replaced by `. $PROFILE` or a theme re-init, or ReadOnly) now gets OSC 7, `133;P;k=i;redraw=0` and B from the reader, with the cursor untouched. Only when the HOST is reading, though: a script that calls `PSConsoleHostReadLine` itself (the host's read has one frame on `Get-PSCallStack`, plus one per `$NestedPromptLevel`; a script's call has more) would otherwise get B in the middle of its command. The check runs on every read and does not trust the drawn-by-us flag: a prompt function that calls the reader runs after the wrapper sets that flag, so the flag is cleared only by a host read (the check costs 10-70 us a line on both hosts). Under `Enter-PSSession` (`$Host.IsRunspacePushed`) the unmarked prompt is the remote one, which would get this machine's cwd and a local P. Its text still arrives before any prompt mark, though, so the terminal tags it as the previous command's output: replayed through the terminal, the next command then recovered `lastCommandString` null and `lastCommandOutputString` "RELOADED> ". A late P cannot retag cells already written; only the terminal can. Its D was lost as well, which cost the replacing command its command-finished notification and exit status; the 2026-10-06 entry recovers it for a replaced prompt, a ReadOnly prompt still gets none, and the `RELOADED> ` copy above still holds. (5) Harness traps: `${alias:global:Name}` is always `$null`, like the `function:global:` provider path; `${alias:Name}` from a global function reads the global alias as its definition string in about 0.01 ms. And `oh-my-posh cache path`, or any oh-my-posh run, creates `%LOCALAPPDATA%\oh-my-posh`: point `LOCALAPPDATA` at a scratch directory before running a downloaded copy.
- 2026-09-24: PowerShell's OSC 133 C, B and prompt wrapper, all measured through a master-side ConPTY recorder on pwsh 7.6.6 / PSReadLine 2.4.5 and Windows PowerShell 5.1.26100 / PSReadLine 2.0.0. (1) `AddToHistoryHandler` is not "every accepted line": PSReadLine's `GetAddToHistoryOption` returns SkipAdding before any handler runs for a line equal to the previous history entry (default `HistoryNoDuplicates`) and for a whitespace-only line, so `Get-Date` twice sent one C, and a repeated `wsl` behind Starship's transient prompt (whose Enter handler redraws the prompt AFTER noctty wrote Enter, re-opening the terminal's prompt) left the nested shell's bare A inside PowerShell's prompt with `redraw=0`: measured live in noctty, the nested prompt survived the resize. C now comes from the line reader: the console host reads every line by running the command `PSConsoleHostReadLine`, and a global ALIAS of that name resolves before PSReadLine's function, so the wrapper emits C after the real one returns. The host only runs that command when a Function or Cmdlet of the name exists, so without PSReadLine the alias is idle (`Remove-Module PSReadLine` leaves `$Error` empty; `Import-Module` revives it). (2) B belongs at the END of the string `prompt` returns: the host draws that string after the function returns, so a directly written B marked the whole prompt as input and `lastCommandString` recovered `PS C:\> Get-Date`. The host draws only the FIRST object a prompt outputs and `PS>` for empty output; PSReadLine's `InvokePrompt` draws `PS>` unless there is exactly one object. `Start-Transcript` records the returned string, so transcripts now carry B. (3) Do NOT hook `prompt` with an alias, tempting as it is (it resolves before the function, so a replaced prompt is never drawn unwrapped). Adversarial review measured two costs: `Get-Command prompt` returns the alias, whose null `.ScriptBlock` breaks a profile that chains to the prompt that way on its next `. $PROFILE` (prompt becomes `PS>`, one `$Error` record per draw); and PSReadLine derives `PromptText` ONCE, at its first ReadLine, from the `prompt` FUNCTION if that function contains no command or method call, which PowerShell's default prompt does not, so exposing the user's own function makes PSReadLine repaint `> ` after B on every parse-error transition and the terminal tags those cells as input. The prompt is instead a generated one-line function, `$__ghostty_ok = $?; __ghostty_prompt_body $__ghostty_ok <id>`, with the wrapped prompt in `$__ghostty_prompts[<id>]`: the id lives in the TEXT, so a venv's `Copy-Item function:prompt` and `deactivate`, or a chain through `$function:prompt`, keep wrapping the right prompt, a copy called inside a newer wrapper passes through without a second D/A (`__ghostty_in_prompt`), and `__ghostty_readline` re-wraps a replaced prompt before the next line: exactly one prompt after `. $PROFILE` / a theme re-init / a venv activation is drawn unmarked (for a venv, that one carries our marks after `(venv) `). (4) The old wrapper handed the user's prompt `$? = True` after every failure (Starship never showed an error in noctty). `Microsoft.PowerShell.Utility\Write-Error -Message '' -ErrorAction Ignore` as the last statement before the call makes `$?` false and records nothing, on both hosts; PSReadLine's `PSConsoleHostReadLine` reads `$?` as ITS first statement (predictor feedback), so the line-reader wrapper does the same. (5) Pin `-Confirm:$false -WhatIf:$false` on every ShouldProcess cmdlet the script runs: `$ConfirmPreference = 'Low'` in a profile stopped every launch at a `Set-Alias` confirmation and `$WhatIfPreference` silently installed nothing. (6) OSC 133 is read into a fixed 2048-byte buffer (`osc.zig` `MAX_BUF`) and a longer mark is dropped WHOLE, so a `cmdline_url` label must be measured after encoding; a line of 800 `"` characters encodes past it. Harness traps: this agent's Git Bash heredocs turned `\\` into `\` and choked on backticks, so write scripts with a file tool; UIA `TextPattern` text drops trailing spaces; and sourcing a profile that chains to the prompt twice recurses with or without noctty, so test that shape sourced once.
- 2026-09-23: The `window-decoration = none` title bar flagged at the end of the #266 entry below is measured and fixed, and the inference there was right. The undecorated host style is `WS_OVERLAPPED | WS_CLIPCHILDREN`, `WS_OVERLAPPED` is 0, and `CreateWindowEx` gives every top-level style that has neither `WS_POPUP` nor `WS_CHILD` a `WS_CAPTION` whatever was asked: `App.createHost` requested 0x02000000 and got 0x06C00000 back (caption and dialog frame, still no `WS_SYSMENU` or `WS_THICKFRAME`), client 1258x744 of 1280x800 at 150%. `SetWindowLongPtr` with the same style keeps it (0x06000000, client 1280x800). Every later style write already went through `SetWindowLongPtr` in `Surface.applyWindowStyle`: `new_window` in `inheritWindowStateFrom`, `toggle_window_decorations`, and leaving fullscreen. So every window created with no window to copy kept the caption until a toggle or fullscreen round trip: measured on the first window, and by the same `createHost` call with no clone source for the quick terminal, the first pane of each restored window and an adopted default-terminal window, and a clone showed it only until its surface had initialized. `createHost` now writes the requested style back while the host is hidden and has no tabs (`restoreRequestedHostWindowStyle`), AFTER `attachShellCompositorWindow`: the frame change resizes the client area, and the host's `WM_SIZE` resizes its compositor target. Before the attach, that resize logged `shell compositor resize unavailable err=error.AttachFailed` for every such window once another window had made the compositor active; adversarial review caught it. Do not dodge the rule by making the undecorated style `WS_POPUP`: `CW_USEDEFAULT` puts a popup at (0, 0) instead of the next default position (measured on a synthetic window). Two probe traps. (1) Alt+Space posted to an INACTIVE window's surface opens the ACTIVE window's system menu (`GetGUIThreadInfo` reports `hwndMenuOwner` = the active host), so a probe of #250's `hasWindowMenu` gate must target the active window. One posted Escape hides the popup but leaves the thread in menu mode (`GUI_INMENUMODE`) until a second Escape, and a probe run in that state measures the leftover menu. (2) `pwsh -File x.ps1 -Config a,b` hands a `[string[]]` parameter ONE string, `"a,b"`: a two-line test config became one invalid line, and both runs silently used the default config. Left as it was, for a product decision (RESOLVED on Windows 11 by the 2026-09-24 frame entry above): an undecorated window has no resize border (`WM_NCHITTEST` answers `HTCLIENT` at every edge), no DWM frame (so no border or shadow), and no `WS_SYSMENU` (so no Alt+Space move or size), and `toggle_maximize` covers the taskbar (3840x2160 on a 3840x2088 work area). All of that held before and after this fix, on every path. A synthetic `WS_POPUP | WS_THICKFRAME` window resizes and keeps the shadow, but Windows 11 draws its top sizing border as a visible strip unless `WM_NCCALCSIZE` removes the top frame, and then the top edge needs its own hit test.
- 2026-09-23: After #264 an OSC 52 paste into Neovim no longer loses bytes inside noctty, and #261's symptom (base64 typed into the buffer) now needs a pause inside the reply that is longer than Neovim's `'ttimeoutlen'`; the pause comes from whatever sits between noctty and Neovim. Measured on main (native, WSL 2 via `wsl.exe`, `ssh.exe` into an sshd; Neovim 0.10.4, 0.11.7 and 0.12.5; clipboards of 1 KB to 700 KB): the bundled OpenConsole hands a console client the whole reply at once (933 KB in 42 ms); `wsl.exe` forwards it in 16-32 byte reads under 1 ms apart, so `'ttimeoutlen'` 1 leaks; OpenSSH for Windows 9.5 holds the last small read back 25-43 ms for keystroke-timing obfuscation, so 10-40 leaks when nothing follows the reply, and `-o ObscureKeystrokeTiming=no` shrinks that pause to 2 ms. Neovim's default 50 never leaked on a local path. Over SSH with an emulated 60-100 ms round trip it leaked 13 of 13 pastes (1 KB to 200 KB) when the paste key went through `ssh.exe` and Allow on noctty's default `clipboard-read = ask` prompt was clicked 3 s later: the connection idles while the prompt waits, so TCP restarts from a small window and `ssh.exe`'s many small packets arrive in bursts one round trip apart. With `clipboard-read = allow`, or Allow within 300 ms, 0 of 17 leaked, because ssh's keystroke chaff keeps the connection busy. Trigger the paste through the terminal when measuring this: a paste started over RPC leaves the connection idle and gave misleading rates (8 of 19 even in allow mode). The round trip was emulated without touching the shared WSL network namespace: two private netns joined by a veth, sshd in one, netem on the ACK side only (delaying the data side puts packets in the sender's own qdisc, where autocorking and TCP small queues change the bursts), and a root-namespace TCP listener bridged in over a unix socket; the delayed TCP sender is therefore Linux, not Windows, so the rates are indicative. With `'nottimeout'` or a negative `'ttimeoutlen'` every paste leaks on any path, because the TUI splits the reply at the ESC of its `ESC \` terminator and forces the first part at once. `'ttimeoutlen'` 0 under WSL or SSH also types noctty's XTGETTCAP and DECRQSS startup replies into the buffer. Traps: (1) the old pool's end-to-end trigger is not a size threshold. On 1.3.129 a 196,000-byte clipboard (a 64-chunk reply) leaked in 4 of 5 runs with 3 keys after Allow, while 199,956 bytes (66 chunks) leaked in 0 of 18 runs on 1.3.129 and 1.3.130 with 3 to 40 keys; sweep sizes around the pool's power-of-two growth points before calling a pool-like bug fixed or absent. (2) `vim.g.clipboard = 'osc52'` as a string needs Neovim 0.11. 0.10 rejects it and `"+` quietly becomes a local register, so a 0.10 trial that yanks and pastes through `+` never sends OSC 52; the 0.10.4 part of the previous entry's native result rested on that. Use the table form with `require('vim.ui.clipboard.osc52')`. (3) PrintWindow returns a white surface for noctty on a hidden desktop because the terminal is a GL child; read Neovim's grid with `screenstring()` over RPC. (4) `nvim --listen 127.0.0.1:<random port>` intermittently dies inside Windows' excluded port ranges (`netsh int ipv4 show excludedportrange protocol=tcp`), and noctty shows that as an abnormal process exit. (5) A Neovim left in a pending command by typed junk answers no RPC and runs no `vim.schedule` callbacks until its TUI goes away, so an in-process state heartbeat lands only at teardown. On the harness side, an idle WSL distro shuts down and empties `/tmp` at its next start (keep a `sleep` session open and use `/var/tmp`), Git Bash rewrites `/var/tmp/...` arguments to `wsl.exe` unless `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1`, and Python text-mode writes on this host turn a whole file's LF endings into CRLF.
- 2026-09-23: Item (b) of the #262 entry below, "the core grid starts one row short", is fixed, and the child did not reach its height late because of some slow layout: the host's frame changed under it. `CreateWindowEx` sends the first `WM_NCCALCSIZE` with wParam FALSE, where lParam is a bare `RECT` rather than `NCCALCSIZE_PARAMS`, and `Host.handleNcCalcSize` left that form to DefWindowProc. Every new host therefore started with the stock caption's client area (1258x744 at 150%, content 1258x684) and switched to the integrated one (1258x789, content 1258x729) at its first `SWP_FRAMECHANGED`. That first refresh comes from inside the core's own init: `Surface.init` reads `getSize()`, then its `size_limit` action runs `setSizeLimit` -> `refreshSharedWindowFrame`, the host grows and lays out again, and `syncCoreSizeFromClientRect` records 1258x729 in `self.size` but cannot forward it while `core_initialized` is false. The core opens the pty at its init-time 89x21, and every later sync compares 729 with the 729 it already recorded, so nothing ever sends it. Measured through the IROW instrumentation and `AttachConsole` on the first window and on every `new_window`, with `window-show-tab-bar` both `auto` and `always`. New tabs, splits, `never` and `window-decoration = none` were never short: either no integrated frame change happens during their init or their host already has its final frame. The handler now takes both forms, and `Surface.init` hands the core `self.size` as soon as `core_initialized` is set. The core ignores a size it already has, so this costs nothing unless something moved the child during init, and then it arrives as an ordinary resize after the pty opened (measured with the handler change reverted: pty opened 89x21, then set to 89x22). The general trap: a size cache that dedupes notifications must be compared against what the consumer actually received. An update recorded while the consumer was not listening is otherwise lost for good, and the only symptom is a grid that is quietly one row short. Harness notes. The whole driver ran on a separate desktop: `CreateDesktopW`, then `CreateProcessW` with `lpDesktop = "WinSta0\\<name>"` for a pwsh driver. noctty, `EnumWindows`, `SendMessage` keys and the console probe all work from there, and the user's foreground is never touched. A driver that is not DPI aware reads a 1258x789 client as 838x526, so call `SetProcessDpiAwarenessContext(-4)` first. `PrintWindow` on that desktop shows the GL child blank, and shows a new window's tab button unpainted for a few seconds, identically on main; do not read either as a regression. Portable mode (`noctty.portable` beside the exe) plus `--window-save-state=never`, with the exe directory wiped between runs, kept every launch off the real profile. Seen on the way, FIXED by the `window-decoration = none` entry above: with `window-decoration = none` the FIRST window still has a stock caption (host 1258x744) while a `new_window` from it has none (1280x800). The likely cause, inferred and not measured, is that `effectiveHostWindowStyle` returns `WS_OVERLAPPED`, which is 0, and `CreateWindowEx` adds `WS_CAPTION` to a zero overlapped style.
- 2026-09-23: Base64 typed into Neovim after an OSC 52 paste (#261) came from termio's pty write pool, which dropped whole 4 KiB chunks of large input. `SegmentedPool` handed out request+buffer slots by ring index and took them back by count, which only works while the ring never grows: growing when the oldest in-flight slot was not slot 0 made it later hand out a slot still queued in xev's intrusive `WriteQueue`, `queueWrite` re-initialized that request (resetting its `next`), and every chunk queued behind it was orphaned, the OSC's `ESC \` included. The rest of the chain is outside noctty. OpenConsole caches an unterminated OSC in its input parser and flushes it to the client as plain text as soon as an escape sequence it does not handle arrives, such as the kitty key-release `CSI 106;129:3u` that nvim 0.12's `CSI >3u` asks for. Neovim's TUI then sees `ESC ] 52 ; c ;` with no ST, and when 'ttimeoutlen' expires it types the whole thing as keys. The paste times out, the keys run as Normal-mode commands, and the first insert command in the base64 starts inserting the rest. Measured on 1.3.130 x64, bundled ConPTY, nvim 0.12.5 (`g:clipboard='osc52'`), default `clipboard-read = ask`, a 196,000-byte clipboard (a 64-chunk reply) and three key presses posted right behind the Allow click. nvim typed 16,373-16,425 keys and ended in Insert mode with base64 in the buffer, in all 3 runs. A console probe got exactly the first 28 chunks, then the keys, and no ST. Size decides it (CORRECTED by the OSC 52 matrix entry above: below about 96 KiB it still cannot trigger, but above that it is not a clean size threshold, and 199,956 bytes never leaked where 196,000 did). Clipboard text up to about 96 KiB fits the fresh 32-slot pool, so the pool never grows and it cannot trigger; 90 KB with 40 keys stayed intact. Above that, it takes 65-N writes queued behind an N-chunk reply before it drains: 120 KB (40 chunks) survived 3 keys and broke with 40, and by the model a 64-chunk reply breaks on the first write. Two OSC 52 queries in one burst for a 684,000-byte clipboard also broke it, 3 of 3 runs: they came back as ONE 842,375-byte reply with chunks 24-40 missing, and no second reply. The fix checks out a `std.heap.MemoryPool` entry per chunk, as upstream did in e0ef934f7, and every case above then arrives intact. Neovim's own half is real on its own: with 'nottimeout', a delivery gap of 60 ms or more, or WSL with 'ttimeoutlen=0', it types an intact reply too. But the bundled OpenConsole hands a terminated OSC to the client in one piece, and native nvim with default settings never did this up to 684 KB (0.10.4, 0.11.6, 0.12.5).
- 2026-09-23: Three harness traps from #261. (1) xev's IOCP `run(.no_wait)` never calls `GetQueuedCompletionStatusEx`, because `tick(0)` breaks before the wait, so a test that pumps `.no_wait` never sees a write complete; bound `.once` with a timer instead. (2) The test `win32 runtime can initialize config` appends an unfinished `init` attempt to the REAL `%LOCALAPPDATA%\noctty\startup-attempts.json` on every full `zig build test` run (measured: one entry per run). Three runs in a row put the user's next real launch into safe mode, which ignores `--config-file` and `-e` and starts `cmd.exe`, and that looks like a broken build. FIXED the same day: `localAppDataPathAlloc` returns null under `builtin.is_test` and `App.init` skips the PowerShell profile install there too (that install had also been overwriting the real profile copy of `integration.ps1` with the branch under test), and "win32 runtime leaves the real profile alone when a test starts it" pins both. If safe mode still appears after a test session, suspect a test that reaches the profile by some other route. Proving such a leak must not write the real profile again: redirect `LOCALAPPDATA` to a scratch directory for the run, and pin `ZIG_GLOBAL_CACHE_DIR` to the real Zig cache while doing it, because Zig's own global cache also lives under `LOCALAPPDATA` and a fresh scratch value fails the build with `failed to spawn ... uucode_build_tables.exe: FileNotFound`. (3) The in-box conhost never forwards an OSC 52 read query: no confirm prompt appears and nvim times out. So an OSC 52 paste report implies the bundled ConPTY, or a relay such as WSL or ssh. To see what a console app actually receives, run a small probe under `noctty -e` that sets `ENABLE_WINDOW_INPUT | ENABLE_VIRTUAL_TERMINAL_INPUT`, sends the query, and logs every key-down character. Drive the confirm prompt with a posted `BM_CLICK` to its Allow button, and send keys to the `noctty.win32` child as posted `WM_KEYDOWN`/`WM_KEYUP` pairs, which the pump's own `TranslateMessage` turns into characters, or as sent (`SendMessageTimeout`) `WM_KEYDOWN`/`WM_CHAR`/`WM_KEYUP` triples. A posted triple types each character twice, as it would in any Win32 program that translates its keys (see the 2026-10-10 dead-key entry). A bare posted `WM_CHAR` writes nothing to the pty.
- 2026-09-23: "Input on the wrong row after minimize/restore" (#262) was not the renderer and not #257. A/B on the release ZIPs reproduces it identically on 1.3.125, 1.3.129 and 1.3.130. The cause: a minimized host reports a 0x0 client rect, every `WM_SIZE` (including `SIZE_MINIMIZED`) ran `Host.layout` against it, `splitSurfacePlacement` clamped the pane to 1x1 px, and the core turned that into a 1x1 grid and a 1x1 `ResizePseudoConsole`. ConPTY keeps no scrollback, so at 1x1 it drops its whole buffer and homes its cursor, and the bundled OpenConsole emits zero bytes after either resize. The terminal reflows its own copy to one column and back and keeps its cursor on the prompt row, though at column 0. After restore the two disagree, and the shell's next output arrives as absolute CUPs (`ESC[1;1H`...) to the top rows. 1.3.124 (in-box conhost) collapses the same way but repaints, so it loses the prompt row instead of desynchronizing. `chrome_layout.hostLayoutClientRect` now keeps the last client rect with an area, and `Host.layout`, `Host.contentRect` and `syncTabButtons` all go through it. Two measuring techniques worth reusing. (1) To read what the pseudo console itself believes, run a helper process that calls `FreeConsole`, then `AttachConsole(<shell pid>)`, then `CreateFileW("CONOUT$")`, then `GetConsoleScreenBufferInfo` / `ReadConsoleOutputCharacterW`. It returns ConPTY's buffer size, cursor and text, which you can diff against the terminal's UIA `TextPattern`. Use `GetSelection()` to get the terminal caret. Run the helper `-WindowStyle Hidden` and have it write to a file. (2) `ShowWindow(SW_SHOWMINNOACTIVE)` then `ShowWindow(SW_SHOWNOACTIVATE)` minimizes and restores a test window without touching the foreground. `WM_COMMAND` 1904 on a minimized host restores it. Two things turned up on the way. (a) Any real resize while cmd or pwsh sat at a prompt erased the prompt row and left the cursor block alone on a blank line. `Terminal.flags.shell_redraws_prompt` defaults to `.true`, so `Screen.resize` clears from the last OSC 133;A down and waits for the shell to redraw it. Plain cmd never redraws it. PowerShell with PSReadLine re-renders only the typed input. ConPTY sends nothing. `--shell-integration=none` is the control that keeps the prompt. Both integrations now send `redraw=0` on the mark, and any Windows shell integration that cannot repaint its prompt on a resize must do the same. Two gaps remain. Clink 1.4+ can repaint the prompt after a resize, and that path has not been measured: Clink is not installed on the dev box, and the harness sandboxes `LOCALAPPDATA`, where noctty looks for it. The flag is also terminal-wide and sticky: a zsh, fish or elvish session nested inside a cmd/pwsh tab, which sends a bare 133;A, keeps `redraw=0` too. FIXED by #267: a bare A now resets the setting to the default unless a prompt is still open (`SemanticCommand.prompt_open`, closed only by a submitted line or a C mark), which keeps a theme's extra A inside a cmd or PowerShell prompt from switching clearing back on. (b) NOT fixed: the core grid starts one row short of the surface. Core `Surface.init` reads `getSize()` while the child is still 1258x684, the child reaches 1258x729 before `core_initialized` is set, and `syncCoreSizeFromClientRect` then sees no change, so 21 rows sit in a 22-row surface until the first real resize.
- 2026-09-21: A `zig build test -Dtest-filter=<one token>` run in this fork reports success while executing ZERO tests, and four places trusted it. (Fixed 2026-10-06: the entry above has the cause. What follows records the pre-fix tree, and its "cause as open" conclusion is superseded.) Measured on 5aa5cbd28: `-Dtest-filter=win32`, `-Dtest-filter=scroll`, `-Dtest-filter=keybind`, `-Dtest-filter=release`, `-Dtest-filter=sysChar` and `-Dtest-filter=Screen` each give `run test ghostty-test success 6ms MaxRSS:5M` with no count line at all, even though `grep '^test "' src/` finds 191 test decls containing "scroll" and 1 containing "sysChar". `-Dtest-filter=ConPTY` alone runs 79 (75 passed, 4 skipped). `-Dtest-filter=ConPTY -Dtest-filter=win32` runs 1501 (1496 passed, 5 skipped) in either order, while `-Dtest-filter=ConPTY -Dtest-filter=zzzznomatchzzz` runs 79 and `-Dtest-filter=scroll -Dtest-filter=zzzznomatchzzz` runs 0 -- so this is not simply "two filters disable filtering", and I have NOT established the mechanism; treat the numbers as the finding and the cause as open. The real suite is `zig build test -Demit-test-exe=true`: 4491 tests, 4418 passed, 73 skipped, about a minute at 380M RSS. The tell is the summary line: `Build Summary: 41/41 steps succeeded` counts BUILD steps and says nothing about tests, so a run with no `N/M tests passed` clause ran nothing, and 6ms at 5M RSS is the signature of an empty test binary. Always pass `--summary all` and read the count. Where it bit: the `Custom shader compiler tests` step in `test.yml` (`-Dtest-filter=shadertoy` with 4 matching decls, 0 run), all four commands in the `Run ARM64 Zig tests` step of `windows-arm64.yml` -- a job named "Native Windows ARM64 Tests" running on a real `windows-11-arm` runner that has never executed a unit test -- and the three Validation checkboxes every contributor is asked to tick in the PR template. What was NOT affected, and is worth knowing before anyone panics about past releases: `test.yml`'s `Full Zig test suite` step and `release.yml` both already used `-Demit-test-exe=true`, so the merge gate and the release gate always ran the whole suite. The ARM64 job's surviving real coverage is build + package + `noctty.com +version`, and `+version` never initializes a renderer or opens a window, which is exactly why an ARM64 startup failure of the OpenGL renderer (#255) could not have been caught there no matter how green that job looked. Local trap while checking the ARM64 side: `-Dtarget=aarch64-windows-msvc` on an x64 dev box dies with `failed to find libc installation: LibCStdLibHeaderNotFound` across 7 C/C++ dependency steps -- that is this machine lacking the ARM64 MSVC headers, NOT a repo defect, so ARM64 build changes have to be verified in CI and must not be reported as product breakage from a local run.
- 2026-09-19: A "beep on every Alt chord" report (#250) is not the terminal bell and no `bell-features` setting touches it: the surface proc handled `WM_CHAR` but not `WM_SYSCHAR`, and `TranslateMessage` turns the `WM_SYSKEYDOWN` of every Alt+<key> into a `WM_SYSCHAR` that fell to `DefWindowProc`, which posts `SC_KEYMENU`; a window with no menu bar then runs a menu loop that matches nothing (`WM_ENTERMENULOOP` / `WM_INITMENU` / `WM_MENUCHAR` / `WM_EXITMENULOOP` inside 2 ms, measured on a standalone probe window with the same parent-plus-focused-child shape) and plays the shell's default sound. The chord itself had already been encoded from the key message, so the terminal worked AND beeped, which is why the report says every chord. Measure it rather than listen for it: `SetWinEventHook(EVENT_SYSTEM_MENUSTART..MENUEND)` filtered to the target pid fires once per beep, and the `IAudioMeterInformation` peak on the process-less `AudioSrv` (System Sounds) session reads 0.346 for the sound and 0.0 otherwise. Posted `WM_SYSKEYDOWN` / `WM_SYSKEYUP` with the context bit (lParam bit 29) reproduce the beep without the foreground and without `SendInput`, but they do NOT reproduce the chord's encoding, because `GetKeyboardState` never sees a posted Alt: the key message reads as unmodified `w` and defers its text to a character message that, being a `WM_SYSCHAR`, is swallowed, so the child receives nothing. Do not route `WM_SYSCHAR` through the deferred-commit gate to "fix" that: a real chord never defers, so the only unit the gate can hold at that point was leaked by an earlier key, and committing it there types a bare character after the chord (adversarial review caught this before it shipped). To hold Alt without injecting input, `AttachThreadInput` to the UI thread and `SetKeyboardState` with `VK_MENU` set in the shared state; the chord then encodes exactly as it does from the keyboard. And `SendInput` goes to whatever window is in the foreground, which on a machine someone is using is not the window you launched: guard on `GetForegroundWindow()` or do not send. Alt+Space is the one `WM_SYSCHAR` still handed to `DefWindowProc`: Windows reserves it for the window menu, and that menu is how a keyboard-only user moves or resizes the window. The chrome controls (tab buttons, search field) and every Settings control are not subclassed for `WM_SYSCHAR`, so the top-level window under them -- the host, and separately the Settings window, which is its own top-level with no owner -- answers the phantom loop's `WM_MENUCHAR` (high word of wParam `MF_SYSMENU` without `MF_POPUP`, measured 0x2000) with `MNC_CLOSE`; a real popup (`MF_POPUP` set, measured 0x2010 for a key typed into the open window menu) keeps the default reply, and measured on Windows 11 that default neither closes the menu nor beeps for an unmatched key, so do not write "beeps like every Win32 menu" -- I did, and the measurement said otherwise. Fixing the host alone leaves Settings beeping: it is not a child of the host, so a host-side reply never sees its loop.
- 2026-09-19: A hidden tab button keeps its last rect. `layoutChromeForRect` hides a tab that scrolled out of `visibleTabRange` (or a whole strip turned off by config) with `ShowWindow` alone, so `GetWindowRect` on it still describes the slot some other tab now occupies, and anything that decides "is the pointer on this tab" from that rect -- the tooltip refresh in #251 did -- lands on the wrong tab. Gate on the strip's own `button_placement.visible` record plus `shouldShowTabBar()`, never on the rect. And refresh the tooltip whenever one is up, not only when its own tab's label was rebuilt: a tab that scrolls out because ANOTHER tab was activated keeps every label input and is never rebuilt (measured: 14 tabs in a 60-column window, hover tab 11, next-tab past the end wraps the range to tabs 1-4 and the popup for tab 11 stayed up over tab 1). Driving the tooltip without touching the user's pointer: `TrackMouseEvent(TME_HOVER)` fires only when `WindowFromPoint` at the real cursor IS the tracked window, so a window slid under a stationary cursor never hovers; post `WM_MOUSEMOVE` then `WM_MOUSEHOVER` to the button yourself. Do NOT keep re-posting `WM_MOUSEMOVE` as a "fidget": it re-arms `TME_LEAVE`, which fires at once whenever another window covers the button, so that variant measures occlusion by the agent's own windows, not the tooltip. `SendInput` goes to the foreground window, which on a machine someone is using is not the window you launched -- guard on `GetForegroundWindow()` or do not send -- and a sampled measurement must record whether the real cursor stayed inside the target rect and count only those samples.
