- 2026-09-15: Owner-drawn chrome ignores the OS focus-cue machinery. `WM_QUERYUISTATE` reads `UISF_HIDEFOCUS` (0x3, measured) on a fresh host and on a fresh Settings window and stays set until the dialog manager sees a Tab or arrow, but a `BS_OWNERDRAW` button gets `ODS_FOCUS` regardless, and every ring in this runtime was painted straight off `GetFocus()` -- so a clicked tab, the `[▾]` whose menu was just dismissed (the BUTTON class keeps focus after `TrackPopupMenu` and nothing handed it back, so the next keystroke went to the chevron, not the shell), and the section button that opens Settings all wore a ring nobody asked for. Track keyboard-versus-pointer mode yourself (`win32/focus_cue.zig`) and gate every ring on it; High Contrast keeps the ring on pointer focus, and a modal prompt's Allow / Cancel keep it regardless because focus lands there programmatically and the ring is the only mark of what Enter will press. Do NOT drop the `* ` active-tab marker while cleaning this up: it looks like a third cue next to the raised button and the accent underline, but `docs/accessibility-matrix.md` records that NVDA reads the BUTTON-class tab items through MSAA window text, never the UIA `SelectionItem`, so the asterisk is the only spoken cue for the active tab (and `test/windows/flagship/contracts/Contracts.20-Zig.ps1` pins the test that proves it). Two places where the navigation key never reaches the control it focuses: `IsDialogMessageW` consumes Tab and the arrows in the App pump BEFORE dispatch, so a subclass on the Settings rail sees the click that focused a button but never the key (feed the pump's `MSG` to the tracker ahead of `IsDialogMessageW`), and the `cycle_focus_region` keybind (F6 from the terminal) is a core action that calls `SetFocus` directly (flag keyboard mode at that entry point). Also measured on the same pass: `WS_EX_CLIENTEDGE` is a hard-coded 3-D bevel that `SetWindowTheme(DarkMode_Explorer)` never touches, which put a near-white 2 px frame on every dark Settings field while a themed `WS_BORDER` EDIT draws the combo boxes' 1 px frame; the EDIT class clips glyphs to its client rect, so a query child inset 6 px inside a 24 px overlay row was 12 px tall under a 19 px font cell and the palette had shown letters cut at the waist since April; and a 2 px accent strip on row 0 of an integrated-titlebar window lands directly under DWM's own 1 px accent border and reads as a blue rim around the whole window, not as a tab-strip accent.
- 2026-09-15: Triaging a "scrambled TUI" report against a ConPTY-hosted multiplexer (#243) needs a master-side recorder, and the recorder silently records nothing useful unless the child is created with `STARTF_USESTDHANDLES` and all three std handles NULL: when the driver's own std handles are redirected -- which they always are under an agent shell or a piped tool -- the child keeps the PARENT's handle values and writes past the pseudo console entirely, so the capture holds only conhost's attach/title/detach boilerplate (measured: 162 bytes, byte-identical for `cmd /k`, `cmd /d /c echo` and `powershell -Command`) and the run looks like "the child produced no output". Clearing `HANDLE_FLAG_INHERIT` on the parent's std handles and passing `bInheritHandles=FALSE` do NOT fix it; only the NULL-std-handle form does, and it works from a pipe-only parent, so it is the portable alternative to the `Start-Process -WindowStyle Hidden` workaround in the 2026-09-14 PowerShell entry below. Two findings that harness bought. (1) The in-box conhost (ConPTY v1) does not merely strip sequences, it reshapes repaints: for one scripted 30 s `herdr` scenario at 160x45 the bundled v2 stream carried 22803 CUP and zero CUF/ECH (the application's own absolute-CUP redraw, forwarded), while the in-box stream carried 6988 CUP plus 15138 CUF, 1933 ECH and 229 LF. Both shapes skip cells they believe are unchanged -- an application repaint skips the whitespace gaps between words -- so the difference is not "v1 skips and v2 does not" (I shipped that wrong once and review caught it); it is that every v2 run carries an absolute CUP that re-anchors the cursor, while v1's motion is relative and context-dependent (CUF clamps at the right margin, LF scrolls at the bottom row, ECH interacts with pending wrap), and that v1 skips at single-cell resolution INSIDE a word -- the capture literally contains `Modif` ESC[1C `ed` and `9/1` ESC[1C `/2026` -- so a divergence surfaces as one wrong letter, which is the shape "scrambled text" reports describe. A control child proves which stream is whose: its exact bytes come back verbatim under bundled v2 (only a `CSI 1t CSI c CSI ?1004h CSI ?9001h` prologue added) and come back as a synthesized top-to-bottom `ESC[K`+CR/LF redraw under v1, with overwritten text simply absent and the child's own CUF replaced by spaces -- so never read a bundled capture as conhost's output or an in-box capture as the application's. For any "stray leftover characters" report, diff `NOCTTY_CONPTY=inbox` against the default BEFORE suspecting the terminal -- but an inbox-only reproduction implicates the in-box path, not the transport as a whole, because the v1 shape reaches terminal code (CUF at the right margin, ECH against pending wrap, LF at the bottom row) that the bundled stream never exercises, so replay the capture through `libghostty-vt` to decide which side. Ask whether a resize preceded it: measured across three `ResizePseudoConsole` calls, the bundled path emitted no snapshot of its own (the next bytes were the application's own `CSI 2J`-led redraw) while the in-box path emitted its own `CSI ?25l CSI H` repaint from home with ECH for the blanks and NO ED -- overwrite-without-clear after an independent reflow is exactly where stale cells survive. Releases before 1.3.125 have no bundled pair and are always on the v1 shape. (2) `libghostty-vt` settles "is this the terminal core?" without a GUI repro: `zig build -Demit-lib-vt=true` produces `ghostty-vt.dll`, every portable release ZIP already ships one for its own version, and both can be driven from a few lines of `ctypes` -- replay the captured bytes through `ghostty_terminal_vt_write`, then diff `ghostty_formatter_format_buf` output and the `ghostty_render_state_*` per-row dirty flags across versions and chunk sizes. That exonerated both 1.3.124 and `main` on the real captured streams (identical screens, no chunk-boundary sensitivity, zero dirty-tracking violations) in minutes.

Two things made it look like a paint bug rather than a layout bug. (1) It is invisible on the FIRST tab only because of z-order: `EnumChildWindows` (topmost-first, measured live) puts tab 1's `noctty.win32` surface at z=0 above all of the chrome, while tab 2's surface lands at z=6 BELOW the chrome children at z=1..5, so the residue appears from the second tab onward and a one-tab repro reports success. (2) The parent-painted parts are already gated (`paintChromeTabBar` on `tab_h > 0`), so grepping the paint path finds nothing wrong; the residue is child-window paint, which `WS_CLIPCHILDREN` guarantees the parent can never erase. Gate child VISIBILITY on `shouldShowTabBar()`, not just child rects, and remember `focusableHwnd` / UIA both key off `IsWindowVisible`, so the hidden-but-alive buttons were also live F6 focus targets and live `TabItem` elements. Harness notes: `Get-Process ... MainWindowHandle` is still the wrong HWND (enumerate top-level windows for class `noctty.win32.host`), a foreground-free repro needs only `WM_COMMAND` 1904 / 1907 / 1908 (new tab / prev tab / next tab) plus 1901 for the palette, and `PrintWindow(PW_RENDERFULLCONTENT)` OVER-reports this bug because it renders occluded children too -- only a screen BitBlt and the child enumeration say what the user sees.
- 2026-09-14: Moving GDI-painted chrome text into a real child window on this host is a five-trap operation, all measured on the confirm overlay's title and body. (1) The host carries `WS_CLIPCHILDREN`, so the parent's band paint is clipped out of every visible child rect: the child must fill its own background with `themeSurface(theme, .overlay_panel_bg)` -- `overlay_brush` is the BAND colour and is 4-6 levels off the panel, which cut two visible rectangles out of the rounded panel -- and it must not cover the panel outline, which `gdi.drawRoundedRect` draws two device-pixel rows deep at every DPI (it insets its `RoundRect` by one pixel and strokes it with the 1px stock pen), so a child starting on the painted rect's first row erased the outline across its whole span with no way for the parent to draw it back. (2) An owner-drawn child's DC is clipped to its own client area even under a `WS_CLIPCHILDREN` parent -- `GetClipBox` inside `WM_DRAWITEM` returns exactly `(0,0,w,h)` -- so text the old paint path let overhang is LOST, not overdrawn. The overlay feedback line overhangs the panel by `scaled(2)+2` rows, and the confirm body's `p` / `g` / `q` descenders ended one row past the child at 96/144/168/192/288 DPI. Do not preserve the painted rect for its own sake once both renderers share a helper: give the body one rect that fits the panel (`chrome_layout.confirmBodyPaintRect`) and let the line move up two rows in both. (3) A plain `SS_CENTERIMAGE` STATIC does NOT reproduce `DT_VCENTER`: the STATIC rounds the half-leading UP where `DrawText` truncates, so the body moved one pixel whenever `height - tmHeight` is odd (it was, at 150% DPI) while the title, whose leading was even, matched exactly -- do not conclude from one line that the alignment is equivalent. `SS_OWNERDRAW` through the same `gdi.drawTextWz` call with the same flags is font-independent; re-deriving the position is not. (4) `overlayButtonUiaProviderForHwnd` had no caller, so the confirm's Allow / Cancel `SettingsControlProvider`s were created, kept and torn down but never returned from `WM_GETOBJECT` -- `AutomationElement.FindAll` on a live paste prompt reported both as ControlType Pane with `invoke_available=False`, and `test/windows/interactive-win11-hints.ps1 -Scenario UnsafePaste` fails on 700bd103b for exactly that reason. A provider that is constructed is not a provider that ships; verify it with a UIA dump, not with the constructor call site. (5) A non-live Text element is never spoken unless something focuses it, and a confirm puts focus on the accept button, so both prompt lines are `live_text` and both raise on open -- a plain text role for the heading would have left the tree correct and the user still uninformed.
- 2026-09-14: OSC 133 D in `integration.ps1` cannot decide an exit code from `$?` plus `$LASTEXITCODE` alone — two native commands failing with the SAME code look exactly like a cmdlet failing after a native one, so `cmd /c exit 5` twice marked `D;5` then `D;1`. The tiebreaker is the HEAD of `$Error` by reference identity (`[object]::ReferenceEquals`), re-snapshotted at the very END of `prompt`; never `$Error.Count`, which freezes at `$MaximumErrorCount` (256 on 5.1) and drops to 0 on a user's `$Error.Clear()` while the head keeps changing. Four measured traps. (1) `$Error[0]` on an EMPTY `$Error` throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` on both hosts under StrictMode 3.0+ — the hazard is the out-of-range ARRAYLIST INDEX, not an unset-variable read, so `Set-StrictMode -Off` quietly yields `$null` for the same expression and casual testing without StrictMode will not see it — and the throw is itself recorded in `$Error`, so swallowing it still leaves a record where the user had none; guard on `.Count -gt 0`. Relatedly, `$Error` does not hold only ErrorRecords and the two other shapes want opposite answers: a parse error typed at the prompt is a bare `ParseException` (a PowerShell-level failure, on BOTH hosts), while `$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'` pushes an `ActionPreferenceStopException` AHEAD of the record it stopped on — so with `$PSNativeCommandUseErrorActionPreference` also true the head hides a perfectly good `ProgramExitedWithNonZeroCode`. Unwrap one level through `.ErrorRecord`, which both inherit from `RuntimeException`, rather than rejecting non-ErrorRecords outright. Classify natives by `FullyQualifiedErrorId`, never by exception type: `RemoteException` is the 5.1 redirected-stderr record's exception but also every failure deserialized out of a job or remote session, so `Start-Job { throw 'x' } | Receive-Job` after a native failure re-reported the native code. (2) `-ErrorAction SilentlyContinue` hides an error from the console but still APPENDS it to `$Error`, so `__ghostty_read_global 'LASTEXITCODE'` was putting our own `VariableNotFound` record on top of the user's `$Error` on every draw; `-ErrorAction Ignore` does not, and is the right spelling for any suppressed read. (3) THE BIG ONE for testing: with stdin redirected to a pipe, PSReadLine's `[Microsoft.PowerShell.PSConsoleReadLine]::ReadLine` throws `NotSupportedException` and PowerShell records that `MethodInvocationException` in `$Error` ONCE PER PROMPT, landing precisely between the end-of-prompt snapshot and the user's next command — so the `-NoExit -Command` + piped-stdin rig in `test/windows/powershell-shell-integration.ps1` measures an artifact and reports the fix as broken when it is not. Either `Remove-Module PSReadLine -Force` in such a child (what the harness now does, and only for those cases) or measure through a real pseudo console; both were run on pwsh 7.6.6 and Windows PowerShell 5.1 and agree. If you build that ConPTY rig, the driver process must own a real console: launched from a tool whose stdout is a pipe, the child inherits the parent's redirected std handles instead of the pseudoconsole's and its output never reaches the PTY — run the driver through `Start-Process -WindowStyle Hidden` and have it write results to a file. (4) `@setEvalBranchQuota(1_000_000)` guarding the comptime SHA-256 of the embedded script in `src/apprt/win32_powershell_install.zig` is a build error waiting to happen: comptime SHA-256 costs roughly 50 backwards branches per input byte and the script crossed the limit at ~20 KiB. Derive the quota from `integration_script.len` rather than pinning a constant.
- 2026-09-14: `background-blur` cannot show anything on Win32 in this build, and the reason is NOT the layered window — correcting the 2026-04-16 `applyDwmThemeWithBuild` entry below, host windows no longer request `DWMSBT_TABBEDWINDOW` on build >= 22621; they always write `DWMSBT_NONE`. Nothing in this runtime can show a DWM backdrop material at all: the chrome is painted opaquely with GDI, the terminal is an opaque OpenGL child whose framebuffer alpha DWM discards, and nothing opts into `DwmExtendFrameIntoClientArea`, `DwmEnableBlurBehindWindow` or `WS_EX_NOREDIRECTIONBITMAP` (the accurate half of the #236 entry below). Measured on Windows 11 build 26200 over a finely striped desktop: `--background-opacity=0.8 --background-blur=true` read back `GWL_EXSTYLE=0x00080100`, `LWA_ALPHA` alpha 204 and backdrop 4, `--background-blur=false` read back backdrop 1, and the two 1500x1000 screenshots were byte-identical (same MD5) including the tab band; flipping the attribute from outside the process on the same window with `WS_EX_LAYERED` stripped by hand also changed nothing. The trap this entry exists for: that pair of null results is equally consistent with "layering suppresses the material" and with "this window can never show one", and the first reading is FALSE — a synthetic `WS_OVERLAPPEDWINDOW` with `DwmExtendFrameIntoClientArea(-1,-1,-1,-1)` and a black client shows a visibly different material per backdrop type both unlayered (`DWMSBT_NONE` #6872A3 vs `DWMSBT_TABBEDWINDOW` #E8E8E8) and layered at alpha 204 (#535B82 vs #BABABA, i.e. the same material composited and then tinted). A null result needs a POSITIVE CONTROL before it becomes a causal claim; without one this shipped a wrong mechanism into a code comment, two docs and a user-visible warning before review caught it. Three harness traps, each of which fakes a broken build: naming the argument array `$args` in a PowerShell probe silently passes NOTHING through `Start-Process -ArgumentList`, and the app then reads back `ex=0x100` with backdrop 1 exactly like the `Get-Process ... MainWindowHandle` trap does; a DPI-unaware probe gets virtualised `GetWindowRect` coordinates (a 1280-wide window reads as 853) and a rescaled `CopyFromScreen`, so call `SetProcessDpiAwarenessContext(-4)` before comparing pixels; and the GUI process's `log.warn` output is only capturable by redirecting the `.exe`'s stderr directly, because `noctty.com` re-launches the `.exe` with `stderr_behavior = .Ignore` and the redirect is lost.
- 2026-09-14: PowerShell shell integration hooked OSC 133 C on `Set-PSReadLineOption -CommandValidationHandler`, which PSReadLine invokes from exactly one place, its `ValidateAndAcceptLine` function — and the default Enter binding on both pwsh 7 and Windows PowerShell 5.1 is `AcceptLine`, so the mark was never emitted for anyone who had not rebound Enter, and it silently replaced any handler the user's profile had installed. Driven with `AttachConsole` + `WriteConsoleInputW` against real interactive sessions (piping stdin into `-NoExit` does NOT exercise PSReadLine at all, and `SendKeys` cannot reach a host launched under Windows Terminal because the spawned process owns no window), `AddToHistoryHandler` fires for every accepted line under the default `AcceptLine` on both hosts, ~8ms before the command runs. Four traps in replacing it. (1) The option is typed `Func[string, object]`, NOT `[scriptblock]`: PowerShell converts at parameter-bind time, so reading it back gives a delegate whose `ToString()` is the type name and whose `Target` is a compiler `Closure` — there is no script text to carry a marker, and "is this handler ours?" has to be reference equality against the delegate read back after installing. (2) The predecessor is NEVER null: PSReadLine 2.4.5 and 2.0.0 both ship a default handler that returns `MemoryOnly` for credential-shaped lines, so an unchained override starts writing the user's secrets to `ConsoleHost_history.txt`; chain it and return its value unchanged, and keep the handler's own output a scalar because a scriptblock-derived `Func` returns the block's whole output collection. (3) PSReadLine 2.0.0 ONLY (5.1) also calls the handler for every line it replays out of the shared history file — measured 1790 invocations before a single keypress — and for lines other live sessions append; gate the emission on `[Microsoft.PowerShell.PSConsoleReadLine]::GetBufferState` EQUALLING the line (`-ceq`), because a `cursor -gt 0` test still fires for a foreign line that arrives while the user has text in the buffer, and a prompt-armed one-shot is eaten by the replay burst, which happens after the first prompt draw. (4) `-ErrorAction SilentlyContinue` suppresses the message but STILL appends the record to `$Error`; only `-ErrorAction Ignore` leaves `$Error.Count` at 0, on both hosts and under StrictMode. That last one is also how the `ssh` block shipped an unconditional `Remove-Item -Path Function:\ssh,Function:\global:ssh` ahead of its own feature check: it deleted a profile-defined `ssh` on every launch even when no wrapper replaced it, and `Function:\global:ssh` is not a valid provider path (scope qualifiers belong on the name, not in a `Function:` drive path), so every session also opened with two bogus `ItemNotFoundException` records. Unrelated but adjacent: growing this `.ps1` by ~4 KiB blew `@setEvalBranchQuota(1_000_000)` on the comptime `Sha256` of the embedded script, and the error points into `std/crypto/sha2.zig` with no hint that a PowerShell comment was the cause — the quota scales with script length.
- 2026-09-14: `buildInjectedArgv` launches PowerShell with `-NoExit -Command "& { $__ghostty_utf8_console = $false; . '<path>' }"`, and `& { ... }` is a CHILD SCOPE that is destroyed the instant the dot-source returns. `integration.ps1` qualified only `global:prompt` and `global:ssh`, so the wrapper prompt survived while all ten `__ghostty_*` helpers and the `$ESC` / `$BEL` variables died with the block. Every prompt draw then threw `CommandNotFoundException`, PowerShell silently substituted its built-in prompt, and the symptom was the pair `PS C:\...>` replacing the user's starship / oh-my-posh prompt AND zero OSC 133 / OSC 7 output for every PowerShell user (#231). Do not "fix" this by dropping the block and dot-sourcing at global scope as the issue proposed: the block is load-bearing. Measured on pwsh 7 and Windows PowerShell 5.1 — a profile that declares `$__ghostty_utf8_console` `-Option ReadOnly` or `Constant` cannot be overwritten by a global assignment (non-terminating `Cannot overwrite variable ...`, the dot-source proceeds, the profile's value wins, codepage ends at 65001 with visible errors) whereas a child scope SHADOWS it cleanly (codepage stays 437 when `$false` is asked for; the one spelling that still defeats the block is `-Option AllScope,ReadOnly`, which makes the name unbindable in child scopes too). The doc comment justifying the block as preventing child-process inheritance was post-hoc and wrong; PowerShell variables are never inherited by child processes. Fix the script, not the argv: `global:`-qualify every top-level name that must outlive load. The reason this shipped is that `test/windows/powershell-shell-integration.ps1` dot-sourced the script at its OWN scope, where unqualified definitions survive, and its prompt fixture was literally `'PS> '` — the failure-mode string — so it could not distinguish "prompt preserved" from "prompt lost". A test for scope loss must spawn a real child process with the real argv and use a distinctive marker prompt.
- 2026-09-14: Two chrome-cache traps, one per review follow-up. (1) `buildTabButtonLabel` compacted the BARE title to the button's whole cell budget and only then prepended `"* "` / `"N: "` and appended `" (N)"`, so the drawn label ran up to nine cells past the button, and the tooltip gate -- which measured that DECORATED label against the same budget -- fired on the active tab while its inactive neighbour showing the same title stayed silent. Charge the decorations to the budget before compacting (`labels.tabButtonTitleBudget`), and once the label is built to fit, the gate has to switch to the BARE title against that title budget: keeping `label width > max_width` then asks whether a label constructed to fit fits, and the answer is always no-tooltip. Measured live with three cmd.exe tabs: `1: C:\WINDOWS\system3...` and `* 3: C:\WINDOWS\syste...` are now both exactly 24 cells, and a tab retitled `ab` shows no tooltip while its neighbours do. (2) Do not gate a cache invalidation on a sync helper's changed-return when other call sites run the same sync and drop the result. `showOverlay` invalidated the chrome paint-text cache only `if (text_changed)` from `syncOverlayLabel` / `syncOverlayHint`, but `stepTabOverviewSelection`, `syncSearchOverlay` and the plain `EN_CHANGE` arm sync without invalidating, so the control text can advance past the paint cache and the next open reports "unchanged" and paints the PREVIOUS overlay's strings. Invalidate unconditionally on a once-per-gesture path such as open, and place it after `rebuildPaletteList`, which mutates the palette state the painted label reads.
- 2026-09-14: `background-opacity` had no effect on the very first window of a session. On Win32 the host HWND's layered alpha (`WS_EX_LAYERED` + `SetLayeredWindowAttributes(..., LWA_ALPHA)` in `Surface.applyBackgroundOpacity`) is the *only* mechanism that makes the setting visible — DWM discards the GL child's framebuffer alpha and nothing in `src/` opts into `DwmExtendFrameIntoClientArea`, `DwmEnableBlurBehindWindow` or `WS_EX_NOREDIRECTIONBITMAP` — yet `applyBackgroundOpacity` had only three call sites: `applyRuntimeConfig` (config reload), `syncSharedHostWindowState` (tab/window activation) and `inheritWindowStateFrom` (the `new_window` clone path). `Surface.init` stored `self.background_opacity_default` from config but never applied it and `createHost` passes `dwExStyle = 0`, so window 1 stayed opaque while window 2 faded, and any later config reload made window 1 fade too. Two traps: the obvious-looking patch (drop `LWA_ALPHA` and push opacity to the renderer instead) removes Win32 transparency altogether, because `changeConfig` already updates the renderer's `background_opacity` and it still does not survive DWM; and when verifying this by hand, `Get-Process noctty | % MainWindowHandle` returns the wrong HWND (it can latch onto the NVIDIA `NVOpenGLPbuffer` helper window), which reads back `ex=0x100` and makes a working fix look broken — enumerate top-level windows and match the `noctty.win32.host` class instead.
- 2026-09-14: A Win32 multiline EDIT breaks a line only on CRLF. Measured with `EM_GETLINECOUNT` / `EM_POSFROMCHAR` on a read-only `ES_MULTILINE` control: `a\nb` and `a\rb` report one line with `b` at the same x as in `ab`, so a lone LF is zero-width and a lone CR is swallowed; only `a\r\nb` reports two lines. Any text shown through an EDIT that must preserve line structure (the confirm preview pane, where the newline is exactly what `input.paste.isSafe` raised the prompt for) has to normalise line endings to CRLF first. Do not take an upstream GTK/macOS text-view behaviour as given for an EDIT; measure it.
- 2026-09-10: The terminal Surface is the TOPMOST child window, not the bottom one. Measured with `EnumChildWindows` (which enumerates topmost-first) while a confirm was open: `noctty.win32` at index 0, the overlay controls from index 6 down. `surfaceWindowStyle()` (`win32.zig:514-517`) does carry `WS_CLIPSIBLINGS`, but that only spares siblings ABOVE the Surface, so any chrome child that overlaps the terminal rect is repainted over on the next present and reads as a flicker. The band's own controls escape this only because their rects never intersect the Surface. A child that deliberately overlaps the terminal has to be raised on the transition to visible: `SetWindowPos(hwnd, null /* HWND_TOP */, 0, 0, 0, 0, SWP_NOMOVE | SWP_NOSIZE | SWP_NOACTIVATE)`. Do not assume lazily-created controls end up on top just because they were created later — they did not here. The 2026-08 tooltip entry concluded `WS_POPUP` was required for the same symptom; that was a different cause, so measure the z-order before reaching for a top-level window.
- 2026-09-10: The confirm overlay never rendered its own title or body. Chrome paint hardcoded `"Confirm"` and `""` for `.confirm` (`labels.buildOverlayPaintLabelText` / `buildOverlayHintText`), and the two child STATICs that carry `confirm_payload.title` / `.body` are never laid out: no `applyChromeChildRect` / `applyChildRect` call site covers `overlay_label_hwnd` or `overlay_hint_hwnd`, `showOverlay` force-hides the label on every open and nothing shows it again, and the hint keeps its 100x18 creation rect at client (0,0). All three prompts (close-surface, clipboard paste, clipboard write) therefore looked identical, so users approved a confirm without being told what it was for. Chrome paint now takes `labels.ConfirmText` from the payload. Two traps worth keeping: the comments on those `.confirm` switch arms claimed the payload was read "at paint time" when no paint path referenced it — treat such comments as unverified; and `syncOverlayLabel` / `syncOverlayHint` must keep writing the text even though both controls are invisible, because `refreshChrome` uses their changed-return value as the trigger that marks the paint text cache dirty.
- 2026-08-20: Correcting the 2026-04-16 palette entry below: `commandPaletteUniqueMatch` no longer powers Enter-to-run, and it has now been deleted. Provenance, since the pieces landed separately: `246e429b7` replaced the `submitOverlay` unique-match resolution with dispatch of the selected ranked row plus action MRU; `dc300a40dc` (#73) added the tab / pane / profile payload arms to `invokePaletteRow`; `3f0a928c5` (#93) added the `palette_list_visible_rows > 0` gate with its `Make the window larger to show and activate palette results.` refusal and removed the last two callers (`buildOverlayAcceptLabel` and `buildCommandPaletteOverlayLabel`); `41061f8c8` deleted `commandPaletteMatchCount` outright and relocated the by-then-orphaned unique helper, and that `commandPaletteMatchCount` absence still holds. In the current runtime Enter invokes `invokePaletteRow(@min(palette_list_selected, palette_list_ranked_count - 1))` only when there are ranked matches AND at least one visible row, and `rebuildPaletteList` resets `palette_list_selected = 0` on every keystroke, so a single-match query with a visible row runs the top-ranked entry; with ranked matches but zero visible rows Enter deliberately refuses rather than activating a selection the user cannot see; parsing the raw query text is only the `palette_list_ranked_count == 0` fallback. Do not resurrect either helper on the strength of the stale 2026-04-16 note, and when a log entry names a function as load-bearing, re-verify the call sites before treating it as live.
- 2026-05-02: Lowercasing Zig type aliases to satisfy naming review can collide with same-named declarations or locals in Zig's shared declaration namespace; check for helper/local name conflicts before committing mechanical renames.
- 2026-05-01: In this fork, with both `origin` and `upstream` remotes configured, bare `gh pr view <n>` can resolve the upstream Ghostty PR number; use `-R amanthanvi/noctty` for fork-local PR review/checks.
- 2026-05-01: When invoking `scripts/dev-windows.cmd zig build test -Dtest-filter=...`, keep the filter value to one token (quote it or use a no-space substring) or `zig build` treats trailing words as step names.
- 2026-05-01: In Zig Win32 FFI, `std.os.windows.HANDLE` is non-null; APIs like `CreateJobObjectW` that signal failure with `NULL` must use `?HANDLE` in extern declarations or future callers cannot model failure correctly.
- 2026-05-01: On Win32 x64, `JOBOBJECT_BASIC_LIMIT_INFORMATION` is 64 bytes, not 56; `SIZE_T`/`Affinity` padding also shifts `JOBOBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION` to 144 bytes with `JobMemoryLimit` at offset 120. Keep ABI tests on real offsets before wiring Job Object FFI.
- 2026-05-01: In this repo's worktrees, plain `gh repo` / `gh pr` commands can resolve to upstream `ghostty-org/ghostty` despite `origin=amanthanvi/noctty`; pin review ops with `--repo amanthanvi/noctty`.
- 2026-05-01: Direct `zig test src/apprt.zig` bypasses the repo build graph and fails on missing generated `build_options`; verify `apprt` package changes with `zig build test -Dtest-filter=...` instead.
- 2026-05-01: In this fork, plain `gh pr view <n>` can resolve the upstream Ghostty repo instead of `amanthanvi/noctty`; pass `--repo amanthanvi/noctty` or verify the target before trusting PR metadata.
- 2026-05-01: In Zig `union(enum)` tests, `Type.tag` names the tag enum, not a union value; cast to `Type` before calling union methods in assertions.
- 2026-05-01: The Windows release workflow's explicit `zig build` and `scripts/package-windows.ps1` fallback build must both pass `-Doptimize=ReleaseFast`; otherwise a successful release can publish `.Debug` binaries even though tests and packaging pass.
- 2026-05-01: A stale queued GitHub Actions run can get stuck in a backend state where `gh run cancel` returns HTTP 500 and `DELETE /actions/runs/{id}` returns HTTP 403; clean failed/cancelled runs normally, but treat that survivor as GitHub-side cleanup debt rather than a script bug.
- 2026-05-01: WinGet release CI must skip `wingetcreate update --submit` when `WINGET_PACKAGE_IDENTIFIER` is not yet bootstrapped in `microsoft/winget-pkgs`; a greenfield fork with no existing manifest path will otherwise fail deployments after release, Scoop, and packaging already succeeded.
- 2026-05-01: In the Windows release WinGet submit path, `Add-AppxPackage` for VCLibs / `wingetcreate` must treat HRESULT `0x80073D06` (“higher version already installed”) as success on hosted runners; otherwise WinGet bootstrap fails after the release and Scoop paths already succeeded.
- 2026-05-01: In GitHub Actions release publish steps, `gh repo clone` of another repo is not enough for a later plain `git push`; wire Git auth explicitly (for example tokenized `origin` URL or `gh auth setup-git`) or the push dies with non-interactive username prompt errors.
- 2026-05-01: In GitHub Windows self-signed release packaging, do not block first-step diagnostics behind runner trust-store import; emit phase markers before signing setup, validate self-signed Authenticode by expected signer thumbprint, and run release scripts directly under `pwsh` instead of nesting `powershell.exe`.
- 2026-05-01: In GitHub Actions PowerShell steps, pass dotted Zig semver build args like `-Dversion-string=1.3.108` as one quoted token (`"-Dversion-string=..."`); unquoted inline semver can be split into `1` plus `.3.108` and trips `std.SemanticVersion.parse` with `InvalidVersion`.
- 2026-05-01: In the GitHub Windows release workflow, do not hide a full release `zig build` inside the opaque `Package Windows artifacts` step. Build explicitly first, then run `scripts/package-windows.ps1 -SkipBuild`, and put timeouts on both steps so a stuck rebuild/sign/package path fails fast instead of burning hours with no live logs.
- 2026-04-30: In self-signed Windows release-signing mode, skip Authenticode timestamping. Public timestamping buys nothing for internal certs and can wedge the GitHub Actions `Package Windows artifacts` step for hours while the local no-timestamp path still signs and verifies quickly.
- 2026-04-30: Self-signed Authenticode on Windows signs successfully but `Get-AuthenticodeSignature` returns untrusted-root `UnknownError` until that cert is also trusted in `CurrentUser\Root` and `TrustedPublisher`; internal CI/release probes must either import trust first or treat self-signed validation as a distinct path.
- 2026-04-30: `gh pr checks --watch` can stay stale at `pending 0` on this repo even while Actions job steps are actively running; verify merge gates with `gh api repos/.../actions/jobs/...` before treating the checks as stuck.
- 2026-04-29: Repeated Win11 harness runs can leave `zig-out\bin\noctty.exe` and `noctty.com` locked by external service `vgc`; confirm the Restart Manager owner before treating install-step `NTSTATUS=0xc0000043` as a code regression.
- 2026-04-29: In `src/renderer/Thread.zig`, do not arm the Win32 render follow-up timer from both the wakeup path and the follow-up callback path; double-submitting `render_c` trips `invalid state in submission queue state=.active` and destabilizes streaming redraws.
- 2026-04-29: On Win32, inactive same-host tabs created by `new_tab` must not propagate shared host-window state during init. Their replayed `toggle_window_decorations` / `float_window` / `size_limit`-style actions can otherwise call `inheritWindowStateFrom(...)` on the active maximized tab and silently drop the host into logical restore state while the outer rect still looks maximized. Interactive harnesses for this path should fail on transient maximize-state drift, not just the final restored rect.
- 2026-04-29: In `interactive-win11-key-input.ps1`, GUI-thread focus probes are diagnostics only. `GetWindowThreadProcessId` / `GetGUIThreadInfo` can race a dead HWND during Win11 automation; treat that as `<unavailable>` focus metadata, not as a failed key-input validation when the child process already read the expected key.
- 2026-04-29: On Win32, renderer follow-up pacing for streaming terminal output must key off actual renderer-wakeup notify traffic, not async wake callback cadence; `xev.Async` can coalesce steady ConPTY output heavily, so callback-timestamp bursts still drop animated TUI frames even when PTY output is healthy.
- 2026-04-29: Foreground-sensitive Win11 perf harnesses should run directly in the suite process, not under nested `Start-Process -PassThru` wrappers; the extra process layer can lose foreground rights and understate real app paint cadence.
- 2026-04-29: (Superseded 2026-10-10: since #8 these frames queue except during resize settle, and synchronous renderer paints inside the mailbox drain starved the UI thread; see the 2026-10-10 entry.) On Win32, renderer-driven `.render` actions must use synchronous child-surface `update_now` paints outside live resize; plain queued invalidation lets the message pump coalesce steady TUI output and visibly drops animation frames even when termio and renderer-thread cadence look healthy.
- 2026-04-29: Interactive Win11 harnesses must not gate success on `std.log.debug(...)` strings (`command took ...`, `taskbar progress sync ...`); ReleaseFast runs at `.info`, so validation must key off real process lifetime, explicit failure logs, or artifact outputs instead.
- 2026-04-29: On Win32, renderer `xev.Async` wakes can coalesce badly under steady ConPTY/TUI output; fixing only the child emitter is not enough. Keep a short renderer follow-up timer burst while recent output is active, or animation-heavy TUIs collapse to a fraction of their intended frame rate.
- 2026-04-28: On Win32 first-window startup, a new hosted surface shown only through `Surface.init` can leave keyboard focus on `noctty.win32.host`; new-host init must run the same surface-present/focus path as later activations unless the launch is explicitly passive (`SW_SHOWNOACTIVATE` quick-terminal case).
- 2026-04-28: In Win32 terminal repaint scheduling, ending `synchronized_output` (`2026l`) must force a renderer wake if visible terminal dirtiness was already pending under `2026h`; otherwise animations can look sticky until unrelated later output or input triggers another repaint.
- 2026-04-28: Win32 `vxfw`/`Sleep`-driven CLI TUIs can miss every other intended 30 fps frame when they schedule the next tick at ~33 ms; on this stack `Sleep(16)` often lands near ~31 ms and `Sleep(33)` near ~47 ms, so prefer clock-synced frame selection plus sub-frame polling over exact-33 ms self-rescheduling.
- 2026-04-28: Windows portable/installer packaging must stage the full `zig-out\share` tree, not only `share\ghostty`; otherwise `resourcesDir()` cannot find the packaged `share\terminfo\ghostty.terminfo` sentinel and packaged `+list-themes` fails.
- 2026-04-28: In `src/cli/list_themes.zig`, buffered stderr writes on early `return 1` paths must flush before returning; otherwise missing-resource / no-theme failures can look like silent exits in shells.
- 2026-04-28: `scripts/package-windows.ps1` package smoke should use a plain three-part numeric semver such as `0.0.1`; suffixed labels like `0.0.0-dev` fail Inno `VersionInfoVersion`, while four-part forms like `0.0.0.1` fail Zig `std.SemanticVersion.parse`.
- 2026-04-28: When `ISCC.exe` is present, `scripts/package-windows.ps1` must treat a nonzero Inno exit code as fatal; otherwise installer compilation can fail while the script still leaves a misleading portable artifact behind.
- 2026-04-28: `scripts/package-windows.ps1 -SkipBuild` still requires `zig-out\bin\ghostty-vt.dll`; a prior `zig build -Demit-exe=true` is not enough for portable/installer smoke. Use the package script's own build step or emit `libghostty-vt` first.
- 2026-04-28: After adding another bootstrapped Win11 harness to `interactive-win11-validate.ps1`, the parallel-stage suite deadline (`max timeout + 10s`) became marginal even though `interactive-win11-progress.ps1` still passed standalone; keep a wider suite-level buffer than the individual harness timeout.
- 2026-04-28: For Win32 `noctty.exe -e cmd.exe /c ...` app-level validation, keep `+action` invocations inside payload `.cmd` / `.ps1` files instead of inline `Start-Process -ArgumentList` command strings; nested quoting/argv translation is brittle enough to silently drop the intended CLI action.
- 2026-04-28: In Win32 child shells, appending the current `zig-out\bin` / install dir to `PATH` is not enough; an older `noctty.exe` shim earlier on `PATH` still wins in interactive `cmd` / PowerShell and reproduces the silent no-op. Child shells must see the current app dir first.
- 2026-04-28: On Windows shell lookup, bare `noctty` resolves `noctty.com` before `noctty.exe`; a console-subsystem `.com` shim can fix terminal CLI behavior without changing Explorer/shortcut launches that still target `noctty.exe`.
- 2026-04-28: After detached GUI-subsystem validation, a leftover `zig-out\bin\noctty.exe` process can lock the install target and make `zig build -Demit-exe=true` fail with `AccessDenied`; check for stale `noctty.exe` before treating the rebuild as a real compile failure.
- 2026-04-28: `src/cli/ghostty.zig` action-help dispatch must not collapse help-text stdout write failures into `return 1`; detached GUI-subsystem launches then exit silently before `main_ghostty.zig` can surface a visible error.
- 2026-04-28: In PowerShell, invoking GUI-subsystem `noctty.exe` with `&` returns before the child exits even with `1>`/`2>` redirection; redirected CLI validation must use `Start-Process -PassThru` plus `WaitForExit()`.
- 2026-04-28: Detached Win32 `+list-themes` validation against a source-built `zig-out\bin\noctty.exe` can fail before the console-handle path because `zig-out\share\ghostty\themes` may be absent; point harnesses at a packaged `share\ghostty` tree via `GHOSTTY_RESOURCES_DIR` when validating no-console behavior.
- 2026-04-29: On this machine, confirm whether a reported Win11 CLI-tab regression is coming from the current repo build or the stale Scoop install (`C:\Users\amant\scoop\apps\noctty\1.3.106`); current packaged probes can pass while the older installed app still reproduces old `noctty +...` tab-freeze behavior.
- 2026-04-29: Win11 screenshot harnesses that use `CopyFromScreen` must force the target host topmost/foreground immediately before capture; otherwise Codex/desktop windows can obscure the rect and yield false no-repaint failures against unrelated pixels.
- 2026-04-25: GitHub Actions restore path rejects `allowed_actions` when `enabled=false`; if you temporarily fence a repo with `allowed_actions=local_only`, restore by disabling Actions with only `enabled` + `sha_pinning_required`, not the full prior body.
- 2026-04-25: Defender `Trojan:Win32/Wacatac.C!ml` on noctty releases can target the unsigned Inno setup wrapper while the unpacked `noctty.exe` still scans clean. Validate installer and raw exe separately before touching runtime code, and fail release packaging closed if Authenticode signing is absent.
- 2026-04-24: `wingetcreate new` still prompts for required fields such as `PackageIdentifier` and crashes in non-interactive shells when they are missing; keep WinGet CI on the truthful `update --submit` path until bootstrap can be supplied non-interactively.
- 2026-04-24: noctty release tags should stay on plain semver with `major.minor` tracking the Ghostty upstream line and `patch` owned by the fork (`1.3.100`, `1.3.101`, ...); keep the exact upstream base in `dist/windows/release-metadata.json` instead of leaking it into the public tag.
- 2026-04-24: Installing `wingetcreate` from the MSIX bundle on a clean Windows runner also requires the `Microsoft.VCLibs.x64.14.00.Desktop.appx` framework package first; skipping that dependency makes the WinGet publish path flaky.
- 2026-04-24: Win32 host-structural and per-surface local undo entries can share the same `GetTickCount64()` millisecond; cross-stack undo/redo selection needs a monotonic sequence tie-breaker or same-tick actions replay out of LIFO order.
- 2026-04-24: In Win32 local undo capture, once terminal snapshot bytes are wrapped into a `win32_undo.Entry`, do not keep a second outer `errdefer free(state_bytes)` guard; a later push OOM will run `entry.deinit()` and double-free the same snapshot buffer.
- 2026-04-24: If a Win32 local destructive action (`reset` / `clear_screen`) proceeds after best-effort undo snapshot capture fails, it must still clear that surface's local redo plus host structural redo; otherwise stale redo survives onto the wrong branch.
- 2026-04-24: Win32 empty-host prune must not destroy a zero-tab Host just because `destroy_after_structural_dispose` is set; if structural undo/redo still exists, the Host must stay alive so last-tab undo remains reachable.
- 2026-04-24: Win32 local terminal undo/redo replay cannot blindly roll the stack move back on generic replay errors; terminal restore/reset mutates before title/pwd/cache/repaint helpers can allocate, so replay-side UI sync must be best-effort and the stack move remains authoritative.
- 2026-04-23: Win32 structural undo/redo must roll a popped entry back to its source stack if replay fails before mutation (for example OOM while restoring a detached tab or re-splitting a pane); otherwise the entry moves to the opposite branch without the UI state changing.
- 2026-04-23: Win32 local redo must preflight replay and roll back a moved redo entry if replay is blocked before mutation; `clear_screen` redo in alternate-screen state is unavailable, not a reason to consume redo history.
- 2026-04-23: Do not paper over Win32 live-resize artifacts by suppressing renderer-origin repaint requests and GDI-filling the child HWND after `WM_SIZE`; that replaces black bands with stale gray/background boxes. Stable resize depends on the renderer-present path remaining active while child size changes are coalesced through the existing live-resize throttle/exit flush.
- 2026-04-23: Win32 structural redo for `new_split` must store the original `SplitTreeSurface.Split.Direction`; replaying every split as `.right` turns `new_split:down/up/left` into a different layout and violates undo/redo replay semantics.
- 2026-04-23: Win32 undo/redo actions must prune through the App-level undo expiry path before selecting a surface/host entry. Per-host pruning can expire an empty-host close-tab entry without running the host-removal/quit-timer path.
- 2026-04-23: App-wide Win32 host destruction must clear structural undo/redo with normal guarded disposal before destroying the host HWND. Otherwise child HWND teardown can reenter `windowDestroyed` while detached close-tab/new-split undo entries still reference hidden surfaces.
- 2026-04-23: Win32 structural undo entries that detach live surfaces must have an App-level prune timer. Pruning only on the next undo/redo/action lets hidden close-tab/new-split surfaces keep PTYs alive indefinitely past `undo-timeout`.
- 2026-04-23: Zig's Win32 resource cache can stay stale when only `dist/windows/noctty.ico` changes. Feed an icon-content hash flag into resource compilation before trusting Explorer/taskbar icon validation.
- 2026-04-22: A PE icon resource alone does not guarantee Win32 runtime/taskbar icon identity. Top-level window classes must register `hIcon`/`hIconSm`, windows should receive `WM_SETICON`, and the AUMID registry `IconUri` must name an image file next to the current exe (`noctty.ico`; the notification platform never extracts an icon from an exe path, and it caches its first resolution per AUMID for the session) or Explorer/Action Center can keep showing stale or missing shell icons.
- 2026-04-22: Win32 integrated-titlebar min/max buttons must dispatch `WM_SYSCOMMAND` from `WM_NCLBUTTONDOWN` and swallow the matching up; after `WM_NCCALCSIZE` removes the top NC margin, mouse-up translation can require a second click. Avoid UxTheme `WINDOW` caption-button painting in custom chrome because it produces legacy glyphs.
- 2026-04-22: Interactive Win11 composite validation must perform one upfront build whenever tracked inputs are newer than `zig-out\bin\noctty.exe`, not only for explicit `-Rebuild`; otherwise parallel child harnesses can race rebuilding the same Zig cache.
- 2026-04-22: PowerShell `Remove-Item -Recurse` and even .NET recursive delete can fail on stale Zig package-cache sentinel paths during Win11 sandbox reset. Keep the sandbox path guard, then quarantine stubborn sandboxes out of the active path and best-effort delete them.
- 2026-04-22: Packaged Win32 toast identity needs the installer-created shortcuts to set the same `AppUserModelID` as runtime (`io.github.amanthanvi.noctty`). Runtime `SetCurrentProcessExplicitAppUserModelID` and registry metadata are not enough for packaged shortcut identity; keep `dist/windows/noctty.iss` `[Icons]` entries in sync.
- 2026-04-22: Zig sentinel slices exclude the trailing sentinel from `.len`; never pass `len - 1` to Win32 `TextOutW` / `DrawTextW` for `[:0]u16` UI caches. Empty overlay strings then underflow in Safe builds, and non-empty strings lose their final character.
- 2026-04-21: Interactive Win11 bootstrap helpers must not `exit (Invoke-...)` around child harness runs; PowerShell captures child stdout in expression position and hides the PASS sentinel used by the composite validator. Report exit code through an out/ref parameter so stdout streams normally.
- 2026-04-20: Publish branches and PRs for this fork against `origin` / `amanthanvi/noctty`, never `upstream` / `ghostty-org/ghostty`. Verify the target repo explicitly before `git push` and `gh pr create`.
- 2026-04-19: Win32 split focus must update the tab's focused split handle from the surface focus path, not only `core_app.focusSurface()`. Otherwise click/refocus changes terminal input focus but later split actions still target the stale active pane.
- 2026-04-19: Win32 native child HWND repaint cannot be tied to hosted-surface repaint. During live resize, WGL surface paints can defer, but docked search child controls must still invalidate; overlay/palette child controls also need explicit invalidation because `WS_CLIPCHILDREN` prevents host paints from repainting their pixels.
- 2026-04-19: In Win32 `paintChrome()`, `BeginPaint.rcPaint` is only a bounding rect for the update region, not exact proof that every chrome band needs work. Guard band painting with actual clip visibility (`RectVisible`) after a coarse intersection check, or top+status invalidations will still drag the content/search path through a false full-window paint.
- 2026-04-19: In the host-painted Win32 content lane, repainting only the new search-frame/divider chrome is not enough after resize/tab/split geometry changes. Clear the exposed content band first or stale frame/group pixels linger until a later full repaint.
- 2026-04-19: In `Host.layout()`, top chrome layout deltas and content/pane deltas must not share one child-paint invalidation path. Otherwise pane-only resizes keep repainting every tab/new-tab/overflow HWND, and top-only layout churn keeps repainting surface/search children.
- 2026-04-19: Once Host chrome invalidation is queued by region, layout and live-resize completion must stop calling raw full-window `InvalidateRect(..., null, 0)` when the repaint scope is known; that bypasses the queue and defeats coalescing.
- 2026-04-19: Host-side Win32 repaint coalescing needs a scope mask, not just a `paint_pending` boolean. A top-only pending paint can be followed by content/status invalidations before `WM_PAINT`; dropping those later scopes loses repaint coverage.
- 2026-04-19: After splitting Win32 chrome text dirtiness by zone, obvious callers must stop using `invalidateTopChromeText()` for banner-only or overlay-only changes; otherwise the overlay/banner caches still rebuild each other and the split buys little.
- 2026-04-19: Narrower Win32 repaint regions are not enough by themselves; if `chrome_text_dirty` stays a single shared bit, banner / inspector / overlay caches still rebuild each other. Split cache dirtiness by zone when splitting chrome repaint scope.
- 2026-04-19: If Win32 top chrome uses per-zone text dirtiness, do not reintroduce a single `chrome_text_dirty` clear at the `statusBarHeight() == 0` early return. Clear each zone when its own cache rebuild runs; a shared clear just recreates cross-zone coupling.
- 2026-04-19: Explicit banners, update notices, and window-title changes only affect the top Win32 chrome. Routing them through full `invalidateChromeText()` repaints bottom/status regions for no visible benefit; use a top-only chrome-text invalidation path instead.
- 2026-04-19: Inspector toggles cannot rely on an explicit-banner change to invalidate chrome. Turning inspector on with no explicit banner leaves `setBanner(.none, null)` as a no-op, but the derived inspector banner still needs `invalidateChromeText()`. Fix the source path in `setInspectorVisible()` before deleting caller-side `refreshChrome()`.
- 2026-04-19: Win32 inspector visibility changes alter `contentRect().top`; `setInspectorVisible()` must run `Host.layout()` after invalidating chrome or docked search child HWNDs keep the old top offset and leave inspector/search artifacts. If a child WGL paint is already pending during split/layout resize, a later `queuePaintRequest(update_now=true)` must still `UpdateWindow()` instead of returning early.
- 2026-04-19: `syncWindowTitle()` already has an internal same-title fast path. `refreshWindowTitle()` must only invalidate host chrome when the HWND title actually changed, or title-setter replays keep repainting top chrome for no visible result.
- 2026-04-19: Selected-profile / launcher-target changes are visually relevant only while the profile overlay is open or a status bar is actually visible. With Win32 `statusBarHeight()` still at `0`, closed-overlay profile changes should not invalidate chrome at all.
- 2026-04-19: Profile overlay selection/target changes already sync the overlay child HWNDs directly; avoid full `refreshChrome()` on every arrow/target step or you rerun title/tab/overlay sync work unnecessarily. Use profile-specific child sync plus `invalidateChromeText()`.
- 2026-04-19: `Host.invalidateChrome()` is mostly a repaint-only path (hover, focus, DPI/layout churn). If it also flips `chrome_text_dirty`, every generic repaint rebuilds cached banner/overlay strings. Keep a separate text-dirty helper and route only real text mutations there (`setBanner`, update-notice changes, `refreshChrome()` after sync work).
- 2026-04-19: `paintChrome()` must not call `buildInspectorBannerText()` on every paint just to decide whether to draw the cached banner. Inspector-banner allocation belongs behind `chrome_text_dirty`; the steady-state paint path should only consult booleans plus `cached_banner_w`.
- 2026-04-19: With Win32 `statusBarHeight()` hard-disabled to `0`, `invalidateStatusBarText()` must return immediately. Leaving it to flip the shared chrome-text dirty bit just forces unrelated overlay/banner/launcher caches onto the repaint path for status updates that can never paint.
- 2026-04-19: `Host.refreshChrome()` must not walk every visible docked search bar and re-run `syncSearchBarResultsText()` on generic chrome refreshes. Search results text is already synchronized on control creation and every actual search state change; the host refresh loop just re-formats and re-compares strings for no visible change.
- 2026-04-19: `setScrollbar(...)` runs on every scrollback update, but the bottom status only depends on whether the scroll indicator is visible and its rounded percentage; invalidating status text on every raw offset change burns paint work for no visible change.
- 2026-04-19: Core search can replay identical total/selected callbacks during docked-search debounce and steady state; `setSearchTotal` / `setSearchSelected` / `setSearchSelectedDisplay` must early-return on unchanged visible state or every duplicate callback still rebuilds results text and bottom status chrome.
- 2026-04-19: `syncWindowTitle()` still builds from `effectiveTitle()` + host tab status only. Readonly/cwd/key-table/search/progress setters were relying on `refreshWindowTitle()` as an accidental status-bar repaint path; trimming those no-op title refreshes must replace them with `invalidateStatusBarText()` or the bottom chrome silently goes stale.
- 2026-04-19: `syncScrollbarWindowNow()` must not call `Host.refreshScrollbarTimerState()` on every scrollbar sync. That path tree-walks the active tab; only refresh the host timer when this surface's heartbeat eligibility actually changes, and let `Host.tickScrollbars()` own the once-per-tick refresh.
- 2026-04-19: `syncScrollbarWindowNow()` must not `InvalidateRect` the layered scrollbar child on every hover/timer sync. Most ticks only change layered alpha, and alpha comes from `SetLayeredWindowAttributes`; repaint only when actual scrollbar paint inputs change (rect, scroll position, hover/drag visuals, or marker revision).
- 2026-04-19: Dynamic Win32 scrollbars do not need the 16 ms heartbeat once they are fully hidden and idle. Keeping `scrollbarNeedsHeartbeat()` true just because a pane is scrollable leaves the timer running forever; hover wake still comes from `WM_SETCURSOR` / mouse paths, so the heartbeat should stop at stable `.hidden` and restart on the next real activity.
- 2026-04-19: `Host.layout()` calls `setVisible(...)` on every pane during ordinary layout churn; `Surface.setVisible` must not unconditionally `refreshScrollbarWindow()` or same-state passes hammer the layered scrollbar sync/timer path for no visual change. Layout-side runtime sync should key off actual pane-rect or visibility deltas.
- 2026-04-19: Win32 docked-search `Enter` / `Shift+Enter` must derive from the same visible Up/Down helper as the search edit arrow keys; separate mappings drift silently the next time the visible button contract changes.
- 2026-04-19: Win32 docked-search result counts should stay newest-first even though the visible navigation moves older/up from the bottom prompt; converting the label to top-down order makes Enter/Up feel backward. If the results chip only appears for non-empty queries, edit changes must also trigger a host relayout or the chip stays hidden after the first typed character.
- 2026-04-19: Scrollback search core indexes matches newest-first (`idx = 0` is most recent), so Win32 docked-search UI must map visible up/prev to core `.next` and down/next to core `.previous`; forwarding the enum names literally inverts the user-visible direction.
- 2026-04-18: For sandboxed Win11 scrollbar hover validation, `SendInput` mouse moves can leave the real cursor elsewhere; use `SetCursorPos` and confirm with `GetCursorPos` before trusting hover results.
- 2026-04-20: Win32 focus-loss synthetic key release must not assert on `.closed`. Paste-protection confirm overlays can move focus while the paste chord is unwinding; treat release failures/closed effects as logged non-fatal outcomes so debug builds do not abort from `Surface.focusCallback(false)`.
- 2026-04-20: WinRT Action Center toast clicks in a warm process do not automatically flow back through argv/single-instance forwarding. Register `IToastNotification.Activated` for live toasts and marshal the parsed launch target back to the UI thread; keep argv parsing for cold/forwarded activation.
- 2026-04-20: Host-owned subclassed child HWNDs must be detached before `Host.deinit` frees the Host. Queued owner-draw/control-color messages can otherwise reenter a child/parent WndProc with stale `GWLP_USERDATA` and crash in helpers such as `isOverlayButton`.
- 2026-04-19: Win11 progress bottom-strip captures are not proof of Explorer taskbar rendering in hosted desktops; they can contain unrelated desktop content, so treat them as best-effort bottom-of-screen diagnostics, not authoritative taskbar evidence.
- 2026-04-19: The Win11 composite validator must do one upfront build when `-Rebuild` is requested; forwarding `-Rebuild` into parallel child harnesses duplicates `zig build` work against the same `zig-out` tree.
- 2026-04-19: In the Win11 composite validator, nested `Start-Process -PassThru` child PowerShell harnesses can finish with a blank `ExitCode` even after `WaitForExit()`. Use the harnesses' explicit `PASS` sentinel plus captured stdout/stderr for suite success/failure, not `ExitCode` alone.
- 2026-04-26: In PowerShell launched via `scripts/dev-windows.cmd`, `Start-Process -PassThru` on the GUI `noctty.exe` child can leave `Process.ExitCode` null even after `WaitForExit()` and `Refresh()`. Interactive close-validation harnesses must keep a native `GetExitCodeProcess` fallback instead of trusting the managed property alone.
- 2026-04-19: The Win11 command-finish harness cannot treat first observation of `command took` as terminal; the `NotifierDisabled` fallback warning can log one poll later, so success/failure needs a short post-command settle window.
- 2026-04-23: Interactive Win11 harness builds must not run with `LOCALAPPDATA` under `.sandbox`; Zig can emit broken relative paths from the sandboxed global cache back to repo `.zig-cache` and fail to spawn generated helpers such as `uucode_build_tables.exe`.
- 2026-04-18: Every interactive Win11 harness must claim its own sandbox name under `.sandbox\win11\<worktree-id>\<sandbox-name>` and use that sandbox id in `--class`; reusing the plain worktree root causes `-ResetState` races and mixed logs across concurrent validators.
- 2026-04-18: Interactive Win11 harness launch helpers must compare `zig-out\bin\noctty.exe` against source/build inputs before reusing it; existence alone is not enough, or validation can silently pass against a stale binary.
- 2026-04-18: On Win32, `IToastNotifier.Show()` can succeed while `ToastNotifier.Setting` is `DisabledForUser`/`DisabledForApplication`; check `get_Setting` before show so the banner fallback still fires when Action Center delivery is suppressed by shell policy.
- 2026-04-18: `std.heap.stackFallback(...).get()` in Zig 0.15 is single-use. In `src/termio/stream_handler.zig`'s OSC 7 pwd path, calling `.get()` twice panics in debug builds once manual PowerShell integration emits OSC 7. Snapshot the allocator once and reuse it.
- 2026-04-18: In `src/apprt/win32_taskbar_progress.zig`, `IID_ITaskbarList3` is `{EA1AFB91-9E28-4B86-90E9-9E9F8A5EEFAF}`. A transposed literal silently makes `CoCreateInstance(CLSID_TaskbarList, IID_ITaskbarList3, ...)` fail and disables native taskbar progress behind the title-only fallback.
- 2026-04-19: Win32 `close_tab:this` undo capture must reserve structural stack capacity before invalidating redo/history; if capture fails, the detached tab must be restored and existing history must remain valid.
- 2026-04-19: Unsupported Win32 split-tree fallbacks (`resize_split`, `equalize_splits`) must invalidate redo/history only AFTER the cloned replacement tree is built; if clone/equalize fails (for example OOM), preserving the current tree but dropping history is a correctness bug.
- 2026-04-19: Win32 split-tree fallbacks clone on some semantic no-op paths; compare the replacement tree against the current tree before invalidating redo/history, or `resize_split` / `equalize_splits` can wrongly drop history while returning `false`.
- 2026-04-19: Win32 last-tab close undo keeps the Host HWND alive with zero tabs and routes host-level undo/redo keybindings while no active surface exists. Do not destroy/recreate the host or reparent child HWNDs for this path; the app-level undo prune timer owns timeout cleanup for the hidden surfaces.
- 2026-04-19: For unsupported Win32 structural actions, do not clear redo/history before the action proves it will commit; `close_tab:other/right` no-op paths must preserve both local redo and host structural history when there is nothing to close.
- 2026-04-19: Unsupported Win32 host-scoped actions started from a split tab (`new_tab`, `move_tab`, unsupported `close_tab:other/right`) must clear redo across the current tab, not only the focused pane, or sibling split-pane terminal redo survives across a host-structure branch.
- 2026-04-18: Win32 `close_surface` / `windowDestroyed` must clear local redo for every surface in the affected tab before host structural invalidation; clearing only host undo/redo leaves sibling pane terminal redo reachable after a pane-removal branch.
- 2026-04-18: In the Codex/desktop Win11 validation path, background PowerShell automation does not reliably give `noctty.exe` true foreground keyboard ownership; `AppActivate`/`SendKeys` and direct `WM_KEYDOWN` injection are not trustworthy replay validators here. Prefer startup smoke + focused Zig replay tests unless you have a real foreground automation channel.
- 2026-04-18: On Win32, `CreateProcessW` with `lpApplicationName` set to a bare executable name (`pwsh.exe`, `powershell.exe`, `git.exe`) does **not** reliably resolve via PATH in this runtime path; treat true bare names as command-line-resolved launches (`lpApplicationName = null`) or pre-resolve them first. Explicit paths (`C:\...`, `.\tool.exe`, `bin\tool.exe`, UNC, drive-relative `C:tool.exe`) must stay explicit.
- 2026-04-18: Unsupported structural actions on Win32 must invalidate the local redo branch too, not only host structural redo/history; otherwise per-surface `clear_screen` / `reset` redo can survive across a new tab/zoom/reorder branch and replay stale terminal history after the structural mutation.
- 2026-04-18: Win32 `close_surface` is an unsupported structural mutation: always clear host structural redo before any surface `DestroyWindow` path (action path, confirm accept, or child-HWND `WM_CLOSE`), and if the surface is the last pane in its tab, clear host structural undo too because removing the whole tab stales saved close-tab reinsertion indices.
- 2026-04-18: Win32 mouse tab-drag reorder (`Host.swapTabs`) must clear host structural undo/redo before swapping; it mutates the same tab order as `move_tab`, so saved close-tab reinsertion indices become stale on both undo and redo branches.
- 2026-04-17: Unsupported Win32 host/tree mutations that commit after an undo, such as `new_tab` and `toggle_split_zoom`, must clear host structural redo even when existing structural undo entries remain valid; otherwise redo survives onto the wrong branch and later replay resets newer tab/zoom state.
- 2026-04-17: Unsupported Win32 tab reordering (`move_tab`) must clear host structural undo/redo before mutation; close-tab replay stores a reinsertion index, so replay after a reorder can silently restore into the wrong slot if history survives.
- 2026-04-17: For Win32 local terminal undo capture, clear host structural redo only AFTER the new local undo entry is successfully pushed; if snapshot capture or push fails and the action aborts, pre-clearing redo silently loses a still-valid structural redo branch.
- 2026-04-17: Win32 undo/redo is split between per-surface terminal snapshots and host-scoped structural history; new local actions must clear host structural redo, and new `new_split` / recoverable `close_tab:this` actions must clear redo on the affected tab's surfaces or stale redo branches survive across scopes.
- 2026-04-17: `TerminalFormatter.extra = .all` does NOT emit window title, and `vtStream()` ignores OSC 7 pwd reports; Win32 local undo snapshots must preserve title/pwd outside the raw VT payload or replay silently drops them.
- 2026-04-18: In Win32 path-normalization helpers, never blindly `TrimEnd('\')` on a fully-qualified path; preserve drive and UNC roots (`C:\`, `\\server\share\`) or you silently turn rooted paths into relative ones.
- 2026-04-18: In PowerShell interpolated strings, wrap a variable in `${...}` before a literal colon (`:`); `"$path: $value"` parses `$path:` as a scoped variable and fails.
- 2026-04-18: On Windows, both `pwsh.exe` and `powershell.exe` accept `/` as a startup switch prefix for forms like `/NoProfile`, `/NoExit`, and `/Version`; PowerShell argv analysis must treat `/` and `-` equivalently or valid interactive launches silently fall into the unsupported path.
- 2026-04-17: Win32 toast activation resolution must consult `tab_id` before falling back to the host's active tab when `surface_id` is stale; otherwise old command-finished toasts can focus the wrong tab in a still-live window.
- 2026-04-17: Warm-instance toast argv interception on Win32 must swallow malformed `wgh://activate?...` tokens too; if parse failure falls through to `applyNewWindowArguments`, the bad toast launch string is misrouted as startup/new-window argv.
- 2026-04-17: `pending_toast_activation` on Win32 must be consumed after the first surface is created; logging the parsed `wgh://activate?...` argv in `App.init` is not enough, and otherwise cold-start Action Center clicks do nothing even though AUMID/launch args are wired.
- 2026-04-17: Win32 Action Center launch args (`wgh://activate?...`) must be intercepted before the single-instance forwarded-argv path reaches `applyNewWindowArguments`; otherwise toast clicks on a warm instance are misrouted as config/new-window args instead of focusing the existing target.
- 2026-04-12: Once Win32 can stay alive with zero windows, `wakeup()` must post to the UI thread queue, not only a window HWND, or mailbox work and quit timers will stall headless.
- 2026-04-12: GDI brush handles (HBRUSH) returned from `WM_CTLCOLOR*` handlers must use `@bitCast` not `@intCast` to convert to LRESULT; GDI handles can have the high bit set on x64, causing `@intCast` to panic.
- 2026-04-14: On Win32, hiding a surface HWND is not enough; tab/window visibility changes must also drive `core_surface.occlusionCallback` or hidden tabs keep rendering and can crash the WGL/NVIDIA present path.
- 2026-04-14: In `src/apprt/win32.zig`, same-host tab/split surfaces must stay hidden through `Surface.init`; letting them show and repaint before `activateSurface()` briefly exposes multiple child GL surfaces in one host and destabilizes the render path.
- 2026-04-14: In `src/apprt/win32.zig`, hide inactive child GL surfaces before showing the active host tab; briefly overlapping multiple WGL child HWNDs can crash `nvoglv64!DrvPresentBuffers` during repeated tab opens.
- 2026-04-15: On Win32, do not trust `wsl.exe --status` as proof that WSL is a safe implicit default shell; actual `wsl.exe` session launch can still fail, so prefer the preview/non-WSL default path unless WSL is explicitly selected.
- 2026-04-15: In `scripts/package-windows.ps1`, avoid `Compress-Archive` for the portable ZIP; on Windows it can intermittently fail on staged theme files with spaces (for example `Monokai Classic`) even when staging itself is correct.
- 2026-04-15: Fresh GitHub Actions checkouts cannot build or test without `src/unicode/generated_props.zig` and `src/unicode/generated_symbols.zig`; keep them versioned until the stale `uucode` generator path is repaired.
- 2026-04-15: `scripts/package-windows.ps1` must build with `-Demit-lib-vt=true`; otherwise warm local trees can mask that fresh CI runners never produced `zig-out/bin/ghostty-vt.dll`.
- 2026-04-15: In this Windows-focused fork, preserve the curated `libghostty-vt`-only Nix layout; do not reintroduce unsupported GTK/Linux app derivations.
- 2026-04-16: `apprt.surface.newConfig()` returns a shallow clone; when overriding `Config.command` on that clone, never deinit the inherited command because it is still owned by the source config arena.
- 2026-04-16: The Win32 chrome metric constants (`host_tab_height`, `host_overlay_height`, `host_overlay_padding`, `host_pane_divider_width`, etc.) now derive from `win32_theme.ThemeMetrics` defaults. Keep the top-level `const` aliases in place for call-site compatibility; new code should consume `ThemeMetrics` via the `Theme` aggregate.
- 2026-04-16: In HC mode, `pane_divider` uses `COLOR_WINDOWFRAME` and `pane_divider_focused` uses `COLOR_HIGHLIGHT`; do not collapse both to `hi_bg` because that erases the focused/unfocused cue required by multi-pane layouts.
- 2026-04-16: Win32 UIA providers live under `src/apprt/win32_uia/`. `RootProvider` is a reference-counted `IRawElementProviderSimple`; `QueryInterface` must `AddRef` on success. Every PR that adds a new UI widget must add a matching provider — retrofitting accessibility is where accessibility debt lives.
- 2026-04-16: The Win32 UIA `VARIANT` mirror in `win32_uia/com.zig` must stay exactly 16 bytes (8-byte header + 8-byte payload union). Never add a second pointer-sized field to the `value` union — it silently inflates the struct and corrupts VARIANTs returned to the UIA host. A comptime `@sizeOf` assertion guards this; do not disable it.
- 2026-04-16: `Host.tween_sched` drives a single 16 ms `SetTimer(host.hwnd, TWEEN_TIMER_ID, …)` heartbeat that starts the instant a tween lands and stops on `scheduler.isEmpty()`. An idle host pays no CPU. `Host.deinit` must `KillTimer` before `tween_sched.deinit()` or a fired timer will deref a dead HWND. Reduced-motion (`SPI_GETCLIENTAREAANIMATION = FALSE`) and HC mode collapse every tween's duration to 0 via `Host.addTween`.
- 2026-04-16: `win32_uia/events.zig` is the canonical path for every UIA event raise — every widget that gains / loses focus, adds / removes children, or mutates a notifiable property routes through there. The helpers short-circuit on `UiaClientsAreListening() == FALSE` so the idle "no screen reader attached" path stays cheap. Raising events directly via `uiautomationcore` in new code is a bug.
- 2026-04-16: `win32_theme.blendSemanticAccent` + `resolveSystemAccentColor` are pure-computation infrastructure for the future accent-follow blend. Consumers (`overlayAccentColor`, profile chrome, etc.) still return fixed palettes today — wiring them to the blend is widget-polish work that ships per-widget. The `WM_DWMCOLORIZATIONCOLORCHANGED` handler calls `reconfigureTheme`, which repaints chrome but currently has no visible effect because the theme doesn't read the accent. The user-facing `accent-follow-system` opt-out lands in the same PR as the first widget consumer — do NOT reintroduce the config knob before that. The blend itself must never be called with a system RGB of 0 or the HSV conversion produces 0-saturation output that defeats the semantic hue (guarded in `blendSemanticAccent`).
- 2026-04-16: `src/renderer/OpenGL.zig` honours `config.window-vsync` via `ensureWin32SwapInterval` (renamed from the stale `enable_win32_swap_interval = false` gate). The minimum GL version is 4.3 per `MIN_VERSION_MAJOR/MINOR`, not 3.3 — any future "core profile" migration must target 4.3 because the shaders depend on 4.3 features. Full ARB-context + reset-recovery migration is deferred until it can land with atlas rebuild support; partial-ARB without recovery buys no robustness.
- 2026-04-16: BSTR ownership in UIA `GetPropertyValue`: the caller (UIA framework on behalf of Narrator/NVDA/Inspect) frees each returned BSTR via `VariantClear → SysFreeString`. Provider implementations MUST allocate a fresh BSTR per call. Caching a `SysAllocString` pointer and handing it back a second time produces a use-after-free on the second query and a double-free when the provider tries to free its "cached" copy on teardown. `RootProvider` now carries no cached BSTR state — `allocBstrFromLiteral` and `allocNameBstr` allocate per-call.
- 2026-04-16: `UIA_NamePropertyId` on the `RootProvider` reads the live HWND title via `GetWindowTextLengthW` + `GetWindowTextW`, not a literal "noctty" fallback, so screen readers announce the current window title (including post-rename). Fallback to "noctty" only fires on `GetWindowTextLengthW <= 0` or allocation failure. Re-announcing on title change requires raising `UiaRaiseAutomationPropertyChangedEvent` at the call site — not in the provider.
- 2026-04-16: Command palette matching uses `zf.rank` against the human title AND the action string (best of both). Tokens are whitespace-separated; extra tokens past `palette_max_tokens` are ignored. The ranker is called per-keystroke from up to 5 helpers; cost is O(N) per call at ~90 entries so no caching is necessary today. `commandPaletteUniqueMatch` now returns the top-ranked entry (not strictly unique) — this powers Enter-to-run semantics without forcing the user to type a full action name.
- 2026-04-16: `win32_palette.rankedForQuery` is a true top-K selection: it scans the full snapshot and replaces the current worst slot when a better rank arrives. Do NOT short-circuit on `count >= buf.len` — that would hide later commands (including user-added `command-palette-entry` items) from Enter-to-run, completion cycling, and banner suggestions once the palette grows past `max_ranked` (256).
- 2026-04-16: `build.zig.zon` fingerprint is derived from the package name; renaming `.name = .ghostty` to `.name = .noctty` makes the old fingerprint invalid. Zig's "suggested" value has a random low-half nonce — the name-hash prefix (`0x3b92d6b5…`) is what must match. Don't silently revert a fingerprint update triggered by a name rename; the package won't build otherwise.
- 2026-04-16: Do not add user-facing config knobs for planned-but-unimplemented features. `command-palette-version`, `bell-features.toast`, `accent-follow-system`, `window-titlebar`, `window-scrollbar`, `material`, `material-transient`, `search-bar`, and `notifications` were advisory fields that parsed cleanly but had no runtime consumer — they were silent no-ops and got removed. When a feature ships, add the knob in the same PR as the path that reads it; the default-on / no-flags rollout policy (§12 Q1 of the redesign plan) forbids staged rollouts with config gates. Pure-computation helpers / internal plumbing (like `blendSemanticAccent`) are fine to land as infrastructure; user-facing fields in `Config.zig` are not.
- 2026-04-16: `App.palette_mru` owns up to 5 most-recently-invoked command palette actions (newest first). `pushPaletteMru` dupes the action string into `core_app.alloc` and frees the oldest slot on insertion; `App.terminate` frees remaining slots. The MRU push must happen BEFORE `Surface.performBindingAction` on the submit path — actions such as `close_window` may synchronously free `self`, and post-dispatch access to `self.app.pushPaletteMru` is undefined. App-scoped (not Host-scoped) so recent picks survive window close. Persisted to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\noctty\palette-mru.txt` (one action per line, newest first) via `loadPaletteMru` in `App.init` and `savePaletteMru` in `App.terminate`. Load / save failures (missing LOCALAPPDATA, permission denied, corrupted file) degrade silently to "start empty" — MRU is a UX nicety, not a correctness invariant.
- 2026-04-16: Command palette list lives in a custom `noctty.win32.palette_list` WNDCLASS (registered in `App.createHost` via `ensurePaletteListClass`). The list child HWND is stamped with a `*Host` via `GWLP_USERDATA`; `paletteListProc` recovers it via `getPaletteListHost` to dispatch WM_PAINT / WM_LBUTTONDOWN / WM_MOUSEWHEEL / WM_GETOBJECT back to Host methods. Ranked matches are cached in `Host.palette_list_ranked` (stack array of `RankedIndex`) rebuilt on every keystroke via `rebuildPaletteList` — do NOT re-rank from within WM_PAINT; that's a per-frame cost paid for no reason. The child HWND is destroyed as part of the parent's destroy cascade, so `GWLP_USERDATA`'s pointer is only dangling briefly between child teardown and host.deinit; the wndproc handles WM_PAINT/mouse only and never touches `self` in that window. `invokePaletteRow` must `pushPaletteMru` BEFORE `performBindingAction` (same lifetime rule as `submitOverlay`). Arrow-key navigation (↑/↓) drives `Host.moveListSelection` on the EDIT subclass — Tab stays on `completeCommandPalette` for EDIT-text completion. `palette_list_selected` tracks the Enter target; the highlighted row repaints with `button_active_bg`. Keybind hints are rendered from `config.keybind.set.reverse.get(cmd.action)` into a 96-byte stack buffer via `std.fmt.bufPrint` — no per-frame heap allocation.
- 2026-04-16: `src/apprt/win32_uia/widgets.zig` hosts per-widget UIA providers. `PaletteListProvider` implements the minimum surface (IUnknown + IRawElementProviderSimple + ControlType=List + live Name) and is created fresh per WM_GETOBJECT query via `handlePaletteListGetObject`.
